## Introduction

#### Final Fantasy VII Remake

#### A Trophy-Based Walkthrough

#### How to Earn All 54 Trophies with a Minimum of Repetition

Now that we have THAT out of the way . . .

Square Enix took a significant gamble in remaking one of the most popular RPG's of all time. Regardless of how well you believe that they succeeded in living up to the standards of the original, it is difficult to deny that FFVIIR is a unique game in which there is a lot to do. Completing all 18 Chapters of the game is relatively straightforward, but earning all 54 trophies requires a lot of time, effort, and organization. I add "organization" because of the unique way that the game is constructed.

In most RPG's if you want to return to an earlier part of the game to complete some mission, you can usually warp to a location fairly close by and just walk the rest of the way to where you need to go. Not so with FFVIIR. This game is divided into Chapters, and once you have finished the final Chapter, you get the "Chapter Selection" option which allows you to return to the Chapter that you need. The hitch is that you must start at the beginning of that Chapter and play through until you get to the part that you need. Even if you choose Easy difficulty, it can often take well in excess of an hour to play through to the spot you need. In particular, Chapter 9 must be completed or substantially completed at least 3 times, and if you aren't aware of every Chapter 9 task that needs to be completed in order to earn all Trophies, you will wind up going through it 6 or 7 times.

My purpose in writing this guide is to help you earn all 54 Trophies while avoiding as much unnecessary repetition as possible. I have culled information from numerous other guides and sources and attempted to organize it all chronologically so that you can complete the game much more quickly than I did.

Updating this guide has been tricky because it is often the case that changing information in one section impacts information in other sections. Although I have worked diligently trying to make all information in this guide both consistent and correct, it is certainly possible that despite my best efforts this guide contains some inaccuracies. If you believe that you have found one, I would sincerely appreciate it if you would write me a courteous email and explain my mistake so that I can correct it for the benefit of other readers. There is no need to tell me that I'm an idiot. My wife and daughter keep me fully aware of that on a regular basis.

I am also a little hazy on when you actually get credit for earning certain Trophies. This is because I had already earned most of them prior to writing this guide, and they didn't actually "pop" when I was doing the playthrough for this guide. I am, however, highly confident that my instructions will help you meet all conditions necessary for earning all of the Trophies.

I hope that you will find this guide both useful and entertaining and that you have as much fun reading it as I had writing it.

## Why You Need This Guide

| **Purpose of This Page**<br>If you are trying to decide whether or not this is the right guide for you, I suggest that you read this page. Otherwise, feel free to skip it for now and move on. |

This is not a conventional walkthrough, yet I would like to make the case that it is the guide for FFVIIR that you want to use IF this is your first time through the game and IF you want to at least attempt to earn all 54 Trophies. If you have already completed a first run through the game or if you are not interested in pursuing all of the Trophies, then this may not be the best guide for you.

So, assuming that you are on your first run through the game and eventually want to earn all of the Trophies, I would claim that this guide is for you. It will provide you with a maximum of information that you need and a minimum of information that you don't need, either because you can figure it out for yourself or because it wasn't essential to begin with. You will get access to the Trophies much quicker than you could by using any other existing guide, and, in the process of getting there, you will hopefully enjoy the game more because you will be focusing on the game play and leaving the sequencing of your actions to me.

Earning all 54 Trophies with a minimum of repetition requires a comprehensive knowledge of the many tasks that must be completed, how to complete them, and, more importantly, when to complete them. Going into the game for the first time, you have no way of knowing all of these things unless you have spent hours researching and organizing all of this information. Even if you did so, much of that information might refer to things like "Battle Intel Reports" that you haven't encountered yet, so some the information you gather may not make sense. This is where I come in.

I have spent literally hundreds of hours researching the intricacies of game, verifying them with alternate sources and actual gameplay, and weaving them into a (hopefully) cohesive and comprehensible document. **I have endeavored to prioritize quality over quantity of information** presented so that even the first-time FF7R adventurer can acquire a solid understanding of the game and can follow an unambiguous path to the Platinum Master of Fate Trophy without having to wade through oceans of minutiae or spend the many hours fumbling around in postgame play that I did. All that is required of you, gentle reader, is to follow this guide from beginning to end and to exercise a modicum of patience in learning new techniques as they are needed.

If you've already started the game and progressed beyond Chapter 2, I'm going to suggest -- and I know that this sounds crazy -- that you could actually save time by starting over and using this guide from the start of a fresh run. I estimate that I wasted at least 20 - 30 hours by chasing down Trophies haphazardly. Starting over could conceivably save you that much time (minus, of course, the time you've already spent).

In its original form, this guide was spotty in its coverage of certain aspects of the game. However, between mid-2020 and late 2021, I have added massive amounts of content of this guide and now consider this to be a "complete" guide to FFVIIR. This is not to say that it contains every possible bit of information that exists about the game. That just isn't a reasonable goal. However, I now believe that this guide contains ample information for you to navigate the game from beginning to end and earn all 54 Trophies without using any other sources.

I should warn you that in the interest of economy, some mainstays of conventional walkthroughs have been omitted.

- I am not going to call your attention to the location and contents of every treasure Chest in the game. Most Chests are right out in the open where they are hard to miss, and they generally contain items you don't really need after the first Chapter anyway. When you are about to encounter a Chest whose contents are vital to our mission, I will see that you do not overlook it.

- I am not going to help you navigate every meter that you have to walk. FF7R is particularly good at showing you where you should go and giving you an unambiguous path towards your destination. In those instances where your path is not clear, I will provide step-by-step directions to help you. I will also show you how to figure out where you should be headed without being told specifically.
- Very little about the story line will be mentioned. I am confident that you will follow and enjoy that without my input.

In writing this walkthrough, I try very hard to create the guide that I wish that I had been able to use on my first run through the game. I think that this is very close to that guide, and I hope that you think so too.

## Some General Advice

Here are some basic tips to help facilitate your passage through the game. Many of these may seem obvious, but I think that all of these are worth mentioning. Some of the terms (such as "Shinra crate") may not be familiar to you now, but that's okay. You can always revisit this page when they are.

- Take your time. Throughout much of the game, other characters will be urging you to hurry, but only in rare situations is time even remotely a consideration. Part of the fun of RPG's is exploring, so take your time and see what you can find. Correspondingly, . . .
- Consult your map regularly. This will help remind you where you are headed, but it will also help you find items in those obscure corners of the map where you don't have to go to reach your destination.
- Money isn't particularly abundant (or, for that matter, necessary) in this game, so take advantage of the freebies. Until further notice, smash every Shinra crate you find, and open every chest. Similarly, . . .
- Check out every vending machine, even if you think that you don't need anything. Many contain music discs that you will need for the Disc Jockey Trophy, and most contain some "bargains" -- items like Megapotions, Ethers, and Phoenix Downs that can be purchased at reduced prices. In addition, many types of armor, accessories, and Materia can show up unexpectedly in vending machines.
- Always fill every Materia slot on every active character. After finishing Chapter 18, you are going to spend considerable time leveling up your Materia. Any head start you can get on this is a plus.
- Don't even think about trying to farm EXP. Enemies in this game are few and far between, and this is by design to prevent you from over-leveling your characters. There isn't a reasonable point in the game to farm EXP until you get access to Chapter Selection, and by then the AP farming you are going to do will easily max out everyone's levels at 50, thereby making the dedicated farming of EXP unnecessary. The first run through the game is totally manageable with available EXP and equipment.
- Whenever you acquire a new weapon, immediately equip it and have your character use and learn its unique ability. This will expedite acquisition of the Intelligence Agent and Weapons Expert Trophies. Each time you get a new weapon, I'll provide more information about its unique ability, and I'll try to remember to remind you to learn it.
- Beginning in Chapter 3, you will be able to upgrade your weapons. When we get there, I'll detail the process more completely, but in a nutshell upgrading can be done either manually or automatically. Until we get to postgame play, I'm going to advise you to let your weapons upgrade automatically. There is a LOT to keep track of in the game, and upgrading manually is rather time consuming. Give yourself a break and let the game do this menial task for you. You will have more important things to think about.
- Use your **Assess Materia** frequently. This will help you fulfill at least 4 of Chadley's Intel Reports, and it will help you bring down tough enemies by learning their weaknesses and attack patterns. It is generally time well spent, even though it takes time away from your attacks.
- I will do my best to give you the appropriate Objective name for every instruction that I give you, but I have to tell you up front that some of these may not be correct. I simply didn't pay close enough attention to these as I was playing through the game and writing this guide. I hope to fix these in future updates, but for now please forgive me these minor lapses in accuracy.

## Alphabetical Listing of Trophies

I thought that you might find the following list of available Trophies useful. Many of the descriptions in the game are not very specific, so in the limited space that I have available in each row, I tried to give you a more definitive description of what you have to do to earn each Trophy.

|     |     |
| --- | --- |
| Best in the Business | Complete all 26 side quests |
| Biker Boy | Complete the Bike minigame with high health (1) |
| Bonds of Friendship | Free a Bound ally (2) |
| Broken Dreams | Complete Chapter 13 |
| Building Character | Elevate one character to Level 50 |
| Cleanup Crew | Obtain the Chocobo and Moogle Materia |
| Crate Annilhilator | Complete all Normal Difficulty Box Challenges |
| Dancing Queen | Receive a gift from Andrea for dancing (3) |
| Destiny's Crossroads | Complete Chapter 18 |
| Disc Jockey | Collect all 31 Music Discs |
| Divine Gratitude | Complete the Angel of the Slums sidequests |
| Dressed to the Nines | Unlock all 9 bridal candidate outfits |
| Emerging from Chaos | Complete Chapter 17 |
| Escape Artist | Complete Chapter 2 |
| Gotta Start Somewhere | Complete a Quest (2) |
| Hardened Veteran | Complete all 18 Chapters at Hard difficulty |
| Heavenly Dart Player | Complete the dart game in 6 or 7 throws |
| In Lockstep | Open the door to the Disposal Room in Chapter 7 |
| Intelligence Agent | Complete all Battle Intel Reports |
| Lights Out | Complete Chapter 6 |
| Master of Fate | Earn all 53 other Trophies |
| Master of Mimicry | Learn all 4 enemy skills |
| Materia for Beginners | Level up an orb of Materia (2) |
| Mercenary Endeavors | Complete Chapter 3 |
| Music Collector | Collect 3 Music Discs |
| My First Ability | Max out a weapon's proficiency (2) |
| My First Summon | Invoke a Summon (use one in battle) (2) |
| Never the Bride | Complete Chapter 9 |
| Night on the Town | Complete Chapter 4 |
| No Appointment Needed | Complete Chapter 16 |
| Onetime Gig | Complete Chapter 1 |
| Paranormal Investigator | Complete Chapter 11 |
| Peeress of Pullups | Complete all pullup challenges |
| Picking Up the Pieces | Complete Chapter 14 |
| Plan E | Complete Chapter 5 |
| Returning Champion | Beat any Colosseum challenge after Hell House |
| Reunited | Complete Chapter 8 |
| Say It With Flowers | Bring flowers to Leaf House (2) |
| Sewer Survivor | Complete Chapter 10 |
| Snappy Dresser | Unlock 3 bridal candidate outfits (2) |
| Staggering Feat | Stagger an enemy to 300% |
| Staggering Start | Stagger an enemy (2) |
| Sultan of Squat | Complete all squat challenges |
| Summon Slayer | Defeat a Summon in battle |
| That's the Smell | Defeat a Malboro |
| The Collapse | Complete Chapter 12 |
| The Johnny Experience | Witness all Johnny-related incidents |
| The Pizza in the Sky | Complete Chapter 15 |
| Trapped Like Sewer Rats | Complete Chapter 7 |
| Ultimate Weapon | Unlock and defeat the Pride and Joy Prototype |
| Warming Up | Win a battle (2) |
| Weakened Resolve | Exploit an enemy's weakness (2) |
| Weapons Expert | Learn all 24 weapons abilities |
| Whack-a-Box Wunderkind | Complete all Hard difficulty box challenges |

**Footnotes:**

1. I have not yet found a definitive standard for achieving the Biker Boy Trophy. Most sources cite either 75% or 80% of health remaining, but this is difficult to confirm since your health meter doesn't have any numbers on it.
2. These 10 Trophies will come either automatically or semi-automatically. By this I mean that it would be very difficult to complete the game without earning these. While I suppose, for example, that it is theoretically possible to complete the game without ever leveling up a single orb of Materia, you would really have to go out of your way to do this.
3. I have not yet found a definitive standard for achieving the Dancing Queen Trophy. For reasons that I will explain when we get to [Chapter 9](#chapter-9----the-town-that-never-sleeps), I'm going say that my best guess is that you get this Trophy if you get 5 "Bad" ratings or fewer.

So, you get 18 Trophies just for completing Chapters, and 10 more will pop without you having to even try. The big picture here is that you will get 28 out of 54 Trophies either automatically or nearly automatically. Because these are so hard to miss, I will not mention most of them again in this guide. Our focus is going to be on earning the other 26.

## Chapter 1 -- The Destruction of Mako Reactor 1

At long last, it is time for us to make our first pass through the game.

| **How to Use This Guide**<br>1. Essential tasks -- navigation, items to collect, and specific actions to take -- will be in bullet form. ALWAYS read these carefully and follow them dutifully. They will give you a "paint-by-numbers" plan for each section of the game.<br>2. Narrative -- things like explanations of why you are doing things will be in paragraph form. Most of these are fairly brief and will give some context to information in lavender boxes. On your first run through the game, you definitely should read these. After that, well, I'll leave that up to you.<br>3. Deeper analysis -- game mechanics, general advice, mini-game rules -- will be in lavender boxes like this one. Use these as needed, but readers who want to learn as much as possible about the game will read these at least once. You'd be surprised how often I myself go back and review the things that I've written in these boxes. The more you understand about how things work, the better prepared you'll be to strategize. |

Okay -- Here we go!

| **Important Points to Keep in Mind**<br>- Chapter 1 contains no items that directly relate to Trophies, so we are going to focus instead on getting accustomed to the game.<br>- Read the tutorials carefully as the game gives them to you, and try to practice the techniques to which you are introduced.<br>- Learn to use your Map and take the time to explore your environs thoroughly for Chests containing items that you will need to get you through the Chapter.<br>- Develop the habit of smashing every Shinra crate that you see to acquire additional items. |

#### Walkthrough

You are going to have to choose a difficulty level, and my advice is to play in Normal Mode. If you want all of the Trophies -- and you DO want all of the Trophies or you wouldn't be reading this -- you're eventually going to have to complete the game in Hard Mode. We're going to put that off as long as possible, but hopefully we can agree that Normal will be much better preparation for Hard Mode than Easy Mode would be.

| **Finding Items in This Chapter**<br>In the first 2 Chapters of this walkthrough, I'm going to call your attention to many of the items that can be collected. However, whenever possible, I'm going to avoid describing their exact locations in order to give you the opportunity to find them for yourself.<br>In case you have trouble finding anything, I have inserted some maps at the bottoms of the pages showing the locations of these items. I recommend that you use these maps only as a last resort.<br>For the record, these maps _may not_ show the locations of ALL items in the Chapter. There _may or may not_ be other items that you should seek out for yourself. |

I'm going to assume that you have never played this game before, so here is an overview of the basics of combat.

| **Combat Basics**<br>- When you only have one character (like at the beginning of the game,) the usual pattern is to attack with the Square button first to fill up the ATB gauge (in the lower right corner of the screen.) Once your gauge is at least half full, you can spend that charge to use an Ability, cast a Spell, or use an Item. You can then repeat this process.<br>- Your basic attacks generally do minimal damage, while Abilities can do considerably more, hence the logic described above.<br>- Observe that you cannot generally begin a battle by healing or casting spells because you lack the ATB to do so. Thus, you must be particularly proactive about healing lest you find yourself in need of healing but lacking the ATB to do so.<br>- When you have more than one character, the usual pattern is as described above, but once you spend that ATB charge, immediately switch to a different character and fill their ATB gauge and use that charge.<br>- You should be aware that if you get attacked between the time you enter a command that uses ATB and the time that you actually execute that action, your action may be disrupted. If that happens, you lose your ATB and, if you were casting a spell, you also lose any MP that you were going to spend. Therefore, try to make sure that you are relatively safe before spending ATB.<br>- Enemies tend to focus their attacks on the player that you are controlling. One of the best ways to keep them off base is to frequently switch the character that you are controlling. |

| **Video Recommendations**<br>I strongly recommend a series of YouTube videos by BltzZ. He has posted videos explaining both basic and advanced gameplay using each of the 4 characters in the game. Many of the strategies he illustrates are not clearly explained in the game, so I think that you can learn a lot by watching these videos and learning their content. For now, I recommend watching his 7-minute video on Cloud's basic moves (https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DgnX-O1nIvk&t=3s) and then doing the same each time you add a playable character. |

Here is a quick overview of Cloud's basic attacks. A lot of this information is not made very clear during the in-game tutorials, so I consider it to be worthwhile reading. Whenever a new character joins the team, I'll insert a similar summary of skills for you.

| **Cloud's Combat Basics**<br>- Cloud has 2 modes for attacking -- Operator Mode (his default) and Punisher Mode. You can toggle between these Modes by pressing Triangle.<br>- Here are the properties of Operator Mode:<br>  <br>  <br>  - Cloud can move around freely.<br>  - He will execute a sequence of basic attacks if you press Square up to 5 times.<br>  - If you press and hold Square, Cloud will execute a circular AOE (Area of Effect) attack. Continuing to hold Square will cause him to execute a second AOE attack.<br>- Here are the properties of Punisher Mode<br>  - Cloud takes a more defensive stance and moves very slowly. Therefore, you generally want to use Punisher Mode when Cloud is already close to his target.<br>  - Cloud attacks faster, which builds his ATB gauge faster.<br>  - You can exit Punisher Mode by either hitting Triangle again or by hitting the Circle button to execute a dodge roll.<br>  - He will execute a sequence of basic attacks if you press Square up to 3 times.<br>  - If you press and hold Square, Cloud will briefly (about 10 seconds) be Berserked. During this state, he will take more damage but he will also deal considerably more damage to enemies. Berserk status is indicated by an orange "teeth" icon in the lower right of the screen. The icon has a little clock that indicates how much longer Cloud will remain in Berserk status.<br>  - If you hold the R1 (block) button while in Punisher Mode, Cloud will Parry the next melee attack, reducing the damage he takes by 60%. This Parry attack will increase the enemy's Stagger considerably. He cannot Parry Magic attacks or distance attacks (like bullets).<br>- Cloud starts the game with 2 special attacks -- Braver and Focused Strike. Each of these attacks costs one ATB bar to use. As he acquires more weapons, he will gain more special attacks or "Weapon Abilities."<br>  - Braver is the stronger of the 2 attacks. It has a long charge-up time, but it deals significant damage to the targeted enemy and to a limited AOE around that enemy.<br>  - Focused Thrust does less damage, but it can significantly increase the enemy's Stagger, especially when the enemy is in a "Pressured" state. A standard strategy against tough enemies is to get it into this Pressured state and then hit it with as many Focused attacks as possible to bring it to full Stagger. |

| **Pressuring Enemies**<br>As the information in the previous box indicates, Pressured enemies are particularly vulnerable. You can elevate the Stagger gauge of a Pressured enemy very quickly, and often the enemy will be stunned and just let you beat up on him for a change. Therefore, a common strategy is to Pressure, then Stagger, and then beat down. The question is, "How do you get an enemy Pressured?'<br>Although there is no simple answer to that question, Pressuring does vary from enemy to enemy. Here are some common actions that will Pressure an enemy:<br>- Hitting him with an elemental spell to which he is weak. For example, human enemies like Grungy Bandit are easily Pressured by Fire spells.<br>- Block, Evade, or otherwise disrupt an enemy's attack. Cloud's Parry, for example, can be very effective at Pressuring.<br>- Some ATB abilities like Tifa's Starshower can increase Pressure.<br>- Some enemies become Pressured when you simply reduce their HP by a certain amount.<br>Enemies are usually only in a Pressured state for a few seconds, so it is important to keep an eye out for that "Pressured" message. As soon as you see it, hit the enemy with a spell or attack that will increase Stagger.<br>There is a [section](#weapons-and-abilities) on Weapons and Abilities near the end of this guide, and it will help you identify those Abilities that are best at increasing Stagger. |

| **Physical Attacks vs. Magic Attacks**<br>Generally, the tendency in an RPG is to depend heavily on physical attacks in order to conserve Magic Points (MP) for healing and special situations (e.g., when an enemy is immune to physical attacks). In FF7R, MP are definitely at a premium, which would make this default strategy seem sound. Many enemies are relatively strong against Magic but weak against physical attacks, in which case melee attacks would be your preferred battle strategy. However, many enemies have relatively high defense and low Magic defense, meaning that a Magic attack will do more damage faster and, in many cases, can immediately Pressure or even defeat an enemy. Thus, your choice of which type of attack to spend your ATB on will be situational.<br>Especially in boss fights, there is generally a tradeoff between using your MP for attacking or using it for healing. If you depend heavily on physical attacks, the battle will often go on longer, thus necessitating more healing. However, if you spend some MP on attacking, the battle might end sooner and there will be less need for healing. Consequently, throwing a spell or two at a tough enemy can actually save you MP in the long run.<br>For most normal battles, relying heavily on physical attacks works just fine, at least for now. As you encounter unique enemies and bosses, I will try to give you some advice about the wisdom of using attacking Magic against them. As you go through the game, you will want to get a feel for the right balance between your 2 types of attacks, but my advice for right now is to attack with Magic just a little more often than you normally would and see how well your MP supply lasts. I'm going to predict that it will hold up better than you would expect. Many Shinra crates contain Mako Shards, each of which will replenish 10% of each character's MP, and until you get to Hard Mode, benches (which will generally be available right before boss battles) will restore all MP. Use these resources at every opportunity, and you should find that you can attack with Magic more often than you might think possible. |

| #### **Elemental Weaknesses and Battle Intel Reports**<br>Most enemies have an innate weakness to a particular kind of Magic. Hitting an enemy with a spell to which he is weak will do considerable damage.<br>Here are the most common elemental weaknesses:<br>- **Human** enemies are generally vulnerable to Fire.<br>- **Beasts** are generally vulnerable to Ice.<br>- **Machines** are generally vulnerable to Lightning.<br>- **Flying** enemies are generally weak to Wind.<br>There are, of course, many exceptions to this. Rude, for example is a human enemy who doesn't fly, but he is weak against Wind.<br>You want to start NOW hitting enemies with a Magic to which they are weak. Doing so will help complete Battle Intel Reports which in turn will lead to new types of Materia.<br>Right now, Cloud has Fire Materia, so when he is fighting human enemies, have him throw a Fire spell as soon as he has an ATB bar. Barret is about to join the team, and he comes equipped with Lightning Materia. Have him use his ATB to throw Thunder spells at any Mechs that you encounter.<br>I will try to remember to specify any elemental weaknesses that bosses have. Otherwise, if an enemy seems to be defying the generalities listed above, you can always Assess it to view any weaknesses.<br>If you want to get a head start on another Battle intel Report [here](#chapter-3----home-sweet-slum) is some information about using unique abilities on Staggered enemies. There really isn't any hurry, so if you'd rather spend the first couple of Chapters acclimating to the basic battle system, you can postpone working on this Intel Report for now. |

| **New Weapons -- Before and After Pictures**<br>As the game progresses you can upgrade your weapons to increase their stats and Materia configurations and acquire new abilities and buffs. This will be explained in more detail when we get to Chapter 3.<br>Whenever your team acquires a new weapon, I will insert 2 images of it showing its appearance and Weapon Ability. The first image will show its beginning stats and Materia configuration and the second show these features after all upgrades have been applied. |

| **Comparing All Weapons and Abilities for Each Character**<br>For a side-by-side comparison of each character's weapons and a summary of the strengths and weaknesses of all Weapon Abilities, please refer to the appropriately named ["Weapons and Abilities" section of this guide](#weapons-and-abilities). |

- Follow the linear path from the train through the first couple of areas. Read each of the many tutorials carefully and practice the methods described as you go.
- Eventually, you will get a tutorial about your Map. However, the tutorial omits this important point: Once you get your Map, press L2 to bring up the mini-map on the main screen. This makes navigating MUCH easier. You may have to press L2 twice because it cycles among blank, distance only, and distance + mini-map.
- Open your Map. You can see the name of the current objective ("Enter the Reactor Grounds") and your current destination, but notice that there is no apparent path to your destination. This is because it is on a different level. Press Down on the D-Pad to view "Connecting Passage," and things will make more sense. Notice that "Connecting Passage" has a marker next to it, indicating that your destination is on that level.

##### Objective: Enter the Reactor Grounds

- Notice that the music and your allies give a sense of urgency, suggesting that you should hurry. Don't. Take your time, explore, and practice the techniques in the tutorials.
- There is a Chest (1) at the top of the stairs (the (1) means that the location of this Chest is labeled with the number one in the map at the bottom of the page). Find another Chest (2) containing 2 Grenades in this area (Station 2F) before moving on.
- Head over to where your team is trying to open a door. You will pass a bunch of crates (3). These are Shinra Crates that can contain items, but more often than not they contain Mako Shards that will recover 10% of all team members' Magic Points (MP). Smash these crates with your sword to get a Potion and a Moogle Medal.

##### Objective: Infiltrate the Reactor

- You will get a tutorial about using R3 to lock on to an enemy. Once you are locked on, pressing the right directional stick L or R will select from among available enemies.

| **Locking On to an Enemy**<br>Learning to Lock On to enemies made battles significantly easier for me. Instead of having each character beating up on whatever enemy was closest (or, worse yet, just attacking thin air because there was no nearby enemy,) R3 made them all focus on the designated common enemy. Especially in battles with a lot of enemies, this whittled down their numbers quickly, thus making the battle much more manageable. As a bonus, if a character is a long way away from the enemy on which you are locked, you don't have to waste time making that character run all the way over to it. Just start attacking, and your character will move really quickly towards the enemy. So, get in the habit of hitting R3 at the beginning of every battle.<br>Unfortunately, when an enemy teleports (disappears and then reappears) you lose your Lock on that target and will need to lock on again. This is especially annoying when fighting a tough enemy like Leviathan, but you just need to learn to deal with it.<br>There will be times that you will want different members of your team to target different enemies. In this case, Locking onto a target generally isn't a good idea. |

- Check your Map. See that dead end branch in Storage Area that points N? This is a common place to find items. Here you will find a Chest (4) containing an Ether.
- Just NE of where you get on the elevator, you'll find some Crates (5) that will restore MP.
- Down the stairs, before going through the hole in the fence, you will find 2 more piles of Crates (6) and (7) to smash.
- In the Gate Passageway area, there is a Chest (8) that you'll practically trip over. It contains 2 Potions.

##### Objective: Breach Security

- In the Front Gate area, you'll find a Chest (9) containing a Phoenix Down and some more Crates (10).

##### Objective: Reach Mako Storage

Somewhere in here, Barret joins the fun, so let's take a minute to look at his attack options.

| **Barret's Combat Basics**<br>- Hold the Square button, and Barret will unleash a series of basic attack shots, the last of which is more powerful than the others.<br>- Barret can move while shooting.<br>- He also has a more powerful attack "Overcharge" that will also considerably increase an enemy's Stagger.<br>- Overcharge is executed by holding down the Triangle button, but it isn't always available.<br>- Overcharge has a meter which is fully charged at the start of each battle, but after that it must recharge before Overcharge can be used again.<br>- The usual sequence after using Overcharge is this: First, hold Square to perform the usual sequence of normal shots. After that last, more powerful shot, hit Triangle to boost the Overcharge meter. Repeat this pattern until the Overcharge meter is fully charged again, then just repeat this entire sequence.<br>- Overcharge will automatically change targets if the targeted enemy dies before the Overcharge sequence completes. This is very nice, because it helps you make best use of this powerful attack.<br>- Like Cloud, Barret starts the game with 2 Weapon Abilities, but will learn more as you acquire more weapons.<br>  - Steelskin is a defensive buff that uses one ATB bar. It reduces all damage Barret takes by 30%, and it has the important property of decreasing the likelihood that an enemy attack will knock him back and disrupt his actions.<br>  - Focused Shot is similar to Cloud's Focused Thrust in that it significantly increases an enemy's Stagger. The bad news is that is uses ALL of Barret's ATB. The good news is that its power increases as more ATB is available and used to execute it. |

- You now have 2 active team members. Switch back and forth between having Barrett attack the Sentry Rays and having Cloud attack the Monodrives.

##### Objective: Follow Jessie

- After you go through the third set of lasers, smash some Crates. They're right in front of you, so I'm not going to put them in the picture.
- After the last laser, there is a Chest with 2 Potions. Again, you really can't miss this one.
- Before advancing to battle the Sweeper, look behind you and around the corner for a Chest (11) containing an Ether.
- Sweeper is your first Mini-Boss. Its Magic Defense (4) is much lower than its Defense against physical attacks (20). Since Sweeper is a mech, it is weak against Thunder, and happily, Barret comes equipped with **Lightning Materia**. So when Barret gets some ATB, try throwing a Thunder spell at Sweeper and see how effective it is. Don't worry about using up your Magic. There are some Mako Shards ahead to replenish it before the upcoming boss battle.

##### Objective: Set the Charge

Useful hint: When going down a ladder, you can speed up your descent by holding R1 while sliding down.

- Open the Main Menu and select "Battle Settings" and "Cloud." Notice that there are shortcuts for certain common actions. For example, L1 + Triangle will execute Focused Thrust without having to go through your battle menu. You don't have a lot of choices right now, but as the game goes on, you can customize these. This can be helpful when battle conditions get frenzied.
- At the bottom of the first ladder, you'll find some Crates containing a Potion and Mako Shard. Again, these are hard to miss.
- On B5, you'll see a Chest right in front of you containing a Phoenix Down. Right after that are some more Crates with a Mako Shard.
- On B7, just beyond a ladder you need to descend, you'll see some Monodrives protecting a Chest and some Crates. It's a pretty easy battle for an Ether and some Mako Shards, so I'd engage them.
- When setting the timer, choose 20 minutes. You'll have plenty of time and you will get some modest rewards for choosing the shorter time. Ignore Barret's comment. Cloud isn't cocky. He's just confident.

Your first Boss is the Scorpion Sentinel. Pay close attention to the discussion between Cloud and Barret. They will give you the specifics you need to lay this ugly thing to rest.

##### Objective: Escape From the Reactor

The timer is really a non-issue, as it stops frequently and you have plenty of time to escape. Jessie will show you the right way out -- probably.

- On your way out, another Sweeper is going to jump in front of you. This is one of a very few battles in this game that you can avoid. Run around him and up the stairs. You DO have to defeat the other enemies between you and the elevator, but they shouldn't slow you down much.
- Hop on the elevator, save Jessie and then impress her with Cloud's leaping ability. You will earn a participation Trophy for completing the first Chapter.

Wasn't that fun? Let's do another Chapter!

* * *

#### Area Maps Showing Item Locations

* * *

## Chapter 2 -- Fateful Encounters

| **Important Points to Keep in Mind** <br>- Cloud is on his own throughout this Chapter, so be especially aware of keeping his health up.<br>- Like Chapter 1, Chapter 2 contains no items that relate directly to earning Trophies.<br>- There are, however, some quality items to be found here, so use this Chapter to hone your searching skills. Search every corner of every area, and use your Map to find previously unexplored areas to check out.<br>- Work on developing your basic battle skills and learn a couple of new ones.<br>- I'm going to talk you through most of the navigation here, giving you both directions of what to do and rationale for doing things so that you can find your own way after this. |

| #### **Elemental Weaknesses -- Reminder**<br>Chapter 2 is a good place to continue exploiting enemies' elemental weaknesses to get a head start on Battle Intel Reports 2 and 7. There are a lot of human enemies here, so use Cloud's Fire magic on them for big damage and credit towards completing these Reports. |

| #### **Finding Items in This Chapter**<br>As was the case in Chapter 1, collectible items will be identified as we go, and maps showing their locations will appear at the bottom of the page. Happy hunting! |

#### Walkthrough:

##### Objective: Use the Escape Route

- Check your Map for a moment. I really don't know how there could be any uncertainty about where to go here.
- Just before going up the stairs, you'll find a Chest (1) containing an Ether.

##### Objective: Reach the Sector 8 Station

- Jessie gives Cloud his second orb of **(Healing) Materia** (he comes equipped with **Fire Materia).** Check out the tutorial -- or not -- but equip the Materia.

- Again, check your Map. The marker is to the NW, so make your way N, checking for Chests along the way. You should find one (2) containing 50 gil. Eventually, Jessie will give you directions.
- On your way up, you'll pass a Chest (3) with 3 Grenades. Grenades are a good substitute for Fire Magic when fighting enemies with shields, as explained in the lavender box below.

##### Objective: Follow Him!

- This is one of several points in the game where you have to move painstakingly slowly. You're going to have to get used to this.

##### Objective: Taking a Detour

- Okay -- best laid plans and all that. The route north is now blocked. Your only other choice is the path to the West. Go up some stairs, up a ladder, and down some stairs. Somewhere along this path is another Chest (4). Grab its contents before you go down that final ladder.
- The street that angles to the SE has guards, so you don't want to go there. In fact, the game won't even let you try, so head West for a scene.

##### Objective: Evade Pursuers

- If you go S to the square, you'll be thrown into a series of battles. Before doing that, explore the rest of the area.
- Always watch for areas of the Map where you haven't been. They often contain items.
- Before going into that S square, you should be able to find 4 items and some Shinra Crates containing Mako Shards. Keep in mind, that you're going to have to win some battles to obtain them:

  - A valuable **HP Up Materia**(5) that you can immediately equip in the second Materia slot on your Buster Sword.
  - A Chest (6) containing a Hi-Potion.
  - A **Deadly Dodge Materia** (7).
  - A Chest (8) containing a pair of Power Wristguards that you should equip right away.
- Once you have picked up these 4 items, put on your game face and continue South to fight several waves of enemies.

| **Deadly Dodge Materia**<br>I don't use this particular Materia much, but some people really like it. You might want to give it a try and see what you think.<br>If a character who has this Materia equipped successfully dodges (Circle button) an attack and then immediately hits Square, the resulting attack will hit all enemies within an area (a so-called "AOE" attack) instead of just one enemy. This is especially effective when you are being swarmed by a multitude of enemies.<br>If you are near enemies but they aren't actually attacking you, the resulting attack will be normal and not AOE. You have to actually evade an attack for the Deadly Dodge to kick in. In other words, you have to get the timing right to get the effect of this particular Materia. |

- Not sure where to go next? Look for the helpful marker to the SE. Nearby, you'll find a Chest (9) containing a couple of Grenades.

##### Objective: Break the Blockade

- The path will lead you up 3 ladders and then down 3 more, but along the way, you should pass a Chest (10) containing an Iron Bangle. Equip it right away and make use of its Materia slot.

| **Enemies with Shields**<br>Enemies with shields (like the Riot Trooper) will generally block your physical attacks. Build your ATB gauge by attacking other enemies, then use Magic spells to knock down the shielded enemy. Alternatively, use the Circle button to roll past them and attack their vulnerable back sides. |

- Once you hit the ground, the path is pretty linear. Advance slowly, defeating enemies and checking side areas as you go, looking for Chests and Shinra Crates. You should find at least the following items:
  - A chest (11) containing an Ether.
  - Some Shinra Crates (12) that you probably need for replenishing your MP.
  - A chest (13) containing a Hi-Potion.
  - Another pile of Shinra Crates (14) to replenish MP.

In the final battle of the Chapter, a Mini-Boss, the Huntsman, will join in.

| **Boss Fight -- The Huntsman**<br>As long as his shield is up, the Huntsman will deflect your attempts to attack him head-on. This still leaves you a couple of options for defeating him:<br>- You probably don't want to try to Parry his attacks because his attacks can Stun Cloud.<br>- Build up some ATB battling the other troops around you. Use Fire to take out the Riot Troopers first. Then use Punisher Mode attacks on the others. Ideally, you'd like to have 2 full ATB bars before taking on the Huntsman.<br>- When only the Huntsman remains, hit him with Fire. This should immediately knock him down and Pressure him, but his Pressured state won't last long. Use your second ATB bar to hit him with a Focused Thrust to boost his Stagger, then hit him with some Punisher Mode attacks to rebuild some ATB.<br>- He is vulnerable from behind, so you can build back some ATB by rolling past him and attacking him from behind if necessary.<br>- His attacks are strong, so watch him carefully. If he is about to attack, hold off on your offense and concentrate on dodging his attacks. This way you won't have to spend your ATB healing, and there is less chance of getting stunned.<br>- When you have ATB again, hit him with Fire to Pressure him again and follow with Focused Thrust. This should be enough to Stagger him. Beat him down with Punisher Mode and finish with Braver. |

Make a daring escape and ride the train to Chapter 3. Along the way, if you search very carefully, you will find a Chest containing 100 gil.

By now, you should be getting pretty good at finding items. After this, I'll only be directing you towards items that I deem essential, and I'll let you decide how much time you want to spend poking around in corners and dead ends for Potions and similar low-priority items.

* * *

#### Area Maps Showing Item Locations

## Chapter 3 -- Home Sweet Slum

| **Important Points to Keep in Mind**<br>- Unlike Chapter 1 and 2, Chapter 3 contains several items and events that lead to Trophies. Follow this guide especially closely so that you don't miss anything! This is where my work and research will hopefully begin to benefit you.<br>- In particular, make sure that you collect all 4 **Music Discs** in this Chapter. |

#### Walkthrough:

##### Objective: Return to Base

- After the scene with Barret and Marlene inside Seventh Heaven, look at the upper left corner of the screen. You'll see a blue musical note in a circle followed by 3 question marks. This means that a Music Disc is nearby. Interact with the jukebox, and you will get **Music Disc #3**.

There are 31 Music Discs all together. You get a Trophy for finding 3 of them and another Trophy for finding all 31. You can keep track of your collection through the Main Menu under Inventory > Music Collection.

- On your way to your new apartment, look across from Seventh Heaven for the girl in the cap and striped leggings. We'll be meeting her again in Chapter 14.

##### Objective: Life in the Slums

- When Cloud arises, there is a Chest with a Phoenix Down that you can access before descending the stairs. Since it isn't essential, I'll leave it to you to find it.
- Your first stop on the "water filter run" is the Item Shop. Check his inventory, and buy **Music Disc #1**.
- While you're here, you might want to buy another **Lightning Materia**. This will ensure that you have one for each member of your team when you get to the final battle in Chapter 6.

| **Upgrading Weapons**<br>When talking with Biggs, you'll get a tutorial in upgrading your weapons. As I indicated in the Section on General Advice, I would advise turning on Automatic upgrades so that you don't have to keep doing this manually. To do this, go to the Main Menu > Upgrade Weapons and choose Cloud. When "Buster Sword" is highlighted, press Triangle and choose your preference of the 3 Automatic choices. For your first runthrough, I would advise either Balanced or Prioritize Defense. You can always change this later if you change your mind.<br>For every weapon and every character, you will need to repeat this process. When Tifa joins the team in a moment, be sure to set her Leather Gloves to automatically update.<br>Whenever you earn Skill Points to upgrade weapons, you will get that same number of SP for every weapon you currently own and for every weapon that you acquire later in the game. Therefore, you don't have to save SP for future weapons or decide which weapon to upgrade now.<br>When we complete the initial playthrough, I will give you more specific information about how SP and weapon upgrades work in a section that is cleverly titled [Skill Points and Weapon Upgrades](#skill-points-and-weapon-upgrades). Feel free to read ahead if you want, but, as I will explain in that section, on the first playthrough you don't have a lot of control over your SP anyway, and I don't want to overload you with information that you don't need yet.<br>So, in a nutshell, . . . |

- Set Cloud's and Tifa's weapons to automatically upgrade.
- Tifa comes equipped with the **Chakra Materia**. Her weapon has another slot, so go ahead and equip her with the **Deadly Dodge Materia** before going out on Pest Patrol.

Since you're about to fight some battles with Tifa, this is a good time for a quick rundown on her basic abilities.

| **Tifa's Combat Basics**<br>- Tifa is widely considered to be your best fighter. She has the highest Speed stat of any of your characters, but unfortunately she also has the lowest Defense stat. She can do a lot of damage, but she will also take a lot if you don't play her defensively.<br>- Like Cloud and Barret, Tifa will defend when you press R1 and will dodge when you press Circle. Additionally, if you hold R1 and tap Circle, Tifa will execute a sliding movement that will help her approach enemies much more quickly than if she just runs. Try it, and you'll see what I mean.<br>- Her basic attacks are executed by using the Square button. The number of attacks in a string depends on her Chi level.<br>- Tifa has 3 Chi levels. Each corresponds to a unique Triangle button attack, and each has a different number of attacks in a sequence. At higher Chi levels, Tifa's attacks do more damage and increase Stagger more.<br>- You can raise Tifa's Chi by one level by spending an ATB bar to perform her "Unbridled Strength" ability.<br>  - At Chi level 1, her Triangle attack is Whirling Uppercut and her basic attack string is 4 attacks.<br>  - At Chi level 2, her Triangle attack is Omnistrike and her basic attack string is 6 attacks.<br>  - At Chi level 3, her Triangle attack is Rise and Fall and her basic attack string is 8 attacks.<br>- Occasionally, you will see a message at the beginning of battle that says, "Concentration Activated." This means that Tifa is beginning the battle at Chi level 2 instead of Chi level 1. This ability is an upgrade that can come with some of Tifa's weapons.<br>- Executing a Triangle attack will cause Tifa to drop down one Chi level. For example, if she is at Chi level 3 and performs the Triangle attack (Rise and Fall) she will drop to Chi level 2. Her next available Triangle attack will then be Omnistrike unless she performs Unbridled Strength again to go back up to Chi level 3.<br>- However, after using Unbridled Strength, Tifa can still execute a Whirling Uppercut without using up those advanced Chi Levels by simply holding the Square button.<br>- Tifa's Triangle attacks are awesome for at least 3 reasons: 1) They can do significant damage to Staggered enemies; 2) They increase Stagger percentages of Staggered enemies; and 3) they recharge Tifa's ATB in varying amounts.<br>  - Whirling Uppercut regains 40% of an ATB bar for normal enemies. On Staggered enemies, it recoups 50% of an ATB bar and increases the damage multiplier by 5%.<br>  - Omnistrike regains 5% of an ATB bar for normal enemies. On Staggered enemies, it recoups 10% of an ATB bar and increases the damage multiplier by 25%.<br>  - Rise and Fall regains 11% of an ATB bar for normal enemies. On Staggered enemies, it recoups 45% of an ATB bar and increases the damage multiplier by 20%.<br>  - Notice that her least powerful and most readily available Triangle attack regains the most ATB. Go figure.<br>- Best of all, Tifa's Triangle attacks can be executed without expending ATB. Therefore, her Unbridled Strength ability can give her a kind of "savings account" of power that she can dish out on a Staggered enemy when it will do the most damage.<br>- A common strategy against tougher opponents is to Stagger them first, then have Tifa alternate between ATB attacks and Triangle attacks. The ATB regain from her Triangle attacks can make this sequence nearly continuous.<br>- Tifa's original ATB ability is Divekick. It costs one ATB bar to use, can cause significant damage, and has an AOE around the target, meaning that it can damage multiple enemies if they are close together. |

| **Checking Your Proficiency Progress**<br>Even if the ability doesn't seem all that useful, make it your purpose to use it repeatedly until you acquire that ability. You can check your progress towards complete proficiency on the Main Menu under Materia and Equipment. Choose the character and then the weapon and look to the right side of the screen. You will see your proficiency progress. |

Once you gain 100% proficiency with a weapon's ability, you can use that ability in battle even if that particular weapon is not equipped.

| **Proficiency Bonuses**<br>Normally, using a new weapon ability once will increase proficiency by 10%. However, for each weapon, it is possible to get a 30% increase per use if you meet certain conditions. These conditions will always be detailed just underneath the proficiency progress information described in the previous box.<br>For example, look in the lower right portion of the weapon screenshots above. Under "Proficiency Bonus" it says "Finish off an enemy." This means that if Tifa's Divekick kills an enemy, she will earn 30% towards learning the Divekick ability instead of just 10%.<br>Sometimes the conditions for receiving a proficiency bonus are rather difficult to achieve, in which case you may just have to use the ability 10 times in order to learn it. However, you should always be aware of what conditions are necessary for earning the bonus so that you can speed up the learning process when circumstances allow it. |

##### Objective: A Job for the Neighborhood Watch

- The Gorgers can attach themselves to you, making you "Bound" and unable to move. Have your other character attack that Gorger. If you manage to free the Bound character, you're bound to get the Bonds of Friendship Trophy for it.

- Pass through the tunnel and check out the "notch" in the Map where, "notcherally" enough, you'll find an important item -- **Ice Materia**. I would advise equipping it on Tifa, which means you'll have to replace one of her other orbs with it.
- If at all possible, keep your new **Ice Materia** equipped on someone from now on. It will be helpful if you can bump it up a level (giving you access to the Blizzara spell) before the end of Chapter 7. You should also use it on the many animal enemies that you will encounter to continue working towards those Battle Intel Reports.
- Back in town, follow Tifa's advice and talk to the Weapons merchant. He will give you an Iron Blade. Equip it immediately and fill its extra Materia slot with whatever you removed from Tifa.

As the tutorial describes, each weapon has a unique ability. Learning all 24 of those abilities (6 each for each of the 4 characters) will earn you the Weapons Expert Trophy. In addition, learning 16 weapon abilities will complete one of the Intel Reports for your Intelligence Agent Trophy.

##### Objective: Problem Solving

- Talk to Wymer about Side Quests, and Chadley, the dorky kid next to him, will start talking to himself and then butts in and addresses Cloud. Equip the **Assess Materia** that he gives you, and get comfortable while I put you to sleep with more of my long-winded explaining.

| **Chadley's Intelligence Reports**<br>First, let's deal with Chadley. There will be a total of 20 Intelligence Reports to complete for him. Every time you complete one, check back in with him and he will give you a Materia for free and/or offer to sell you Materia at a discounted rate. He is your key to the Intelligence Agent Trophy, so check in with him every time you complete a Report or really whenever you see him, which will be often. Likewise, as you go through the game, periodically go to the Main Menu under Battle Intel and see what you still need to do to complete Reports he has assigned to you. This can remind you, for example, to Assess more often or to exploit enemy weaknesses more.<br>Right now, try to knock out Report 02. You just have to hit 3 enemies with spells to which they are weak, [as I listed for you earlier](#chapter-1----the-destruction-of-mako-reactor-1). Completing this Report will allow you to buy a **Wind Materia** for a mere 100 gil, and you're going to want that Materia for 2 upcoming Quests. |

| **About Side Quests**<br>Now let's talk about Side Quests. You will get the Best in the Business Trophy for completing all 26 Side Quests. As it turns out, that cannot be done in your first trip through the game because 2 pairs of Quests are mutually exclusive. That is, if you take a path that leads you to the first pair, you will not have access to the second pair. That is only going to be a minor problem, and I'll help you deal with it.<br>A bigger consideration is the rather complicated conditions you must meet in order to attain the Dressed to the Nines Trophy. Earning this will require at least 3 passes each through Chapters 3, 8, and 9, and in each pass you have to be sure to make all the right choices. I'll explain more about this Trophy as we progress. For now, suffice it to say that although there is more than one way to get this Trophy, one wrong move can cause you to have to repeat an entire Chapter, and, if you read my introduction to this guide, you should know that I am here to help you avoid that very thing.<br>I promise you that the path down which I am going to lead you will absolutely earn you this difficult-to-get Trophy. I try to avoid putting anything into one of my guides without personally verifying that it works. All you have to do is read and follow directions as they are given. I ask you to put your trust in me and to avoid researching this topic on your own and then deviating from the path I lay out for you. I have read many online descriptions of how to get this Trophy, and some contain information that is vague, incomplete, or downright inaccurate. My initial approach to the game was so haphazard that I personally went through Chapter 9 enough times that I can probably recite the dialogue by heart. So believe me when I say that I am well versed in the potential pitfalls of trying to get this Trophy and am committed to helping you avoid my mistakes. |

| **Resetting Weapon Upgrades**<br>I have been advising you to set your weapons to upgrade automatically, and for your first run through the game, I remain convinced that will do just fine. However, when you get to Hard Mode, you may want to manually choose which upgrades to apply, and if so Chadley is your guy for this.<br>The third choice in Chadley's menu is "Reset Upgrades." Pay him 100 gil and he will remove all upgrades from a single weapon and then refund to you all the SP you spent to redistribute as you see fit. This can be handy if you are building a particular type of character (e.g., one that focuses on Magic instead of melee attacks) or if want to sacrifice one specific buff for, say, another Materia slot.<br>For now, let the AI do the upgrades for you, but keep in mind that you can always redo them whenever you want to. |

For now, we are going to complete all 6 Side Quests available in this Chapter, but you should be aware that we are going to have to do them all again later on.

I now return you to our regularly scheduled programming . . .

##### Quest: Chadley's Report

- For the foreseeable future, every time you encounter a new enemy, use one of your ATB charges to Assess it. You will complete Intel Reports for Assessing 2, 10, 20, and 30 enemies, which is 4 of your 20 total Reports. Additionally, you will get useful information about enemies' weaknesses, attack patterns, and the like.
- Take a minute to run ALL the way back East of town to the Item Shop near the train station (it's marked on your map with the usual "bag" icon) and purchase **Music Disc #4**. This should pop the Music Collector Trophy for you.

- Go back to Seventh Heaven and go down the alley just to the right as you are facing Tifa's bar. Talk to the NPC (non-playable character) in front of the Clubhouse with the Pizza sign, and he will give you a copy of **Music Disc #27** to add to your collection.

| **Monitoring Your Side Quest Progress**<br>To see what Side Quests are available, call up your Map. It will show green markers where you can go to initiate available quests. Hit the R2 button to bring up more specific information. Besides the assignment that Chadley gave you, there should be 3 more listed. Notice, for example, that Rat Problem shows 4 little circles. This means that you have 4 steps to completing this Quest. Highlight the Quest and press X and you can view what you have already done and what you need to do next. |

The order of completing these Side Quests doesn't matter, but let's head over to the Item Shop and solve the Rat Problem first.

##### Quest: Rat Problem

- After you speak with the Item Shop owner, your Map will show you where the "edge of town" is. Right now, you'll only find Wererats there. Wipe them out, then go back and talk to the Item Shop owner again. When you return to the rats' location, you'll find the Doomrats. Remember to Assess and to use Cloud's Triple Slash ability and Tifa's Divekick ability so that you will start learning them.
- Doomrats can be tough. Lock onto one at a time so that both characters attack that one enemy. Doomrats are weak against Ice spells, so use your ATB to cast Blizzard spells.
- When a Doomrat gets Pressured, have Cloud hit it with his Focused Thrust to Stagger it and start working towards completing Battle Intel Report 4.
- Return to the Item Shop owner and report. He will offer you a **Cleansing Materia** for a discounted price, and I would advise buying it. You might also want to buy another **Healing Materia** so that both Cloud and Tifa can use Cure.
- Go over to talk with Chadley. He will give you some gil and some new assignments and will offer you the **Auto-Cure Materia** for the bargain price of 100 gil. Buy it, but read my paragraph below about its limitations.

**Auto-Cure** isn't as amazing as you might think. It will only cast Cure automatically on a character other than the one who has it equipped, i.e., a character can't use it on himself. It also won't Auto-Cure until someone's HP are critically low, which, in many cases, is just too late.

Be sure to read your new Intel assignments and try to work these into your battle strategies. Many of these will come naturally, but some you may have to think about using.

| #### **Battle Intel Report 03 -- The Stagger Effect, Pt. 1**<br>One of your new Intel assignment is Report 03 - The Stagger Effect, Pt. 1, and you want to make completing this a high priority. However, many people, myself included, have trouble completing Intel Report 03, so let's take a closer look at it.<br>First, let me explain what the Report is asking you to do. The text says, "Use unique abilities on staggered enemies and charge the ATB gauge 10 times." There are 2 things here that may not be clear.<br>1. "Unique abilities" are the ones controlled by a character's Triangle button, e.g., Cloud's Punisher Mode or Tifa's Whirling Uppercut.<br>2. "Charge the ATB gauge" means that you have to increase it. It is not clear whether the gauge must be completely filled or just increased, but I'm pretty sure that just adding to the gauge's charge is enough. Either way, if the character using the unique ability already has a full ATB gauge, you won't get credit. Those Triangle attacks must increase the ATB gauge.<br>I am not certain whether the character that staggers the enemy must be the same as the one that uses the unique ability. To be on the safe side, I would probably stick with one character.<br>So, for example, you could fill Cloud's ATB gauge and then have Tifa, the Queen of Stagger, get the enemy close to stagger. Switch to Cloud and have him use Focused Thrust. This will do 2 things. Hopefully, it will stagger the enemy and it will also use up an ATB segment, allowing you to then fill it using his Punisher Mode.<br>The reward for completing this Report is the **First Strike Materia.** Besides being crucial in leveling up our Materia in preparation for Hard Mode, it can help you finish routine battles in seconds. For example, if Cloud enters battle with at least one ATB bar, he can immediately execute a Triple Slash. This can immediately finish off a group of 3 Wererats. |

##### Quest: On the Prowl

- Wymer has another Quest for us. It's a tough one, but we're right here anyway, so we might as well head over to Scrap Boulevard to the west and take on the Wrath Hound. He's weak against Ice, so make sure that someone still has the **Ice Materia** equipped.

##### Quest: Lost Friends

- Next, let's go help Betty find her Lost Friends. I agree with Cloud that this Quest is beneath him, but we may as well get it over with. Here's where you will find them.
  - Follow the road that goes NE from Betty until it T-intersects. One of the cats will be right in front of you.
  - From there, go NW to Seventh Heaven. The cat will be on the front porch.
  - Go West, and when the path splits, take the South path. You will see an NPC with a green marker over her head. Talk to her (or not) and go through the tunnel to the south to find the last cat.

- Return to Betty when prompted to do so.

##### Quest: Nuisance in the Factory

- Head back to the tunnel you went through to get to the Doomrats and talk to the guy at the marker.
- Flying enemies like Lesser Drake are a pain. Your team members have to sort of fly in the air to attack and build ATB, and they can't really keep up with something with wings. However, once you have ATB, a Wind-based spell like Aero will bring a flyer down to earth pretty quickly.
- Go through the factory, kill everything that you see, and don't forget to pick up that **Fire Materia**!
- Return to complete this Quest when prompted to do so

| **Planning Ahead**<br>You now have 2 orbs of **Fire Materia**. Try to keep both of these equipped on active members until they accrue 300 AP and move up to Level 2. When we get to Chapter 8, you are going to want 2 characters to be able to cast Fira. |

##### Quest: Just Flew in From the Graveyard

- Our 6th and final Quest is back in the factory. Make your way to the area circled on the map below and smash the crate on the left just before reaching the bench. There you will find the Watch Security Key.

- Use the Key to access the only area you haven't visited yet (shown by the green marker in the picture above). Take down the Cerulean Drake and report back when prompted to.

| **Alone At Last**<br>Completing all 6 Quests unlocks the "Alone at Last" Discovery event, and this event is the key to unlocking two of the dresses we need for the Dressed to the Nines Trophy. If this event does not show up, go to your Map and hit R2 and see which of the 6 Quests you did not complete. |

##### Discovery Event: Alone At Last

- Go directly back to the apartments (the blue marker on your map). Have Cloud change the filter in his room and then go to Tifa's room. After much conversation, Tifa will ask "What do you think would suit me, huh?" You are going to choose the response, "**Something exotic?"** Don't ask why. Just do it. I'll explain later. Right now, I don't want to give anything away.
- Tifa suggests heading back into town. Choose "no" and continue into the bar.

When Cloud is left alone, you have a chance to win the Heavenly Dart Player Trophy, and I would like to convince you that it is worth your while to take the time to do this now. It's a relatively easy way to add another Trophy to your collection. Moreover, if you earn this Trophy now, Wedge will give you a rare **Luck Up Materia** (which raises the chances of a critical hit) in the next Chapter. There are only 2 of these Materia in the game, and it is one of the more commonly used Materia in postgame play. If you get frustrated with trying to win this, you can try again on a subsequent pass through Chapter 3. However, postponing this will necessitate completing all 18 Chapters (to make Chapter Selection available), and then completing Chapters 3 and 4 again in order to actually get the Materia. Ergo, you save time and unnecessary repetition by beating this mini-game now.

Personally, I'm not very good at this type of mini-game, and even I got this Trophy within about 15 - 20 minutes, so I know that you can too.

##### Objective: Ominous Shadows

- Go outside and pick a fight with a bunch of hoodlums. Your Fire spells can make short work of them.
- Head home. You'll be greeted by a surprise visitor who gives you the **Ifrit Materia**. Equip it in the rightmost slot of your sword and head off on a mysterious mission.

That's about it for Chapter 3. As I suggested, this was a longer, more eventful, and ultimately productive Chapter that you probably don't want to repeat any more than you have to. Collect another Chapter-completion Trophy and prepare for some motorcycle action.

## Chapter 4 -- Mad Dash

| **Important Points to Keep in Mind**<br>- There are no items in this Chapter that lead directly to a Trophy, but you can get a couple of good orbs of Materia.<br>- With patience and practice, you can earn a Trophy at the beginning of the Chapter.<br>- Cloud will be your only playable character for most of this Chapter, so keep an eye on his health. |

#### Walkthrough

Chapter 4 is relatively short and uneventful. You should be able to navigate this Chapter with a minimum of assistance from me, so hopefully you'll have a lot less reading to do than you had in Chapter 3.

The Chapter begins with an annoyingly long battle on motorcycles. If you complete this sequence with sufficient health remaining (there seems to be little consensus on what constitutes "sufficient," but most estimates range from about 75% - 80%) you will win the Biker Boy Trophy. However, my advice is to just do your best for now. Since this battle is right at the beginning of the Chapter, it is easy to access postgame via Chapter Selection, which will allow you to attempt it in Easy Mode, which will increase your probability of earning the Trophy.

##### Objective: Annex Infiltration

- There is a Training Center here where you can fight some easy enemies, but the EXP and AP gains are so slow that I would advise just skipping it altogether.
- There is also a Bench here that you can use to top up your HP and MP.

##### Objective: A Little Diversion

- At some point, Biggs will suggest that you equip the Summon that Jessie gave you. Use it in battle and you'll receive the My First Summon Trophy.

|     |     |     |     |     |     |     |     |     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Summon Stats**<br>Besides being useful as allies fighting alongside your team, Summons can increase certain stats of the character who equips their Materia. The table below shows these increases.<br>Be aware that percent increases apply only to the character's base stats and do not take into account other equipped Materia or any weapon upgrades. So, for example, if a character has a base stat of 100 MP but equips a **MP Up** Materia that increases that to 125 MP, equipping the Leviathan Materia will increase that MP count by 4% of 100 (4 MP) instead of 4% of 125 (which would be 5 MP).<br>Most of these bumps are fairly insignificant -- on the order of 1-2% increases -- but they cost nothing, so you might as well take advantage of them. |
| Summon | HP | MP | ATK | MATK | DEF | MDEF | STR | MAG | Spirit | Vit | Luck |
| Ifrit |  | +2% | +4 |  | +2 |  | +2 |  |  | +2 |  |
| Shiva |  | +3% |  | +4 |  |  |  | +2 |  |  |  |
| Chocobo & Moogle |  | +2% |  | +4 |  |  |  | +2 |  |  | +2 |
| Fat Chocobo | +5% | +2% | +4 |  |  |  | +2 |  |  |  |  |
| Leviathan |  | +4% |  | +4 |  | +2 |  | +2 | +2 |  |  |
| Bahamut |  | +5% | +4 | +4 |  |  | +2 | +2 |  |  |  |

- Squeeze through the doorway and you'll fight several waves of enemies. Just keep yourself healed and you'll do fine. Eventually, you will go one-on-one against Roche.

##### Objective: Return to the Slums

- Leading you back to the Slums, Jessie will open a door on the left and take you down a narrow passage. When the passage takes a jog to the right (North) look to your left for an orb of **Revival Materia**.

In Hard Mode, you won't be allowed to use Items, so your Phoenix Downs will do you no good when a member of your team "loses consciousness." Consequently, you are going to want to have a maxed out **Revival Materia** for each of your active characters. We'll buy 3 more a ways down the road.

##### Objective: Remaining Payment

- After getting Wedge home to his cats, go see Jessie for some gratitude and an orb of **Barrier Materia**.

##### Objective: Slum Wisdom

- When Cloud returns to his room, Tifa will come check in with him. When she leaves, look in the lower left corner of the screen. When the "Menu" icon appears, press and hold the Square button. This will allow you to adjust Equipment and Materia before the next battle.

##### Objective: Sudden Attack

- You'll fight 2 waves of Spectres. For the first wave, just defeat all of the enemies.
- For the second wave, defeating Mysterious Spectres increases the Enigmatic Spectre's Stagger Gauge and makes it available as a target. When you get a message saying the that Enigmatic Spectre is vulnerable, direct your attacks on him for as long as he is Pressured. Once the Pressured state lapses, direct your energies back on the Mysterious Spectres because Enigmatic Spectre will resist damage.

##### Objective: A New Operation

- Back in the bar, talk to Wedge. If you took top place on the Dart Player Board, he will give you a **Luck Up Materia**.
- Meet up with Barret at the train station, and Chapter 4 is in the books!

## Chapter 5 -- Dogged Pursuit

| **Important Points to Keep in Mind**<br>- We only have 2 Trophy-related items to pick up in this level, and one of them comes automatically. As was the case with Chapter 4, you shouldn't have any trouble finding your way with minimal help from me.<br>- Remember to continue Assessing enemies and follow the combat basics we've been working on, and you should do just fine here.<br>- There are lots of side passages along the way that you should explore. Some contain useful items, so remember to explore. |

#### Walkthrough:

##### Objective: All Aboard for Sector 4

- When you go to check on Barret, there is a **Healing Materia** on the train seat, but it is REALLY hard to miss. I mean, it's as obvious as a guy with a machine gun arm.

##### Objective: Looking for a Friend

- Be sure to use the Bench to recover after your first battles in Corkscrew Tunnel.
- Youre heading NE from the Bench. There is a room to the SW, but it just contains a Potion.
- Head North, eliminating enemies and exploring all side passages as you go. Once Tifa spots Barret, she will lead you upstairs, thereby showing you the way. I love Barret's victory song!

##### Objective: Find Stamp

- If you look at the Map after the cutscene, it would appear that you can go either way. However, since Cloud is facing SW, that is the direction to go. As a rule, it will be true that the path forward after a cutscene is the direction that your main character is facing.
- Notice that when you approach the stairway, Tifa and Barret run ahead, again showing you the way.
- After killing the Queen Gashtrike, you're facing a dead end. A quick check of your Map shows that you need to turn around and go SE.
- There's a Bench you can use in the SE corner of Railway Control E2.
- Beyond the Bench, you'll pass through a gate and will head NW. The path will split as shown below. The N path has a room to explore, and, as Stamp will tell you, the S path is the way forward.

##### Objective: Secret Passageway

- After fighting the Sentry Launchers near the end of Corkscrew Tunnel, you will go up the stairs. The second time that the path splits, go straight instead of heading right. You will find a bench for recovering and a vending machine that will sell you **Music Disc #28**. If you check your Map, you will see that you are going past the turnoff to your next checkpoint. Before moving on, don't forget to pick up the **Lightning Materia** sitting on the floor.

| #### Poison Materia<br>The vending machine with **Music Disc #28** also sells **Poison Materia,**a very useful Materia to have on hand. As a rule, mechs can't be Poisoned, but most human and animal enemies can. Many very strong enemies can be Poisoned, allowing you to bleed off lots of their HP even at times when they are out of reach and cannot be attacked with melee weapons.<br>You will want **Poison Materia** for the boss fights in Chapter 8, so I strong advise you buy one now. |

| **Planning Ahead**<br>**Lightning Materia** is one of your best weapons against machines, and you should now have at least 2 of them. By the end of Chapter 7, you will want both of these bumped up to Level 2 so that you can use the Thundara spell, so try to keep both of these equipped on an active player as much as possible between now and then. |

You might want to check your Inventory. You should now have 5 Discs -- #1, 3, 4, 27, and 28.

- You'll have to fight another battle on your way to the boss encounter. You can backtrack to the bench you just used to refill HP and MP if you so desire.

##### Objective: Stairway Detour

This level's boss is the Crab Warden.

- After beating Crab Warden, you will get a set of Metal Knuckles for Tifa. Equip them on her, fill the Materia slots appropriately, and make a mental note to use her new Overpower ability as often as possible until she learns it. Remember to set them to auto-upgrade.

The ever-optimistic Barret warns us that things aren't going to get any easier. On that cheerful note, we put an end to Chapter 5.

## Chapter 6 -- Light the Way

| **Important Points to Keep in Mind**<br>- Once again, there are only 2 Trophy-related items to collect (a weapon and a **Music Disc),** and the weapon will come automatically. However, we will also have the opportunity to collect a 3 must-have orbs of Materia, so we want to be sure not to miss those.<br>- Additionally, you will have the opportunity to earn a Trophy by completing a fairly simple event. Read carefully so that you don't miss this one!<br>- Uncharacteristically, since your path isn't always very clear in this rather confusing area, I'm going to do my best to talk you through all of the twists and turns.<br>- Even though I'll try to give clear directions, be sure to check your Map frequently. On my first time through, I made some wrong turns and got scolded by Barret for taking too long to get where we were going. You really do not want to upset Barret. |

#### Walkthrough:

##### Objective: Head for Section G

- Follow Tifa and Barret down the ladder, then go east across the planks. Continue to follow them into the Control Room.

##### Objective: Head for Section H

- Go South to fight 2 Grashtrikes and then East and North to the area marked G-04. Head down the stairs to G-05 and follow the linear path.
- You will see some **Materia** in H-07, but you can't get to it from this side. Fortunately, its location will be marked on your Map.

- The square area to the West is a dead end with 3 Grashtrikes and a Chest with a Remedy. Take out the enemies, get the Remedy, then backtrack East and go up the stairs. Tifa will suggest you stop and get your bearings.
