There are 7 new challenges in the Colosseum for you, and there are several things to be gained by doing these now.

1. You need to complete these eventually before you can access the Top Secrets Challenge.
2. You can learn Barret and Cloud's new weapon abilities.
3. Barret and Tifa can earn their Level 2 Limit Breaks.
4. Barret and Tifa will get Vol. II of their Manuscripts, which equates to SP for weapon upgrades.

So, . . .

- Catch a ride back to either Wall Street destination, and head back down to the Colosseum.
- Fight and win the 7 challenges that are not yet checked off the list of available Battles. You can fight these in Easy Mode, and they will end quickly. Here are some reminders for Materia to equip:
  - The "vs. Wild Animals" enemies will mostly be Ice-weak, but the last battle is against a flyer, so you'll want **First Strike**, **Wind**, and the **Ice** \- **Elemental** pair on your weapon.
  - Your enemies in the "vs. Shinra Thugs" challenges will be a mixture of men and machines, so be sure to equip both **Lightning** and **Fire** in addition to **First Strike**.
  - In the "Three-Person Team vs. Shinra Warriors" challenge:
    - You will want all 3 team members to have **Ice**, **Lightning**, and **Fire**.
    - Two members of your team should have **First Strike** as well.
    - Give Cloud the **Fire** \- **Magnify** combo in paired slots so that he can torch multiple enemies simultaneously.
  - In the "Two-Person Team vs. Team Payback" challenge, I would advise using Barret and Cloud.
    - No enemies are Fire-weak, but you will want both Cloud and Barret to have **Ice**, **Lightning**, and **Wind** as well as **First Strike**.
    - In round 3, you will have to take on another Chromogger, and this time you won't have Aerith to help you. Have Barret equip the **Elemental** \- **Lightning** Materia pair on his weapon and attack from a safe distance, and you should be fine. You can find our previous strategy [here](#chapter-8----budding-bodyguard).

##### Quest: Wavering Heart

- Go next door to the gym. Talk to Andrea, who will challenge Tifa to a pullup contest.

This is definitely harder than the squats competition, but beating Andrea should only take a couple of tries. That will allow you to challenge Jay and Jules, as you did with Cloud. Beating Jay isn't that hard, and it will earn you a rare **Magic Up Materia**, so I would definitely do that now. To beat Jules and earn the Peeress of Pullups Trophy, you have to be pretty near perfect. The good news is that this is easy to access postgame, so if you want to save Jules for later, it's no big loss. You will, however, have to beat Andrea and Jay again before getting another shot at Jules. My advice is to get this out of the way now.

Beating Andrea will complete this Quest and earn you Tifa's Way of the Fist, Vol. III Manuscript.

##### Quest: Subterranean Menace

- Catch a ride to Evergreen Park and talk to Wymer, then head underground. Make note of the message that you can return topside by just holding L1.
- Follow the looooooong linear path to the Type-O Behemoth.

- Hit and hold the L1 button to return to the surface. Talk to Wymer to clear this Quest and get a Wrecking Ball. Terrific. Now you have TWO useless weapons for Barret and 2 abilities to learn.

| **Learning the Smackdown Ability**<br>You should consider making a quick side trip over to the Colosseum long enough for Barret to fight his solo vs. Wild Animals Challenge and learn the Smackdown ability. The game tells you that you get the Proficiency Bonus if you "Strike three or more enemies," but I have consistently earned the Bonus in the first Stage against 2 Wererats. You should be able to learn to ability by the time you complete these 5 battles, and then you can set the Wrecking Ball aside until you feel like using it again. |

##### Quest: Secret Medicine

- Hitch a ride to the Sector 5 Undercity Entrance and head over to the Community Center. Talk to the NPC with the professional-looking lab coat and the green marker over his head. He will give you a list of items that he needs, but you should already have all of them:
  - Moogle's mortar, that you bought from Moogie;
  - Behemoth Horn, that you got from the last quest;
  - Medicinal flowers that you got from the Chapel when you visited Kyrie.
- Talk to him again, and this Quest will be marked as Complete.
- Completing this quest earns you Aerith's Telluric Scriptures, Vol. III Manuscript. It's a shame she isn't here to enjoy it, but if she were, I'm sure that she would say, "Thank you!"

##### Quest: Corneo's Secret Stash

We saved this one for last because it is the longest and most complicated. Once we complete it, we'll have some time to tidy up some loose ends.

- Check your Inventory of Key Items and make sure that you have the Corneo Vault Key (you should have got this from Kyrie at the Church). You need to use it to open 3 treasure vaults.
- One of Corneo's Vaults is in the Steel Mountain area, very close to Sam's Delivery Station there.

- Unfortunately, that Station is for pickup only. Undercity Station is the closest drop-off point. Head N from Sector 5 and take the right fork towards Steel Mountain.
- Smash all of the crates and open all of the Chests, and head over to the conveniently located Chocobo Station.
- Ride to the Collapsed Expressway Entrance. Head inside and you should quickly find the next Vault, which should be marked on your Map. Raid it for loot, then return to the surface.

BTW, you can't equip those Tiaras. We have other plans for them.

- The last Vault is accessed through the ornate locked door we found in the Sewers in Chapter 10. Our tour guide is going to be Leslie, so hitch a ride to Wall Market and head over to Corneo's Mansion.
- Go upstairs and enter the second door to go through Corneo's Office for a scene with Leslie. He will lead you into the sewers.

##### Objective: Through the Underground Waterway

- We're going to have some battles to fight along the way.
- You might want to pair your new **AP Up Materia** with your **First Strike Materia** until it maxes out.
- Many upcoming enemies are weak against Fire, so that is probably your preferred elemental Materia here.
- Once you are underground, you need to walk straight ahead up and over the rubble in front of you. It appears to be impassible, but it isn't.

- The path is linear for a while. You've been here before, so most of the ways (and the Chests) have been opened. Some recent cave-ins limit your path choices even further.

##### Objective: The Hideout

- Right after Leslie offers to take the lead, you'll come to a bench and a vending machine that will sell you **Music Disc #13**. You should now have all Discs except #5 and #31.

##### Objective: Recovering the Key

- In the SW corner of Old Aqueduct 1 is an orb of **Poison Materia** you should probably grab.
- Mischievous Shoat will lead you through a series of rooms in which you will have to fight battles. After defeating the enemies, be sure to check all nooks and crannies of every room for Chests and Crates before moving on.
- Eventually, you catch up with the Mischievous Shoat and get to beat him up.
  - At 85% health, he will summon 3 Abzu Shoats.
  - If you hit him with a couple of Firagas to Pressure him right off, it's possible to Stagger and finish him before he can summon his allies.
  - If not, just deal with the Abzu Shoats. No biggie.

##### Objective: Fateful Decisions

- After finally defeating the Mischievous Shoat, Leslie will tell you that he knows a shortcut. Follow him through the door, but DO NOT GO UP THE LADDER YET!
- Instead, throw the switch next to the ladder, making the last Corneo Vault accessible. Your Map will show you its location in the Former Disposal Area. Go there now, but read the next 2 points before entering the Vault.
  - The East-West branch that leads to the vault will take a right-angle bend to the North. Look in the corner of that bend (the vertex of the right angle for you geometry nerds). There you will find a Chest containing the second pair of Protective Boots in the game. **BE SURE TO GRAB THEM! YOU'RE GOING TO NEED THEM!** The Boots are located at "A" in the screenshot below.
  - Continue towards the Vault, but prepare your team before entering. Equip everyone with **Fire Materia**, and give either Cloud or Tifa the **Fire**-**Elemental** Materia pair on his/her weapon.

- You'll have to fight a battle against 3 fire-weak enemies -- 2 Sahagins and a Sahagin Prince. All of them can turn you into toads, so you want to hit them hard before that happens. You get to recover HP and MP before your next fight, so be aggressive with your Fire spells right away or this can get out of hand quickly.
- Once that battle is behind you, loot the vault, head back to the ladder, and follow Leslie to the locked door and a recovery Bench.

##### Objective: For the Reunion

- Leslie will lead you to a rematch against Abzu.

I realize that the above isn't coherent or sequential enough to constitute an actual "strategy," but it should give you enough information to prevail.

- Return to Marle when cued to do so, and complete this seemingly never-ending Quest. As a reward, you get Cloud's Art of Swordplay, Vol. III Manuscript.
- After your raid on the Corneo vaults, you should be flush with Moogle Medals. Head over to the kids' hideout and buy any Manuscripts that you haven't purchased yet. If you are short a couple of Medals, start a game of Whack-a-Box and then quit. You will get a Medal as a participation prize.

##### Objective: Over the Wall

- Catch a ride to Wall Market -- Urban Advancement District and make your way towards the marker. On the ground, you will find the Letter from the Guardian Angel Key Item and another orb of **Elemental Materia**. This should grant you the Divine Gratitude Trophy.

You might observe that the passage that runs West from this road is now unlocked. It leads back to the sewers in case you forgot something down there.

There are other things that we can do now, but generally they can be done more efficiently postgame. Let's go ahead and head over the wall and on in to our next adventure.

## Chapter 15 -- The Day Midgar Stood Still

| **Important Points to Keep in Mind**<br>- Once you go over that wall, there is no turning back. Anything you left behind has to wait until after completing Chapter 18.<br>- This is another conventional level. We'll follow a fairly linear path, fighting enemies as we go, with the level culminating with a boss fight.<br>- We'll collect one more **Music Disc** and one more enemy skill. |

#### Walkthrough:

##### Objective: The Climb

- Check your Map as you ascend. When you reach 55M above ground level, equip Cloud with the **Enemy Skill Materia** and do a Manual Save. On the next level up, you'll fight 2 Cerulean Drakes, and you want to learn a Skill from them.
- At 65M above ground level, put Cloud in front of the Drakes one at a time. If he doesn't learn their Skill before the battle ends, reload your game and try again.

You can now remove the **Enemy Skill Materia**. We have one more skill to learn, but it is going to take some time before we're ready to do that.

- When you reach a dead end, you'll get a tutorial in locating places to use your grappling guns, as shown below.

##### Objective: The Crumbling Building, etc.

- After climbing a short ladder, you will be looking directly at a vending machine that will sell you **Music Disc #5**. Just one more to go before earning the Disc Jockey Trophy.

- The area SE of the Bench has a chest that contains 3000 gil. You have to fight a battle to get it, but since you can heal before and after, it seems like it's worth your while to raid it.
- At 115M Above Ground Level, the path seems to split.
  - The stairs will take you directly to 120M and the path forward. However, . . .
  - The grappling point to the W leads to an outdoor path downward that leads you to a Chest containing an Enchantment Ring. From there, you can grapple back up to 115M and take the stairs to 120M.
- At 120 M Above Ground Level, you encounter a Blast Ray, which, when Staggered, will put up a barrier preventing you from getting close to it. Stay cool. You can still hit it with Magic, Barret's guns, Cloud's Blade Burst ability, or Tifa's Chi Trap.

- At 135M up, there are 2 ladders that go up. The one that is clockwise from where you climbed up has a Chest. The other one is the path forward.
- Be sure to look down when Barret says not to. Great view!

- At 155M, this level's boss appears and blasts you with its unlimited supply of bullets. Hide behind the barriers and wait for Valkyrie to pause its attacks, then dash to the next barrier and repeat, thanking your lucky stars that Valkyrie doesn't just fly over to your side of the barriers and blast you from there. FWIW, Valkyrie's bullets here cannot reduce Cloud's HP below 1, so he will always survive.
- End the Chapter with a battle against this flying boss that, for a while anyway, only Barret can hit. My favorite.

Well, I probably wasn't a lot of help in that Chapter, but then again, you probably didn't need (or expect) much from me. Let's move on to the Shinra Building.

## Chapter 16 -- The Belly of the Beast

| **Important Points to Keep in Mind**<br>- Navigating some parts of this Chapter can be confusing, so for a change, I'll try to provide some guidance that doesn't include the word "linear."<br>- We're going to pick up 2 easy-to-miss weapons and get that Disc Jockey Trophy for finding our last **Music Disc**.<br>- Wouldn't it be nice to see Aerith again? I miss her sassy sense of humor, don't you? |

#### Walkthrough:

##### Objective: Infiltration

- On your way in, you face ever-increasing numbers of enemies in each cluster, so proceed with caution.

##### Objective: Shinra Building Intel/Acquiring the Keycard

- Once inside the building, take the stairs to the next floor. To advance the story, you have to try to open the doors at the North end and discover that you cannot do so without a keycard.
- When Tifa is on the ground, she will get up and look directly at a car on which she can climb to move forward.

- On the first landing, before climbing the ladder, look to the West and you will see a purple Chest. That is our next objective. Go West, then South, then West again to claim Tifa's Purple Pain weapon.

- Return to the first landing, go up the ladder, and follow the unambiguous path to the keycard.

##### Objective: Use the Elevator/Emergency Stairwell

- You can use either the stairs or the elevator. If you use the elevator, you'll have to fight some battles on the way up. If you take the stairs, you'll have to listen to Barret complain the entire way up. Me, I would try to keep The Big Guy happy and take the elevator.
- Talk to the hologram, and open a door to the East of you. Take the path N and ride the escalator up to the 60th floor.

##### Objective: Playing the Tourist

- Go through the Memorial Museum and exit through the SE door into the hallway to the South. You have to walk around a bit and let the Guide finish her spiel before you can open the door to the SW. The path is shown below.

- Take the SE exit from the Business Division Exhibit and again walk around a bit and let the Guide finish so that the door will open. Exit to the North.
- Go around the model of Midgar and use your keycard to exit North. Go upstairs and into the Cosmos Theater.
- Use your keycard and exit the theater to go have a chat with the Mayor.

##### Objective: A Helping Hand

- When you exit the Mayor's office, **STOP AND TALK WITH HART**, the guy who escorted you up here. He offers information in exchange for compensation. Pay him the 10K. In addition to his useless information, he will give you an EKG Cannon for Barret.
- Open the Chest at the North end of this circular area, and you'll get back 3k of you 10K investment.

- Use the door at the SE position on the circular path to go down a level, and exit North from there.
- Take the escalator up to 63 and use your keycard to enter the area to the South. Head West toward the vending machines and exit South through the door that is there.
- Go up the sweeping staircase to 63F Upper Level -- Recreation Facility. At the SE end, you'll see a blue bench and a vending machine that will give you -- ta da! -- **Music Disc #31**, to complete your collection and earn you the Disc Jockey Trophy.
- While you're up here, you might want to check out the Shop. Among other things, you can buy a Supreme Bracer for a cool 6400 gil. The Supreme Bracer only has 3 Materia slots, but it offers +64 Defense as well as +16 Magic Defense. (Don't buy more than one of these. You're going to get one for almost free in a moment, and a better Bracer will come your way in Chapter 17).
- Talk to the guy in front of the Combat Simulator/ You need to defeat 2 Cutters, which can be simplified by equipping the **Lightning**-**Elemental**Materia combination on 2 of your characters. You can also choose Easy Mode. Be sure to use the Abilities of your new weapons to gain some proficiency.

##### Objective: Reconnaissance

- Backtrack down the sweeping staircase and exit out of the Recreational Facility through the door to the N. Continue E and then N for a scene.
- Take the escalator up twice to arrive at 64F and use your keycard to go South through the door. Follow the marker to the bathroom.
- Head up into the ductwork and crawl towards the marker, which, it seems, would go a lot faster if Cloud had left his huge sword behind with Barret.
- Keep your eyes peeled for a **HP Up Materia** at a dead end to your left just before reaching the marker.

##### Objective: Operation: Save Aerith

From here, navigating becomes a lot easier. You follow straightforward (I'll bet you were really getting tired of the word "linear" weren't you?) paths.

- You will pass a **Bench** ((1) in the map below) on your way to Hojo's lab. You shouldn't need to heal, but you might want to buy some Star Pendants from the vending machine to prepare for the upcoming boss battle. Read the box below to help you decide.

- When Barret says, "Hojo's gettin' away," be sure to heal at the nearby **Bench** (2) before following him into the elevator. Make sure that everyone has **Fire Materia** and **Lightning Materia** for the upcoming battles. Consider giving Cloud the **Fire** \- **Magnify** Materia pair.

#### Final Chapter 16 Battles

- Hop on the nearby elevator and ride it to your next battles. You'll be fighting two batches of Shinra bad guys.
- The first batch is just a bunch of chumps consisting of 2 each Elite Security Officers, Elite Grenadiers, and Elite Shock Troopers. They are all weak against Fire.
  - Before casting a spell, have Cloud immediately move way over to his right. You want to hit both Elite Riot Troopers so that you can knock down their shields, but they are way out to the sides. If you stand more or less in front of the one who was originally on Cloud's right, you can hit all the enemies with a Magnified Firaga spell.
  - Switch to Barret and have him execute his Overcharge and then follow with Maximum Fury. These attacks will track any survivors and finish off most of them. Have Tifa move in and clean up as necessary.

- Once the first group is dispatched, you face 2 Armored Shock Troopers, shown below. They have very high Defense (259) against physical attacks, but a relatively low Magic Defense (31) and are weak against Thunder, so you want to take them out with Magic. However, there's a catch.
- Once they reach half health, they execute an action called "Eject" which has 2 bad consequences.
  - First, it causes an AOE explosion that can do some serious damage.
  - Second, you will then need to defeat the Enhanced Shock Trooper that emerges from the shell of the Armored Shock Trooper.

- Consequently, you want to damage the Armored Shock Trooper (AST) to just above half health, and then finish it off convincingly. Here's what worked for me:
  - I had Barret (whose Magic stat is lower than Cloud's) hit one AST with Thundaga. This reduced its HP by about 40% and did some damage to the other AST.
  - A Thundaga from Cloud then finished the first one handily and reduced the HP of the second one to about 60%.
  - I had to build up a little more ATB, but once Cloud and Barret each had an ATB bar, I had Cloud use Thundaga and then while that spell was charging up, I had Barret use Thundara. This way, the 2 spells hit at about the same time and the AST didn't have time to perform Eject.
  - In Hard Mode, 2 Thundagas didn't finish off the first AST. To fix this, I had Tifa follow with a Thundara, and that did the trick.
- You are going to face these enemies again in a Combat Simulator. In that setting, they do not perform Eject.
- Even if one AST performs Eject, the battle isn't much tougher. Here's the deal with Enhanced Shock Troopers (EST):

  - The Enhanced versions have stats that are the reverse of the Armored versions -- low Defense (31) but high Magic Defense (259). This suggests using physical attacks against them.
  - The problem is that they move so fast that they can be very difficult to hit with a melee attack.
  - The solution is to hit it with a Firaga spell. This won't do much damage, but it should Pressure an Enhanced Shock Trooper and knock him down. From here you should be able to Stagger and finish him easily.
  - If you let them hang around, beware of this: When they stop skating and start spinning, you need to get out of their way. The spinning can suck in a member of your team and hit him repeatedly for lots of damage.
  - Cloud's Punisher Mode Parry is effective against their normal attacks while they are just skating around but is useless against their spinning.
  - Barret's ranged attacks can be executed from a safe distance, and they will track a moving target, but they just don't do much damage. Your best strategy is to Pressure with a Firaga spell, Stagger, and pound.
- You're going to face EST again in a Combat Simulator, so remember that you can just knock them down with Firaga and Stagger them from there.

Afterwards, you get a joyous reunion and make a new friend before Cloud relives some disturbing memories.

Just 2 more Chapters to go.

## Chapter 17 -- Deliverance from Chaos

| **Important Points to Keep in Mind**<br>- This is a really long and really difficult Chapter with no fewer than FOUR boss fights.<br>- In this Chapter, we pick up our last 2 weapons. Learn their abilities, and the Weapons Expert Trophy will be yours!<br>- I'll give more detail than usual about how to proceed.<br>- You'll be switching back and forth between two parties of 2. Find Materia combinations that you can live with because it gets really tiring constantly redistributing Materia among your characters.<br>- Along the way, we will pick up 3 pieces of armor capable of holding 4 orbs of Materia each. One will come automatically, but be sure not to miss the other 2. In these latter stages of the game, maximum number of Materia slots will become increasingly important. |

#### Walkthrough:

##### Objective: A Way Out

- Don't rush away after the opening scenes! The room in which this Chapter starts has a purple Chest containing a Reinforced Staff for Aerith. She still probably hasn't learned the Lustrous Shield ability from her Bladed Staff, so complete that first then switch weapons.
- Aerith is now on your team, and she probably has no Materia equipped. While you're fixing that, you may want to update her armor also.

- After the first battle, you will find a Bench and vending machine in the E side of the area. N of there is the Combat Simulator. You will be spending a good deal of time there eventually, but right now it isn't available.

##### Objective: Find the Others

- When Cloud is by himself in The Drum - Level 1, be sure to explore that dead-end branch to the E for an orb of **Poison Materia**.

- Keep going until Cloud reaches the entrance to 1st Ward. He can rest at the **Bench** beside the entrance.
- Before entering the 1st Ward, equip the armor on both Cloud and Barret with the **Lightning**-**Elemental** combo in preparation for the upcoming battle against a M.O.T.H. unit. This will reduce damage done by an upcoming enemy's really powerful Lightning attacks.
  - If you feel like getting little more mileage out of your **Steal Materia**, equip it for the battle against the M.O.T.H Unit. This enemy is the only source of the Iron Maiden armor, which provides a whopping +124 Defense as well as +20 Magic Defense. The down side is that it has NO Materia slots.
  - M.O.T.H. Unit begins the battle surrounded by rotating blades which will prevent Cloud from getting in close for melee attacks, so don't even try. Barret's guns can still do damage, and Cloud's Punisher Mode Parry is very effective against the enemy's physical attacks.
  - Once the enemy sustains sufficient damage, it will drop those blades, allowing Cloud to beat it to death with his Punisher Mode.

- After defeating the M.O.T.H. unit, cheapskates like me will backtrack to the **Bench** outside the 1st Ward and heal for free. There isn't another opportunity to heal for free before the next battle.
- Return to where you fought the M.O.T.H. Exit and you will be locked outside. Nearby you will find the last weapon in the game, Cloud's Twin Stinger.

You will be split into 2 teams of 2 -- the gentlemen and the ladies. Your actions may not always be obvious, so I'll walk you through this section of the Chapter.

I'll also be pointing out the locations of Mako Shards, to help reassure you that it's okay to use Magic. Remember that each Mako Shard replenishes 10% of each team member's MP.

- Have Cloud and Barret follow the path to the far side of the room in which Tifa and Aerith find themselves to a PHS Terminal (com device). When prompted, switch parties to Tifa and Aerith.

**Tifa and Aerith**

- They will go to the entrance to the 3rd Ward. A **Bench** is there, but they probably don't need it. Throw a switch and then use the nearby PHS Terminal and switch back to Cloud and Barret.

**Cloud and Barret**

- Keep both Cloud and Barret equipped with the **Lightning**-**Elemental** combo for the upcoming fight against a Blast-Ray.
- Remember that when Staggered, Blast Rays put up a force field that prevents you from getting close to them. You can still damage them with Barret's guns, Thunder-based spells, and Cloud's Blade Burst ability.
- Pull the switch and go over to the Central Terminal. While you're there, pick up the **Time Materia** lying on the ground. Then open the door to the 3rd Ward and use the nearby PHS Terminal to switch teams.

**Tifa and Aerith**

- Tifa and Aerith will enter the 3rd Ward, open a door, and fight some Unknown Entities. Afterwards, they can backtrack to the **Bench** outside the entrance to heal without using MP.
- Next, the ladies will fight a bunch of Bloodhounds, so have **Ice** on Tifa's gloves and somewhere on Aerith. After the fight, squeeze through the opening and smash the crates blocking the doorway for a **Mako Shard**.
- Defeat the Zenene, but before going up the stairs, smash the crates underneath them for a **Mako Shard**.
- After you fall to the floor, fight the 2 Zenenes. BEFORE YOU LEAVE THIS ROOM, go to the NE corner and throw a switch.

- The gate will open, giving you access to a chest containing a **Force Bracelet**.

With only +9 Defense and +9 Magic Defense, the Force Bracelet seems like a pretty crappy piece of armor. However, it is one of a very few pieces of armor with FOUR Materia slots. That extra Materia slot becomes more important as we near completion of the game.

- Head SW to go outside. Go down the stairs, and head S to the Crates for a **Mako Shard**.
- Head N to fight some mechs. As soon as the battle starts, hit the "Options" button and choose "Retry from Last Battle". This should cause the Crates to the S to regenerate.
- When the battle ends, backtrack to the Crates to the S and grab some more MP from the **Mako Shard** therein.
- Climb the ladder, pull a switch, and head across the pods to another **Mako Shard** and a PHS Terminal. Call the guys and switch parties.

**Cloud and Barret**

- Open the door to the 2nd Ward. Head over there and heal at the Bench before going inside.
- Enter the 2nd Ward and fight 2 Zenenes. As you head SW down a passage, you will find a pile of crates with THREE **Mako Shards**.
- Next up is another M.O.T.H. unit, so equip accordingly. After this battle, you will find 4 **Mako Shards**, so you don't need to skimp with your MP.
- In the NE-SW hall there is a **Mako Shard** at the dead end to the NE and 3 more **Mako Shards** at the SW end. Wow!
- Next up are 3 Sledgeworms (one of which is pictured above). I REALLY hate these things!
  - They can Poison you, which is bad enough but . . .
  - Worse yet is that they will hit you with Stop and then just pound on you while you stand there defenseless. So, equip Protective Boots to prevent Stop and just deal with being Poisoned.
  - They only appear briefly before diving back down out of your reach, so you generally can't use Thundaga because of its long charge time. Stick to Thunder, Barret's guns and Cloud's attacks when he can get close enough.
- Exit E and head up the stairs and go NE to the dead end room for a **Mako Shard**.
- Before you climb those stairs, **explore the SW corner of the 2nd Ward - Control Deck area!** You will find a chest containing the one and only **Chain Bangle** in the game at the spot circled in the screenshot below.

With +50 Defense, +50 Magic Defense, and 4 Materia slots, the Chain Bangle is arguably the best armor in the game. Sadly, you only get one. If you return here in postgame play, you just get a Potion.

- Head up the stairs to Level 6 where you'll find another pile of Crates with 3 **Mako Shards**.
- Cloud and Barret will fight a room full of Brain Pods.
  - Brain Pods will Poison anything in sight, and they can hit you with Stop.
  - Equipping Protective Boots will prevent being Stopped. At this point, you really can't do anything about being Poisoned, but you can do 2 things to make this battle as short as possible.
    - Equip Barret with the **Elemental**-**Lightning** pair on his weapon.
    - Equip Cloud with the **Magnify** \- **Lightning** pair.
  - Use physical attacks to whittle the first Brain Pod's HP down to half. It will then summon 11 weaker versions of itself into the fray.
  - Have Cloud cast Magnified Thundara spells on as many of the mob as he can hit at once while Barret follows with his Overcharge and Maximum Fury. Even though the picture below makes things look bad for our boys, they should be victorious rather quickly.

- Exit NE to go outside. Go upstairs to the entrance to the 4th Ward.
- Heal at the Bench, then go down the stairs on the W side. On your way to a PHS Terminal, you get sidetracked by another mission. Call and switch parties.

**Tifa and Aerith**

- Equip **Lightning Materia,****Fire Materia** and **Ice Materia** on both team members, and equip Protective Boots for the upcoming battle against Sledgeworms.
- Go NW to defeat the Sledgeworms with Thunder and then descend the ladder.
- At the bottom of the ladder, Tifa and Aerith will then have to defeat 2 Enhanced Shock Troopers. Remember that a Fira or Firaga spell will knock them down, and after that Tifa's physical attacks will do the most damage.

- After the battle, go almost due S to get to Red and defeat all the Unknown Entities. The crate at the S end of the area has a **Mako Shard**.
- Take the S stairs to Level 4 and, with Red's assistance, head N towards the elevator which, of course, doesn't work yet.
- Pull the switch, fight a battle, and go over to the Central Control Terminal to open up the 4th Ward.
- You probably want to heal before switching back to the guys. This is your last chance before a boss fight.
- When you're ready, call Cloud to switch parties.

**Cloud and Barret**

- Go back SE and up to the entrance to the 4th Ward. Rest at the **Bench** and equip your teams per the tips below before heading inside and initiating the boss battle.

Defeating Swordipede will earn you a second Force Bracelet.

- After the battle, the ladies will go down a tunnel and throw a switch to reunite with the team, though not before giving the guys a scare.
- It is possible to backtrack to the previous Bench and heal, but there is no need. You will pass another Bench before fighting the next battle.

##### Objective: The Ominous Trail

- Call the elevator and ride it to the top floor. Head NE, the way that Red indicates. Follow the blue slime to 69F and then to 70F. Tifa will point you towards a bench and the source of the pleading.

Hang a bit with the President of Shinra, then head towards your next boss fight, in which Cloud, Tifa, and Aerith will be your team.

- Before proceeding, I would advise you to equip Cloud for Boss Fight #3 below AND equip Barret and Aerith for Boss Fight #4 below. This way, if you need to retry Boss Fight #4, you won't have to equip for the battle repeatedly.
- Rest up before heading towards the marker and climbing up the very long ladder. Cloud has to fight this one on his own.

- The focus will shift to Barret and Aerith. When they are in the elevator passing Wedge, watch the lower left corner of the screen for the Menu/Square button symbol. Push and hold Square so that you can access the Main Menu and swap out equipment before the last battle of the Chapter. (If you forget to do this, you can just Retry at the beginning of the next battle).

The team has to make a daring escape. Let's move on to Chapter 18 (in a stylishly retro vehicle) to see how that turns out!

## Chapter 18 -- Destiny's Crossroads

| **Important Points to Keep in Mind**<br>- There are no Trophy-related items to collect here. We just need to get through this Chapter so that we can access Chapter Selection.<br>- Chapter 18 is basically another motorcycle chase followed by one long battle in 6 phases with only one opportunity to recover HP and MP.<br>  <br>- You will eventually use all 4 characters, and you will have an opportunity to rearrange equipment and Materia before the final phase. |

#### Walkthrough

The Chapter starts with another motorcycle battle. Oh joy. You get your HP at least partially replenished after each stage.

(This paragraph is a rant against this particular battle. Feel free to skip it.) This battle, like the one in Chapter 3, goes on WAY too long and is WAY too frustrating and tedious. The time that you are able to attack is just way too short compared to the the time that you can do nothing except play defense. The game designers seem to have been aware of this, as evidenced by the fact that in Chapter Selection, you can skip this part altogether. Furthermore, although it is generally not productive to try to apply logic to video games, I have to wonder this: If Motor Ball has the ability to stay ahead of you and safely pelt you with attacks, why on earth would it ever slow down and let you near it?

Now that I have THAT off my chest, here is what you are up against.

- In the first stage, you'll be pestered by a bunch of motorcycles, but the big truck is the greatest threat to your health. You cannot go fast enough to pull up beside it for a spin attack, so you're going to have to use your long-range attacks on it. While you're waiting for that meter to fill, you're going to have to whittle away at the motorcycles and play defense.
- In the next stage, you'll be fighting a M.O.T.H. Unit - type enemy. It will outrun you about 90% of the time, so again you're going to have to rely primarily on long-range attacks and defense. It will sling a bunch of whirling electrified arcs at you that you need to dodge. It telegraphs these attacks pretty clearly, but dodging them is a whole 'nother ball game. Good luck.
- In the next phase, you're up against a couple of motorcycles that can set off fire bombs in front of you -- bombs that are somehow perfectly timed to detonate when you cross their plane. You have to try to take out the motorcycles with your sword while dodging the fire bombs.

The last stage is a boss fight against Motor Ball. I'll summarize the basics in the lavender box below.

You now face the multi-phase battle described earlier. I'll do my best to describe what is going on and a way to deal with each situation. The action will frequently be interrupted by cutscenes, but I see no reason to detail those here.

Remember that this battle will involve all 4 members of your team, so be sure that all of them are equipped and ready for battle before moving forward. **Make sure that Cloud is equipped with** **Steadfast Block Materia**! His primary strategy is going to be guarding and counterattacking. He will also be focusing on a Fire-based enemy, so equip his armor with the **Fire**-**Elemental** pair to absorb Magic attacks.

##### Phase 1 -- Barret, Cloud, and Tifa vs. 4 Whispers (well -- really 3)

Your long-term goal is to defeat Whisper Harbinger, but you do so not by attacking it directly but rather by damaging the other Whispers.

The Whispers are brutal attackers who will interrupt most of your attempts to hit them directly. Whisper Rubrum (the red one) will mainly attack Cloud, so your best strategy is to have Cloud focus on him.

- Barret and Tifa won't be doing much just yet, but they will take some damage.
  - Have Barret use Steelskin to reduce the damage he takes directly.
  - You might want to consider having him use Lifesaver as well. If he takes Tifa's damage too, you'll only have to heal one character instead of 2.
  - You can equip Barret's armor with **Elemental-Wind** pair to absorb the Aeroga attacks from Croceo, the yellow Whisper that focuses on Barret.
- Nearly all of Rubrum's attacks are melee physical attacks. For these, you want Cloud in Punisher Mode and guarding (R1) so that he will counterattack.
- Crimson Arc and Firaga are Magic attacks.
  - Firaga is actually your friend, because this will restore Cloud's HP. When Rubrum announces Firaga is coming, Cloud can attack without fear of knockback.
  - For Crimson Arc, you have 3 options:
    - Use Counterstance;
    - Dodge as best you can; or
    - Guard (R1) in Operator Mode. For some reason, Guarding in Punisher Mode won't deflect Magic attacks. Counterstance is generally your best choice because, if done successfully, it does significant damage to the enemy.
- Cloud's ATB should be used primarily for Counterstance and Triple Slash. Triple Slash executes quickly and is difficult for enemies to block, so it is a good way to damage all 3 Whispers.

Until Rubrum gets Pressured, your job is to monitor his coming actions and prepare accordingly, by dodging, using Counterstance, or entering Punisher Mode and guarding. Use your teammates primarily for healing and for pouring on the damage when a Whisper gets Pressured.

You probably haven't used Counterstance very much. You might want to run through this first Phase a few times to get the feel for the timing before moving on. The more practice you get, the fewer MP you'll use healing.

In this first Phase, you don't have to actually defeat anything. The next phase starts when you have inflicted a certain amount of total damage on the 3 Whispers.

##### Phase 2 -- Barret, Cloud, and Tifa vs. 4 Whispers (well -- still really 3)

Play this Phase exactly the same way you did Phase 1.

##### Phase 3 -- Barret, Cloud, and Tifa vs. Whispers Harbinger and Rubrum

This should be a bit easier than the first 2 Phases because you're only fighting one Whisper. Your strategy from the first 2 Phases still works here. Once you Stagger Rubrum, the damage you inflict on him will also be magnified and conveyed to Whisper Harbinger. When you have inflicted sufficient damage, you move on to the next Phase.

##### Phase 4 -- Cloud and Barret vs. Whispers Harbinger, Viridi, and Croceo

You have to defeat both Viridi and Croceo to advance to the next Phase.

- Your Punisher Mode-Counter strategy isn't going to work very well because most of their attacks are Magic and not physical.
- Counterstance is still effective, but you're going to need to do some direct attacks.
- Barret's ranged attacks will connect, but your attacks are going to be constantly interrupted.
- Have Barret use Steelskin to increase his Interrupt Defense.
- Concentrate on one Whisper at a time. I recommend taking out Viridi first because his attacks can stun you.
- If a Whisper survives a Stagger, its HP meter will be completely refilled and you have to start over on it. You don't want this to happen.

##### Phase 5 -- Cloud, Tifa, and Aerith vs. 4 Whispers Who Morph into Whisper Bahamut

After dealing a small amount of damage to the usual trio of Whispers, they will unite and form Whisper Bahamut. Yuk!

Top level spells like Firaga go a long ways towards Pressuring him, so have Aerith set up an Arcane Ward and make good use of all those MP she brings to the fight. This Bahamut also has a Mega-Flare, which he discharges very early on. Fortunately, it isn't nearly as devastating as the one that real Bahamut has. What a relief! If you have the Gotterdammerung equipped, this is a good time to unleash that stockpiled Limit Break.

Once Whisper Bahamut goes down, you still have to take out the individual Whispers, but this should be relatively easy. When they are down, Whisper Harbinger still has a few HP. Have Aerith hit him with a double Thundara from her Arcane Ward or a Ray of Judgement, and he should go down. As a bonus, having Aerith finish off Whisper Harbinger should ensure her a place on your team for the final battle against Sephiroth.

Defeating Whisper Harbinger gets your team's HP and MP restored (HP only in Hard Mode).

After much chaos and destruction, Sephiroth will come gliding down and face Cloud Just before he alights, you will see the Square button sign in the lower left of the screen. That's your cue to press and hold Square so that you can regroup before the last phase. Unfortunately, you aren't informed as to who your final team is going to be, so do your best to plan for any eventuality.

##### Phase 6 -- Cloud + 2 Allies vs. Sephiroth

Cloud's allies in the last battle are determined by their contributions to the first 5 Phases. Whoever earns the fewest "points" is out. The normal preferred team is Cloud, Tifa, and Aerith, so consider using Barret in more of a Tanking role against the Whispers so that he is left out of this last Phase.

This final battle is fought in 4 parts. Cloud is solo in the first part, then an ally joins in each of the next 2 parts.

- Sephiroth is extremely aggressive, and your opportunities to get in attacks will be few and far between. Basically, you want to use the same strategy you used against the Whispers:
  - You want to use Cloud's Punisher Mode Counter against melee attacks.
  - You want to do your best to dodge Magic and AOE attacks. Guarding while in Operator Mode will reduce damage from Magic attacks and can help you dodge Ice attacks before they explode and do serious damage.
  - You want **Steadfast Block Materia** on Cloud so that he gains ATB while guarding.
- Sephiroth will give you a few tiny windows for attacking:
  - Magic spells like Firaga have long charge times. When you see one of these coming, hit Sephiroth with a Focused Thrust to push him towards Stagger.
  - After you Counter a Telluric Fury, you should have enough ATB and time to execute a Focused Thrust.
- I would advise you to hold off using any Limit Breaks until the last part of this battle. For one thing, Sephiroth often moves around so quickly that there is a high probability that your Limit Break will miss him, and you don't want to waste a Limit Break.
- Sephiroth has a multitude of powerful abilities, but be especially vigilant when you see him prepare "Heartless Angel". This attack will reduce the HP of anyone in range to 1, so run as far away as possible when you see this ability charging.
- When the final part begins, Sephiroth will be at around 15% health and will use an ability called "Divine Proclamation." He will start a (mercifully slow) countdown from 9, and if that countdown finishes, it is Game Over. Therefore you'll want as much firepower as possible.

There is much more to this battle than I have described here. The GameFAQS guide by Lost\_Nemo10 does an excellent job of describing the details of this battle, so I would direct you there if you are having trouble or just want more information. For what it's worth, even with minimal knowledge of the mechanics of this battle, I have never had a "Game Over" in Chapter 18, so I'm confident that you will succeed.

Okay. That's the end of that, but there is still much for us to do. The game will give you some good information about how things work from here. I'll summarize their key points for you in a little bit, but first let's pause and check our progress towards our goal of earning all 54 Trophies.

## Look at Where We Are

I think that it is a good idea for us to occasionally get our bearings -- to look at what we have accomplished towards our goal of earning the Platinum Trophy for this game and what we have left to do. I will then suggest a strategy for moving forward.

First, I suggest that you go to System > Play Log. It will detail 15 aspects of the game and how much of each you have completed. It is not necessary to complete Enemy Intel Collected, Enemies Assessed, Manuscripts Obtained, or Chapter 14 Resolutions Witnessed. The other 11 must be done to earn the Platinum. At this point, you probably only have completed Music Discs Collected and possibly Weapon Abilities Learned.

Now hit the Playstation button so that you can see which Trophies you have collected so far. I'm going to make 3 lists for you -- Trophies you should have, Trophies that you may or may not have, and Trophies that you definitely do not have yet. I will list these in the same order in which they would appear in your Trophy list for your convenience.

You should definitely have the 39 Trophies listed below right now. If you are missing any of these, you should consult a list of Trophies either in this guide or online and figure out what you are missing and what you need to do to rectify that. I am going to discuss these no further.

- The 34 Trophies from Onetime Gig through Sultan of Squat inclusive (1)
- Returning Champion
- Snappy Dresser
- Divine Gratitude
- The Johnny Experience
- Disc Jockey

**Footnotes:**

(1) Sorry, but I'm too lazy to type all 34 Trophy names here.

##### Trophies That You May or May Not Have Yet

- Biker Boy
- Whack-a-Box Wunderkind
- Peeress of Pullups
- Weapons Expert

Here's what you can do about these:

- Biker Boy is at the beginning of Chapter 4. Choose Chapter 4 from Chapter Selection, opt for Easy Mode, and do your best. I would advise watching some videos before undertaking this to get the benefit of the experience of someone who has been more successful at this than I have.
- Whack-a-Box Wunderkind and Peeress of Pullups are both in Chapter 14, and it's pretty quick to get from the beginning of the Chapter to a point where you can try these. The former is in the kids' hideout in Sector 5 Slums (where you already are at the beginning of Chapter 14 anyway), and for the latter you can just hitch a ride over to Wall Market. These are best done at times when you don't have other goals you are trying to meet so that you can give them your full attention.
- If you don't have the Weapons Expert Trophy, just see which abilities you are missing and work on learning them. If you go to the Main Menu under Materia and Equipment, choose a character and select the currently equipped weapon. To the right you will see the proficiency gained so far. You can then scroll down the list of weapons for that character and see which abilities have less than 100% proficiency.

That should take care of those 4 Trophies eventually. No hurry.

You almost certainly do not have the following 11 Trophies, and I will help you take the steps necessary to earn these.

- Master of Fate
- Dancing Queen
- Dressed to the Nines
- Best in the Business
- Building Character
- Staggering Feat
- Intelligence Agent
- Master of Mimicry
- That's the Smell
- Ultimate Weapon
- Hardened Veteran

I'm going to propose that we begin by repeating Chapters 3, 8, and 9 (in that order) using Easy Mode. This will be primarily to pick up 3 more dresses towards our Dressed to the Nines Trophy. We will then visit just the early portions Chapters 14 and 17, primarily to prepare for Hard Mode. By revisiting these 5 chapters, we should be able to pick up the following 7 Trophies:

- Dancing Queen
- Building Character
- Staggering Feat
- Intelligence Agent
- Master of Mimicry
- That's the Smell
- Ultimate Weapon

After that, you will need to play through the entire game again in Hard Mode, and that is going to take you a while. Doing so should earn you the last 4 Trophies:

- Best in the Business
- Master of Fate
- Dressed to the Nines
- Hardened Veteran

Okay. We have a plan now. Before we execute it, let's make sure that we understand how Chapter Selection works.

## How Chapter Selection Works

Here are some key points about Chapter Selection. Some of this information is not described very clearly in the game, so you might want to read this.

- As the name suggests, Chapter Selection allows you to choose to replay any Chapter in the game.
- You may choose to replay that Chapter at any level of difficulty.
- The level of difficulty that you choose does not influence the EXP or the AP earned.
- In Chapter Selection, you earn twice the normal amount of EXP (!!) and triple the usual amount of AP (!!!)
- EXP levels are capped at 50.
- All Items, Weapons, Materia, EXP earned, and AP earned in your first playthrough are carried over into Chapter Selection.
- Whenever you choose a Chapter, you always begin at the start of the Chapter, even if you chose that Chapter earlier and play partially through it and then left it.
- Any items you pick up while in Chapter Selection stay with you from that point on (unless, of course, you sell or use them).

Let's say, for example, that you missed the Twin Stinger on your first time through the game. You can go into Chapter 17 and play through just until you pick up that weapon. If you then jump to another Chapter, you still get to keep the Twin Stinger. However, . . .

- **Actions that you take that influence future events are NOT retained unless you complete the entire Chapter in which you took those actions.**

Basically, the game keeps track of your most recent completion of each Chapter. So, when you do Chapter 3 differently than you did the first time in order to unlock a different dress for Tifa, you MUST complete ALL of Chapter 3 in order for that new dress to appear.

- Your list of Quests completed in a given Chapter is wiped clean every time you start that Chapter again. You still get credit for having completed them, but they will not show up as completed if you choose your Map and hit R2.

This one seemed kind of odd to me at first, but it is necessary because of the mutually exclusive Quests in Chapter 9 that I previously described.

- One-time items like weapons, Music Discs and some rare Materia (like the Magnify Materia) will not respawn on subsequent passages through the game.
- Common Materia (like the Ice Materia in Chapter 3) will respawn. Most Chest contents will reappear, but if they do they are almost certainly something that you don't need.
- Trophy information is preserved. Once you earn a Trophy, you keep it and do not have to earn it again during Chapter Selection.

Perhaps our main takeaway from all this should be the item in **blue** above. We are about to spend a LOT of time making another set of 3 dresses appear in Chapter 9. You need to be sure to play through all the way to the ends of Chapters 3 and 8 so that you don't have to repeat those Chapters again.

The Chapter Selection Screen looks something like this:

Observe that the screenshot above reminds me that I have already completed Chapters 11, 12, 13, and 14 in Hard Mode. It also shows that I have yet to earn 1, 1, 4, and 2 Manuscripts in Chapters 15, 16, 17, and 18 respectively and that I did not leave any Music Discs or Manuscripts behind in Chapters 11 - 14 inclusive.

Okay -- that should be enough background. In the Main Menu, choose System and then Chapter Selection. You will get a message that conveys what I tried to communicate in the 13 bullet points above, only it does so much more concisely. Hit X to confirm, choose Chapter 3 and **Normal Difficulty**. Easy Difficulty is tempting, but interestingly, it will actually make our next Trophy harder to win, not easier.

## Three More Dresses

#### Chapter 3 -- Take 2

- Replay [Chapter 3 as before](#chapter-3----home-sweet-slum) (except, of course, you do not have to pick up Music Discs or weapons that you already have) up to the point where you can talk to Chadley.

If you didn't earn the Staggering Feat Trophy earlier, here is the easiest way to do so.

- If Tifa has already learned the Purple Pain's True Strike ability, equip her with the Sonic Strikers for speed. If she doesn't know True Strike, equip the Purple Pains.
- Talk to Chadley and undertake the Fat Chocobo VR Mission.

As mentioned earlier, Fat Chocobo has a long Stagger gauge, giving Tifa sufficient time to boost him to 300%. However, you don't want to kill him off before you get to 300%, and if you are in Easy Mode, that can be a real problem. That's why I advised you at the end of the last section to choose Normal Difficulty.

- Switch to Tifa immediately. We will be controlling her almost exclusively.
- As her ATB gauge fills, have her use Unbridled Strength twice. This should make her Triangle attack read "Rise and Fall."
- Have her start pounding on Fat Chocobo. Do not use her ATB because we want her gauge full when FC Staggers. Likewise, do not use Triangle attacks yet because we are saving those.
- When Tifa is ready, you can switch to Cloud long enough for him to Stagger FC with a Focused Thrust, but change right back to Tifa.
- Have Tifa spend her full ATB charge to use her True Strike ability twice. This will increase Stagger by 60% immediately.
- Have her then use Triangle attacks until she has enough ATB to use True Strike again. Keep alternating between True Strike and Triangle attacks, and FC's Stagger gauge will easily go above 300%.

The True Strike ability made that Trophy pretty easy, right? Ever onward!

- Complete all 6 Quests. As you do so, keep the following in mind:
  - Make sure that Tifa and Cloud learn any previously unlearned weapon abilities. This will save some time later on.
  - As you work through the Quests, you will fight some battles. Remember that you are now earning double EXP and triple AP. Be sure to fill every Materia slot on both active characters. Your priority should be to get one of each of the 12 types of **magic Materia** maxed out to fulfill Intel Report 18.
- When you have completed all 6 Quests, the "Alone at Last" Discovery Event will appear.
- When it does, the only thing that you do differently is that when Tifa asks, "I wonder what would suit me." You are going to choose the response, "**Something...sporty?**" This will unlock her silver dress in Chapter 9.

- Go back to Seventh Heaven. When Cloud is left alone, he has to at least start a game of darts. He can then exit the game and return to his bar stool to advance the story ling.
- Exit to beat up the thugs, and return to the apartments for a scene with Jessie, as before.
- Finish the scene with Jessie, and the game will advance to Chapter 4. You can then Skip the scene with Biggs and Wedge, the bike mini-game, and 2 scenes after the mini-game.
- After that you will have control again. Use Chapter Selection to choose Chapter 8 in Normal (not Easy) Difficulty. Your team has moved up several levels since we were last here, so even at Normal Difficulty, the battles will be over very quickly.

#### Chapter 8--Take 2

Now that you have a new dress for Tifa locked in, it's time to get one for Aerith (although it's bound to be a notch below that dazzling red number she wore earlier).

Aerith's dress is dictated solely by the number of side Quests you complete in this Chapter. Last time through, you did all 6 Quests. This time you are just going to do 3. The dress we are trying to unlock appears if you complete 3 - 5 Side Quests, so we're doing the minimum.

- [Play through the Chapter](#chapter-8----budding-bodyguard) until you get to the Sector 5 Slums and Aerith cuts you loose to see the sights of the town.
- To advance the story line, you have to talk to Oates at the intersection shown below.

- Follow Oates to Aerith and then go rescue the kids.
- When you return to the Hideout, Side Quests become available. This would be a good time to do a manual Save.
- Do the Mysterious Moogle Merchant Quest while you're here. Also, buy any manuscripts you did not get the first time through.
- The only other Quests available now are Kids on Patrol and Weapons on a Rampage. Kids on Patrol is probably a bit quicker, especially since I already gave you all of their locations. [Here](#chapter-8----budding-bodyguard) is a link to their locations.
- Completing the Kids on Patrol Quest unlocks the Verified Hero Quest. For your third Quest, you can either smash boxes or chase down monsters. I'm inclined to do the former because it's probably a bit faster, but it's your call. A possible path to Whack-a-Box victory can be found [here](#chapter-8----budding-bodyguard).
- Whichever you choose, check your Map + R2 to make sure that you have completed exactly 3 Quests.
- Then head towards Aerith's house and kick Rude's behind.
  - Equip Aerith with a Headband to protect her against Rude's attempts to put her to sleep with Sweet Dreams.
  - A dose of Poison and a couple of good Aeroga spells will nearly finish him off.
- Go to Aerith's house and press Triangle. Say that "Yeah", you're going in already. You'll get a warning that this will prevent you from completing active quests, but that's okay. Just answer "Yes" and proceed inside.
- When you leave Aerith's house, take a left and return to her garden for a **MP Up Materia**.

- Finish the Chapter and just let it progress naturally into Chapter 9, because that is where we are going next.

| **VERY IMPORTANT ALERT! PLEASE READ!**<br>If you began your current run through FFVIIR prior to January 20, 2021, you really need to follow [this link](#important-revision-alert) to another section of this guide before going any further. I have made some revisions to this guide that may affect your ability to earn the Dressed to the Nines Trophy in an efficient manner, and I want to make sure that you know the best way to proceed. |

#### Chapter 9 -- Take 2

- [Play through the beginning of Chapter 9](#chapter-9----the-town-that-never-sleeps) again all the way until you reach Evergreen Park.
- This time when Tifa goes by in the carriage, she should be wearing a full-length silver dress with black leopard-skin print.

- Move on towards Wall Market. I strongly recommend that you do a manual save before talking to the Stablehand. The circumstances that unlock Cloud's dress are the most complicated. If you Save now and something goes wrong, you can try again from this point instead of starting the Chapter over again.
- When Sam asks what she looks like, describe Tifa as **a great fighter.**(This choice is neutral, i.e., it gives no points to either Sam's or Madame M's Questline).
- Do NOT talk to the Barker in front of the Inn and do NOT talk with the Innkeeper who is inn-side the hotel.
- Go to the Weapons Shop and purchase several (at least 10 - 15) Revival Earrings. This is a commonly used Accessory during the Simulator Challenge you will have to fight to earn the Ultimate Weapon Trophy. Revival Earrings are also available from most vending machines.
- When Johnny goes running past, ignore him. Do NOT follow him.
- Head over to Corneo's, talk to Leslie, and then head over to Sam's.
- When Sam flips a coin, choose **Tails**. (+1.5 points to Sam's Questline).
- Head to the Honeybee Inn and then over to Madam M's as before. This time when she asks which hand massage Cloud wants, choose **Standard Course - 1000 gil**. (+1 point for Madame M's Questline).
- Afterwards, Cloud will be asked his opinion of Aerith's outfit. Describe Aerith's outfit as **comfortable**. (Neutral -- no points).
- Head over to the Colosseum. When you visit Johnny before your first match, do not interact with the small bottle on the table.
- Win your 4 matches. Afterwards, DO NOT LEAVE THE BUILDING YET. Talk to the Gatekeeper in the lobby and accept his invitation to fight some more. Then go back down below.

There is one challenge that you have not yet done, and that is Aerith vs. Shinra Thugs. We didn't do it last time we were here because it only becomes available in Chapter 14, and Aerith wasn't available then. Now that the game has a completed Chapter 14 recorded, this Challenge is available in Chapter 9 and we can do it now.

Completing all of the Colosseum Challenges is one of the conditions for unlocking the Pride and Joy Prototype, which you have to defeat to earn the Ultimate Weapon Trophy.

- Complete the "Aerith vs. Shinra Thugs" Battle Challenge. It should take about 2 minutes tops. Winning this battle earns you Aerith's Telluric Scriptures, Vol. II Manuscript.
- If there are any weapon abilities that Aerith has not mastered, she absolutely MUST learn them now! Have her fight solo or 2-person fights and use those abilities until she has learned them. Then return to Madam M's.
- Madame M will send Cloud to Sam because his Questline has 1.5 points to Madame M's 1. He will assign you some Quests, but ignore them and read the next box before going on.

| **Check Before Proceeding**<br>This time through, you will get the dress that you seek for Cloud if you simply fail to complete all 3 assigned Quests. Before going on, check Map > R2 and make sure that less than 3 (hopefully zero!) Quests are marked as "Completed." |

- Go straight back to Madame M's. You'll get a warning about abandoning your Quests, but just accept the choice to check on Aerith.
- As before, Johnny will intercept you. Follow him to Corneo's, talk to Leslie, and then head back towards Madame M's.
- On the way, you will meet up with Aerith. She should be wearing a coral dress with spaghetti straps and have a big red bow in her hair.

- At the Honeybee Inn, remember to DO A MANUAL SAVE before talking to the bees in the next room. It is time to claim the Dancing Queen Trophy, and that will probably take a couple of tries.
- You can skip the practice session this time. Do the dance with Andrea and get your rating. If you need a refresher on how this mini-game works, you can find one [here](#chapter-9----the-town-that-never-sleeps).

As best I recall, the Trophy doesn't actually pop until Aerith talks to Cloud outside after the makeover, but that might have been because I skipped the makeover scene itself. If you see the words, "Andrea's Earrings Obtained" in the upper right, then Cloud proved himself and you should get the Trophy. If you don't get the Trophy by the time Aerith and Cloud head over to Corneo's Mansion, load your manual Save and try again. Keep trying until you get it. The dance scene is kind of long, but it shouldn't take you more than a few tries to snag the Trophy.

- After the makeover, Cloud should be in a black and gray dress. He should have white ribbons in his hair and nothing around his neck, and will be looking really happy about all this, as shown below.

In the Main Menu, go to System > Play Log. You should show 2/3 dresses for each of the 3 characters.

I'm about 98% sure that it isn't necessary to complete the Chapter, but I'd like to suggest that you might as well do so anyway. It doesn't take very long, and while you're at Corneo's Mansion, you can and should pick up another Fury Ring. In case you've forgotten where that is, as soon as Tifa and Aerith change back into their usual clothes, have them go downstairs, fight a quick battle, and raid the chest underneath the stairs. You can find pictures [here](#chapter-9----the-town-that-never-sleeps).

Here's a pic of our second set of three dresses:

Well that was certainly productive. We're about to prepare for Hard Mode, but first let's go through a brief tutorial on upgrading weapons.

## Skill Points and Weapon Upgrades

I'm going to warn you ahead of time that this section is going to be a bit wordy, but I feel that most of this is pretty important information that you will want to know going forward. Although I don't recommend doing so, you can save some time by skipping the information in the lavender boxes.

As I promised you in Chapter 3, I'm devoting this section to a description of how SP are obtained and how weapon upgrades work. I'll also explain why you really didn't need this information before now and will give you some free advice for moving forward.

#### Skill Points

Here is a breakdown of all of the Skill Points (SP) available in the game and how they can be earned.

| **Earning Skill Points**<br>- Skill Points can be earned 2 ways -- by going up an EXP level and by obtaining Manuscripts.<br>  - Leveling up will earn you a total of 216 SP.<br>    - Attaining levels 9-49 inclusive will earn you 5 SP each, for a total of 5 x 41 = 205 SP.<br>    - Attaining level 50, for some unknown reason, will earn you 11 SP<br>    - Thus, total SP from levels = 205 + 11 = 216. This is the same for all characters.<br>  - There are 14 Manuscripts for each character, and each Manuscript is worth 10 SP, for a total of 140 SP.<br>- Therefore, each character can earn 216 + 140 = 356 SP.<br>- As mentioned earlier, SP earned can be applied to _each_ weapon. So if, for example, you have accumulated 100 SP, that means that you have 100 SP to spend on each weapon that you currently own as well as each weapon that you eventually acquire. Therefore, there is no need to prioritize one weapon over another.<br>- Here is the breakdown of the 14 Manuscripts available for each character:<br>  - 1 can be purchased from the Moogle Emporium. You should have done this already.<br>  - 1 is earned for completing a Chapter 14 Side Quest. That Side Quest is different for each character, but since we completed them all, you should have already earned this Manuscript for all characters.<br>  - 1 can be earned by completing a solo Colosseum challenge. This can be done in Easy or Normal Mode, and we have already done this for all characters.<br>  - 1 can be earned by winning a solo challenge in the Shinra Combat Simulator. This challenge MUST be fought in Hard Mode. I did not advise doing this in Chapter 17, but you might have done it on your own.<br>  - 10 are only available in Hard Mode during postgame play. |

#### Upgrading Weapons

As the picture at the top of the page illustrates, you can redeem SP for upgrades in a "grid." So far, we have chosen to have that done automatically for us, but you can choose upgrades manually if you prefer. Here is how that works.

| **Using SP to Upgrade Weapons**<br>- Each weapon upgrade grid has a core and up to 5 sub-cores.<br>- Each core and subcore has a certain number of upgrades that can be activated in exchange for SP.<br>- The upgrades in the higher subcores require more SP than the upgrades in the lower subcores, as one might expect.<br>- At any given time, you only have access to certain subcores, depending on your weapon level.<br>- Weapon level is determined by the total SP you have earned, and the maximum weapon level is 6.<br>- Thus, as you gain more SP, more subcores of the upgrade grid will be available to you.<br>- I haven't been able to find the precise relationship between SP and weapon level, but it seems that reaching level 6 requires at least 250 SP for all characters. |

| **MP Regeneration Upgrades**<br>**WARNING! This box contains nerdy math stuff which may induce dizziness and headaches.** **Read at your own risk.**<br>During battle, all characters will automatically regenerate some MP, and there are weapon upgrades that can increase this rate of MP regeneration. Although some guides get very excited about these upgrades, I think we should look at the numbers before judging the value of these upgrades.<br>The normal rate of MP regeneration is 1 MP per 20 seconds of battle time. For many battles, that's a gain of about 0 - 3 MP. Over a 5-minute battle, this is a total of just 15 MP regained. I'd like to suggest that in a tough boss battle, this regeneration is going to do very little to stem to rate at which you will be expending MP for healing alone, let alone for attacking.<br>The percent increases promised in the upgrade descriptions are not correct, but the good news is that the percentages quoted are too low rather than too high. Allow me to explain.<br>If taken literally, the upgrade described as "MP Regeneration 50% boost" would suggest a rate of 50% more MP per second, which would be 1.5 MP per 20 seconds or 9 MP/120 seconds instead of the usual 6 MP/120 seconds. In fact, this 50% boost results in _twice_ as many MP per second, which would be 2 MP per 20 seconds or 12 MP/ 120 seconds. The "50%" figure actually means, "the time required for each MP to regenerate is reduced by 50%," but that really isn't the same as a 50% increase in the regeneration rate, as implied by the description.<br>So, acquiring the "MP Regeneration 50% boost" ability will increase the MP regenerated in a 5-minute battle from 15 to 30. This is a nice boost, I guess, but I certainly wouldn't make this a high priority in selecting the best weapon for a character. I could give you some very convincing and well-reasoned examples of the trade-offs here, but I'm guessing that you get my point and are ready to move on, so let's do that. |

At this point in the game, your total number of SP earned should be pretty close to 190. You probably finished Chapter 18 somewhere around level 40, which would be worth 160 SP. You should have also earned Manuscripts for the Colosseum solo challenge and the Chapter 14 Side Quest plus the one that you bought at the Moogle Emporium. That would be another 30 SP for a total of around 190.

So, even though you set your weapons to auto-upgrade you should have access to all subcores except the last one, and there shouldn't be many upgrades left in the other subcores that you haven't earned yet. I would argue that you would be in almost exactly the same place if you had been upgrading your weapons manually, and that would have taken a whole lot more time and thought. However, . . .

In the next phase of our adventures, we should accumulate enough SP to attain weapon level 6 and unlock that last subcore. After that, our SP gains are going to come relatively slowly since we will have to slug our way through the game again in Hard Mode to earn the last 100 SP. Therefore, **I'm going to advise you to upgrade all weapons manually from this point on** so that we can prioritize those upgrades that will do us the most good. Here is how to do that:

| **Changing Weapon Upgrades from Automatic to Manual**<br>1. Press the OPTIONS button to access the Main Menu.<br>2. Choose UPGRADE WEAPONS.<br>3. Choose the first character (Cloud)<br>4. Highlight the first weapon (Buster Sword) and press TRIANGLE<br>5. Highlight MANUAL and press X<br>6. Choose YES to accept this change.<br>7. Move down to the next weapon (Iron Blade), press TRIANGLE, and repeat steps 5-7 until you have changed all of Cloud's weapons.<br>8. When you have changed the upgrade option for all of Cloud's weapons, hit Circle to go back one screen and repeat steps 3-8 until all weapons for all 4 characters have been set to Manual Upgrade. |

The first thing that we're going to do in the next section is level up Materia, and in doing so, we will max out all of our characters at Level 50. This will bring your SP total up to 246. After that, we will take on that solo Shinra Combat Simulator challenge for one more Manuscript, pushing us over that magic number of 250 and unlocking that last subcore. Once that has happened, you will have access to all abilities that you haven't yet acquired and can make more informed choices about where to spend your SP.

Once you have access to the last subcore, I'm going to advise you to open up Materia slots first because in the challenges ahead, you are going to want to equip as much Materia as possible. After that, activating Reprieve would probably be a good choice whenever it is available.

#### Manuscripts

If you count everything, there are 60 Manuscripts in the game. Four of these are upgrades to the Limit Breaks of the 4 characters. These are important to get, but in a way they don't really count since they don't show up in your Game Log. You already got these from the Colosseum anyway, so for now, let's focus on the other 56 Manuscripts.

The minimum number of Chapters that you need to complete to earn all 54 Trophies is 39. (Chapters 3, 8, and 9 must be completed 3 times each, and the other 15 Chapters must be completed twice each, once in Normal or Easy Mode and once in Hard Mode). Following this guide can earn you all 54 Trophies with this minimum number of Chapters completed. However, if you follow the path outlined in this guide -- and I sincerely hope that you do -- you will NOT obtain all 56 Manuscripts.

When I first wrote this guide, Trophy acquisition with a minimum of repetition was my goal. I didn't consider Manuscripts because a) they didn't lead to Trophies and 2) I frankly didn't know that much about them.

Now that I am older and more knowledgeable about the game, I do see a path for earning all 54 Trophies and all 56 Manuscripts after completing that minimum number of 39 Chapters. However, that path also makes some compromises and does make obtaining the Trophies somewhat more difficult. Consequently, I opted against re-writing the entire guide to describe this alternate path. Instead, I made some minor changes so that you would leave behind only 5 Manuscripts instead of 9 and I'm adding the information below about how to get those remaining Manuscripts with only 2 more Chapter completions instead of the 4 which were required by my original version of this guide.

Obtaining these last few Manuscripts is optional. Four of these are for Aerith and one is for Cloud. Either way, Tifa and Barret will acquire all of their Manuscripts and will be able to fully upgrade all of their weapons. I'll let you decide whether or not you want to pursue this. Personally, I would go after all of the Trophies first, then pursue the Manuscripts, but again that is up to you.

For the record, once you obtain the desired Manuscript(s), you do not need to complete the Chapter. Manuscripts work like Music Discs in that once you get them, you keep them even if you don't complete the Chapter. However, most of these missing Manuscripts are earned very near the ends of the Chapters.

Here are the Manuscripts in question and how you can obtain them:

| **Manuscripts Not Acquired Elsewhere in This Guide**<br>**Cloud's Art of Swordplay Vol. X**<br>You win this Manuscript by defeating the two Bombs in the [A Dynamite Body](#chapter-9----the-town-that-never-sleeps) Side Quest of Chapter 9 in Hard Mode. Therefore, you will need to start Chapter 9 in Hard Mode and play all the way through until Sam offers you this Quest.<br>**In order for this Side Quest to appear, you need to take the actions and give the responses outlined in** [our first run through Chapter 9.](#chapter-9----the-town-that-never-sleeps)<br>Unfortunately, that means that you need to defeat Hell House in Hard Mode a second time, and you are going to find that to be an adventure. However, once you have all of your Trophies, this will be a fun little task to complete, even though it means going through Chapter 9 for a 4th time.<br>**Aerith's Tellluric Scriptures, Volumes V, VI, VII, and VIII**<br>All of these are obtained by completing Side Quests in Chapter 8 in Hard Mode. This means that you need to start Chapter 8 in Hard Mode and play through until Side Quests become available. This is going to entail defeating Rude in Hard Mode a second time, but that is a whole lot easier than beating Hell House again, let me tell you.<br>MP can be restored as necessary using the sources indicated on [this Map](#hard-mode--chapters-3-8-and-9). This will be further explained in [an upcoming section](#completing-chapters-in-hard-mode).<br>The Quests that you need to complete in Hard Mode and the associated enemies are as follows:<br>- Defeat both batches of Monodrive Mk. II in the [Weapons on a Rampage](#chapter-8----budding-bodyguard) Quest for Telluric Scripture Vol. V.<br>- Defeat the Hedgehog Pie King in the [Kids on Patrol](#chapter-8----budding-bodyguard) Quest for Telluric Scripture Vol. VI.<br>- Defeat the Chromogger in the [Angel of the Slums](#chapter-8----budding-bodyguard) Quest for Telluric Scripture Vol. VII.<br>- Defeat the 2 Venomantis in the [Paying Respects](#chapter-8----budding-bodyguard) Quest for Telluric Scripture Vol. VIII.<br>To initiate the Angel of the Slums Quest, you must talk with Oates in the Hideout after completing the Kids on Patrol Quest.<br>Again, going through this Chapter for a 4th time is repetitive (I've probably been through it at least 20 times now) but after you tackle the last few Chapters of the game in Hard Mode, this will seem like a welcome vacation. |

Now it's time to level up our Materia and characters.

## Leveling Up Your Materia and Characters

In this Section, we're going to build our characters and our Materia up to levels appropriate for dealing with the toughest challenges of the game. We'll also complete the requirements for the Intelligence Agent Trophy and earn the Building Character Trophy.

Before we begin, let's make sure that we have all the Materia we're going to need. Again, this list is not comprehensive, but it seems to include most of the Materia that is commonly recommended. Materia is listed in the same order in which it appears in your list.

Don't worry about buying any missing Materia just yet. We'll have a chance to go shopping in just a moment.

|     |     |     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Materia** | **Where Obtained** | **AP to Max** | **Materia** | **Where Obtained** | **AP to Max** |
| **Healing** x 4 | Materia Shop (1) | 1200 | **Warding** x 2 | (5) | 10,000 |
| **Cleansing** x 1 | Materia Shop | 1200 | **AP Up** x 1 | (6) | 0 |
| **Revival** x 4 | Materia Shop (1) | 5000 | **ATB Boost** x 1 | Chadley | 2400 |
| **Fire** x 3 | Materia Shop | 1200 | **HP Up** x 8 | Materia Shop (1) | 10,000 |
| **Ice** x 3 | Materia Shop | 1200 | **MP Up** x 8 | Materia Shop (1) | 10,000 |
| **Lightning** x 3 | Materia Shop | 1200 | **Magic Up** x 2 | (7) | 10,000 |
| **Wind** x 2 | Chadley | 1200 | **Luck Up** x 2 | (8) | 10,000 |
| **Poison** x 2 | Materia Shop | 1200 | **Parry** x 1 | Chadley | 500 |
| **Barrier** x 1 | Materia Shop | 1200 | **First Strike** x 2 | Chadley | 1000 |
| **Binding** x 1 | Materia Shop | 1200 | **ATB Stagger** x 2 | Chadley | 1000 |
| **Subversion** x 2 | Materia Shop | 1200 | **ATB Assist** x 1 | Chadley | 4000 |
| **Time** x 1 | (2) | 1200 | **Steadfast Block** x 2 | Chadley | 1000 |
| **Elemental** x 2 | (3) | 10,000 | **Skill Maste** r x 3 | Chadley | 1000 |
| **Magnify** x 1 | (4) | 2000 |  |  |  |

**Footnotes for Materia That Cannot Be Purchased In Shops**
