1. Generally, we're only planning for Materia for the active team. However, all 4 characters will be participating in the battles in Chapter 18, so each will need 2 orbs of theses 4 crucial Materia.
2. **Time Materia** is a reward for a [Chapter 14 Side Quest](#chapter-14----in-search-of-hope) and can be found during [Chapter 17](#chapter-17----deliverance-from-chaos). Additional orbs can be purchased from vending machines from Chapter 15 onwards, but apparently not from Materia shops.
3. The 2 orbs of **Elemental Materia** can be found in Chapters [6](#chapter-6----light-the-way) and [14](#chapter-14----in-search-of-hope).
4. There is just one **Magnify Materia** in the game, and it is found in [Chapter 9](#chapter-9----the-town-that-never-sleeps).
5. The 2 **Warding Materia** can be found in Chapters [10](#chapter-10----rough-waters) and [13](#chapter-13----a-broken-world).
6. The only **AP Up Materia** in the game evolves from the **Pedometer Materia** found at the beginning of [Chapter 14](#chapter-14----in-search-of-hope).
7. The 2 **Magic Up Materia** can be found in Chapters [7](#chapter-7----a-trap-is-sprung) and [14](#chapter-14----in-search-of-hope).
8. One **Luck Up Materia** is a reward for [winning at darts in Chapter 3](#chapter-3----home-sweet-slum), although you don't actually get the Materia until [Chapter 4](#chapter-4----mad-dash). The other is won in the squat contest in [Chapter 9](#chapter-9----the-town-that-never-sleeps).

The specific locations of all of these Materia are detailed in the appropriate Chapter sections of the main guide. Use the handy hyperlinks to navigate there if needed.

OK -- Let's get started!

- Use Chapter Selection to jump to Chapter 14, and choose Easy Mode. Exit the house and go left for a scene. This time you should get the scene with Tifa. The box below explains why -- feel free to skip it if you aren't interested.

| **Chapter 14 Resolutions (Take 2)**<br>On our second (and most recently completed) pass through Chapter 3, we again completed all 6 quests, which gives Tifa 6 x 2 = 12 points. On our second pass through Chapter 8, we completed just 3 quests, giving Aerith 3 x 2 = 6 points. We didn't repeat Chapter 10, so the 1 point Aerith got on our only pass through it still stands. Even so, Tifa wins 12 to 7, which is why you should get her scene.<br>BTW, it isn't necessary to complete Chapter 14. As soon as you witness the entire Resolution, it will show up in your Play Log, and it won't go away even if you jump to another Chapter before completing Chapter 14. |

- When you exit the house for good, look back towards the garden. You may see a piece of purple Materia shining there. If so, it is another **MP Up**, and you should go grab it.
- Run into town. You will be drawn towards our delightful Kyrie, but skip her scene when you can and head North.
- The Stablehand will have forgotten that you already rounded up his chocobos once and will ask you to do the Quest again. You don't have to do the Quest, and you should still have your Lifetime Pass. Use it to get a free ride to Wall Market.
- Go to the Materia Shop and/or Chadley to purchase Materia as necessary to complete the list above.
- Stop by Corneo's Mansion to pick up a third Fury Ring. A picture showing its location appears at the bottom of [this page.](#chapter-9----the-town-that-never-sleeps) Then head over to the Colosseum.
- If there are any weapon abilities that Barret has not mastered, have him fight a solo battle in Easy Mode and use the ability until it is learned. You should have already learned all abilities for the other 3 characters during Chapters 3, 8, and 9, but Barret didn't appear in any of those Chapters.

Once you have learned all weapon abilities, the Weapons Expert Trophy will appear.

Our next priority is going to be leveling up Materia, and I'm going to recommend doing so in the Colosseum.

| **Where to Farm AP**<br>Others have claimed that going through the battles at the beginning of Chapter 16 repeatedly will yield more AP per hour than the Colosseum, but I've done the math and the numbers seem to contradict that assertion. I'll show my calculations here in case you're interested.<br>A crucial element in the calculations is the amount of time it takes to complete one cycle of battles. For my calculations, I used the numbers from the timers on YouTube videos espousing each method (as opposed to claims made by the narrators). I then verified those times with my own trials. Here's what I found:<br>**Chapter 16:** (279 AP/cycle)(60 min/hr) ÷ (4.25 min/cycle) = 3938.82 AP/hr.<br>**Colosseum:** (165 AP/cycle)(60 min/hr) ÷ (2.25 min/cycle) = 4400 AP/hr.<br>The person who espoused using the Chapter 16 battles claimed a yield of over 8000 AP/hour without using the **AP Up Materia**. In his calculations, he seems to have figured 30 cycles/hour or about 2 minutes/cycle. That simply isn't possible. According to the timer on his own video, it takes nearly 2 minutes just to reload the game and advance the game to the point where you fight the first battle and a full 4 min 15 seconds to complete a cycle.<br>Here's the bottom line: Although Chapter 16 yields more AP per cycle, each cycle takes considerably longer because you have to reload your game each time. The Colosseum yields about 60% as many AP per cycle, but each cycle only takes about half as long. In fact, with a little practice, you can run through a cycle in the Colosseum in about 2 minutes, which would increase the yield to 4950 AP/hr.<br>Overall, I remain convinced of the conventional wisdom that fighting the "Three-Person Team Vs. Shinra Warriors" Challenge in Easy Mode is the best place in the game to harvest AP. |

We are first going to work on **Magic Materia** so that we can complete Battle Intel Report 18. However, the strategy we'll be using to level up our Materia will be the same after we complete that Report, so here is the plan.

##### Step 1: Preparation

- Equip Cloud with his Mythril Saber. His primary job is going to be slinging Magic, so we want to give him the weapon with the highest Magic boost.
- Give Barret and Tifa equipment that has a maximum number of Materia slots. They won't be doing much actual fighting. We mostly need them to carry Materia to level up. This is a good time to use those 4-Materia Slot Force Bracelets and Chain Bangle that you picked up in Chapter 17.
- Cloud needs a linked pair of slots somewhere. Fill those with a maxed out **Fire** and your **Magnify** Materia. Give him a maxed out **First Strike Materia** in any other slot and both of your **Magic Up** Materia.
- Equip each character with a Fury Ring to give their attacks a 30% boost.
- Give any character with a linked pair of slots the **AP Up** \- **Revival** combination. This is because **Revival** is the **Magic Materia** that requires the most AP.
- Fill all remaining slots with Materia. For now, prioritize getting one of each type of your green **Magic Materia** maxed out. After that, your next priority is Materia such as **HP Up** that was on our "must have" list and takes a lot of AP.

##### Step 2: Execution

- Go to the Colosseum and choose the "Three-Person Team Vs. Shinra Warriors" Challenge. Select Easy Mode.
- As soon as the battle begins, have Cloud cast Fira on the enemy closest to the center. Since he has **First Strike Materia** equipped, he doesn't have to wait to fill his ATB gauge. In the first 3 battles, this should instantly destroy all enemies because they are weak against Fire.
- In the 4th battle, cast Fira and center the attack on the Riot Trooper. It will damage the Sentry Gun and Laser Cannon in the rear, but it won't eliminate them. As soon as Fira hits, switch to Barrett. Have Barrett unload his Overcharge ability on the Mechs in the rear, and follow up with Maximum Fury if necessary. This should eliminate those last 2 enemies without having to run all the way to the back to attack them.

- The enemies in the last battle are weak against Ice, not Fire. Cast Firaga anyway. This should Stagger at least one of the Venomantises. Attack manually with your entire team to finish off all 3 enemies.

- After completing all 5 battles, hit the Triangle button to repeat that same Challenge.
- Watch the screen messages for Materia that has improved. Every 5 rounds or so, you should hit X instead of Triangle and then Circle to exit. Take a breather and remove and replace any equipped Materia that has maxed out (except, of course, for the maxed out Materia you gave Cloud in Step 1 above).
- Keep a high-AP Materia paired with your **AP Up Materia** at all times.

| **Fira vs. Firaga**<br>Initially, I advised (as most guides do) having Cloud cast Firaga instead of Fira. Recently though (July 2021) I got an email from alert reader "RPGfreek" who pointed out that Fira is just as effective at eliminating the Fire-weak enemies and has the added benefit of taking less time to cast. A quicker casting means that the enemies have less time to move away from the center of your attack and are less likely to survive. I was concerned that Fira would be too weak a spell when you are first beginning to develop your characters and Materia, but as long as Cloud has a Fury Ring equipped, his Fira spell seems more than strong enough to get the job done.<br>So, special thanks to RPGfreek for this useful tip. |

##### Step 3: Analysis (aka, nerdy math stuff)

With double EXP and triple AP, each round of 5 fights will net your team 4664 EXP and 165 AP. You can easily finish a cycle in under 2 minutes so you can do at least 30 cycles in an hour. That comes to a minimum of 4950 AP and 139,920 EXP per hour. For the one Materia that is paired with your **AP Up Materia,** that makes 9900 AP/hour. Like wow!

Even though Aerith is not in your party, she will benefit from the EXP your party gains during these battles.

- Repeat (still in Easy Mode) the "Three-Person Team Vs. Shinra Warriors" Challenge you just fought until you have maxed out your entire inventory of essential Materia. Keep rotating your Materia as some orbs get maxed out, and always keep a high-needs Materia like **Luck Up** paired with your **AP Up Materia**.

| **Battle Intel Report 18 and MP Absorption Materia**<br>Once you have maxed out one of each of the 12 types of **Magic Materia,** you will have completed the requirements for Battle Intel Report 18. For this, Chadley will sell you a **MP Absorption** Materia for a measly 100 gil. If you want to return to the surface and buy one, be my guest. Personally, I never found much use for this particular Materia.<br>You would think that it would be useful in Hard Mode where MP are at a premium, but the rate of recovery is a paltry 0.1% of damage dealt by paired Magic. So, if you manage to do the maximum of 9999 damage with a Magic spell, you will recover 10 MP. Big whoop. IMHO, that hardly warrants one of your precious Materia slots, but I'll let you make your own decision on this.<br>The good news is that it does not need to be leveled up with AP. This particular materia arrives fully developed. |

- Long before that job is done, your characters should reach their maximum EXP level of 50, thus earning you your Building Character Trophy.
  - Yes, grinding like this is tedious, and yes, this is going to take a while. I hasten to point out that this is the one and only time in this game you will need to do this.
  - Don't try to do too much too fast. Grind for 30 minutes, then Save and do something else for at least 10 minutes before grinding some more. This part of the game will make your brain go numb quickly.
  - Notice, as I mentioned early in this guide, that no specific farming of EXP is necessary. Leveling up your characters comes naturally when you level up your Materia with AP.
- When you have maxed out all of your essential Materia, head to the surface. Talk to Chadley and get credit for Battle Intel Report 18.
- You are now prepared to take on Leviathan. If you followed my advice and began this Chapter in Easy Mode, then you'll fight Leviathan in Easy Mode, which will be, well, relatively simple.

- Once you have defeated Leviathan, you should have completed reports 1 - 19 inclusive. If so, then Chadley will make available Report 20, which is a VR Mission to defeat Bahamat.

- Beating Bahamat will complete your last Battle Intel Report and will therefore earn you the Intelligence Agent Trophy.

| **Status Report**<br>Please observe that all of your characters have now reached the maximum level of 50 and all primary Materia is fully leveled as well. You are still lacking a few weapon upgrades since we haven't collected all of the Manuscripts yet, but otherwise your team is now as powerful as it is going to get. From here on, then, any problems that you have in defeating enemies is going to need to be solved by adjustments in equipment and/or strategy rather than brute force. In other words, this is where the fun begins. |

##### Typical Materia to Equip in Hard Mode

Your equipment will clearly change depending on the battles you will be fighting. Still, here is what I would consider to be a starting point for Materia:

- **HP Up** x 2 on everyone -- to survive those extra-painful enemy attacks
- **MP Up** x 2 on everyone -- since MP won't be restored and you can't use Items
- **Healing** \+ **Magnify** on your healer -- to allow you to heal your whole team at once
- **Magic Up** on your healer -- to get the most out of attack and healing magics
- **Elemental** \+ \[**Fire**, **Ice**, **Lightning**, or **Wind**\] on attackers -- if enemies have an elemental weakness
- **Healing** on both attackers -- in case your healer is Silenced or loses consciousness
- **Revival** on everyone -- for when things go very wrong. Remember that you won't be able to use Phoenix Downs in Hard Mode.

There are plenty of other options -- **Time**, **Poison**, **Barrier**, etc., but the above list seems to constitute a fairly standard starting point.

More advanced strategies such as those found in YouTube videos for winning the "Vs. Top Secrets" Challenge will also require the following **additional Materia**:

- **ATB Boost**
- **ATB Stagger** x 2
- **ATB Assist**
- **Luck Up** x 2
- **Skill Master** x 3
- **Parry**

Now that our team is considerably more powerful, it's time to tackle some combat challenges.

## On To the Shinra Combat Simulator

When you have your Materia properly leveled, head over to Chapter 17 in Easy Mode.

- Go through the opening scenes, fight the battle, and head towards the Combat Simulator, where Chadley is waiting for you.
- Inside the simulator, you will find 5 solo Challenges, a Two-Person Challenge, and a Three-Person Challenge that you haven't yet tackled. Some of these must be done in Hard Mode, so let's take a moment to see how Hard Mode is different.

##### Hard Mode

1. Enemies have more HP and deal more damage.
2. You cannot use consumables (i.e., Items) at any time.
3. Benches restore full HP but NO MP.

So, you're facing tougher enemies and have fewer resources at your disposal which, I suppose, is pretty much what the name implies. Allow me to give you an example of how different enemy stats are in Hard Mode.

One of the toughest bosses for me was Arsenal, the enemy that Barret and Aerith fight at the end of Chapter 17. The following table compares Arsenal's stats in Normal Mode to those in Hard Mode.

|     |     |     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
|  | HP | Attack | Magic Attack | Defense | Magic Defense |
| Normal Mode | 86496 | 297 | 297 | 145 | 197 |
| Hard Mode | 124974 | 508 | 508 | 212 | 288 |

Pretty scary, huh?

Consequently, . . .

1. You are going to have to play more defensively. This will often include running around trying to rebuild ATB and/or defending with R1 and waiting for your opponent to take a break from attacking. You might consider letting your ATB gauge fill completely at the beginning of battles and trying to keep one bar in reserve at all times. This can be a lifesaver!
2. Learning your enemies' attack patterns can really help. You have to learn to identify the proper times to attack and the proper times to back off and wait for an opportunity.
3. To avoid running out of MP, you are going to have to find more creative ways of Healing. Chakra and Prayer are common options, though honestly I never use them. You can also pair a maxed out **Elemental Materia** with a **Magic Materia** such as **Lightning** in your armor to restore HP when an enemy attacks you with that element. You are really going to have to conserve MP and find creative ways to regenerate them. Aerith's Soul Drain skill is a common option.
4. Recharging at benches isn't going to be a problem right now. Since we started Chapter 17 in Easy Mode, the nearby bench will still regenerate all MP. When we tackle entire Chapters in Hard Mode, you are going to want to break all available Shinra crates looking for Mako Shards.

##### On to the Shinra Simulator

All Simulator Battles of Rank 5 and above must be done in Hard Mode.

As far as Simulator battles are concerned, the good news is that 50% of each character's maximum HP and 30% of each character's MP are restored in between battles. The game claims that you will get 50% of your MP restored, but check the numbers some time and you will see that this simply isn't the case.

| **MP Regenerated in the Shinra Simulator**<br>I posted a topic about MP restored between battles on Game FAQs and got a reply from "Snake2410" who confirmed my 30% number. He explained that it was only ever intended that you get 30% restored and that a mistake was made in the on-screen text that says 50%. Thirty percent seems an unusual number to me, and it seems at least as likely that the text is correct but the coding was done incorrectly. Either way, don't count on getting back as many MP as the game claims. |

I'm going to warn you up front that there is going to be a bit of a learning curve here. You will have to undertake the Challenges, see where you failed, and adjust accordingly. I will suggest strategies for you, but feel free to ignore those and try to win the Challenges on your own first.

- First tackle Aerith's Rank 4 solo Challenge first. You can do that one in Easy Mode. Cloud, Tifa, and Barret already won this Challenge in Chapter 16. Notes on how to equip Aerith can be found [here](#chapter-16----the-belly-of-the-beast).
- Next try her Rank 5 solo Challenge (vs 3-C Soldier Candidates) until you win it. Once you do, you should be prepared to fight that same Challenge with Cloud, then Barret, then Tifa. Notes on these Challenges are in the box below.

- Completing those Rank 5 solo Challenges will earn the active character a Manuscript (Vol XIII for Cloud, Tifa, and Barret, and Vol. XI for Aerith), which bestows 10 SP.

| **Remember to Upgrade Your Weapons!**<br>In a previous section, I advised you to switch your weapons to upgrade manually. At this point, you should have earned enough SP be at Weapon Level 6 and therefore should have access to all possible weapon upgrades. Since it's going to be a while before you earn that last 100 SP, this is a good time to look at all upgrades you don't yet have and choose the ones that will best serve you from here forward. Again, my advice is Materia slots first and then Reprieve, but if you've made it this far, you should have enough expertise to make your own informed decisions. |

| **Hardedge Materia Slots**<br>The next couple of strategies recommend equipping Cloud's Hardedge and filling it with 6 orbs of Materia. However, at this point, his Hardedge probably only has 4 Materia slots. You can either equip a different weapon that DOES have 6 Materia slots or pay Chadley 100 gil to remove all of the upgrades to the Hardedge so that you can reassign them. Either way, you definitely want to prioritize having 10 total Materia slots (6 on the weapon and 4 on the armor) for each combatant.<br>Ditto for Tifa's Metal Knuckles. It probably only has 5 Materia slots right now, but another one is available in Sub-Core V. |

Next up is Two-Person Team Vs. Dynamic Duos.

The Three-Person Team Vs. Monsters of Legend Challenge is next, and it can earn you two of your missing Trophies.

Learning the Bad Breath skill will earn you the Master of Mimicry Trophy since you have already acquired the other 3 Enemy Skills. You also get the "That's the Smell" Trophy for defeating the one and only Malboro in the game.

Completing all of these Challenges and all of the Colosseum Challenges unlocks the last Simulator Challenge. It's a doozy, and it is the subject of the next section of this guide.

## Three-Person Team Vs. Top Secrets Challenge

This final challenge in the Combat Simulator is arguably the most difficult part of the game. This sequence of 5 brutal battles will test your skills and tax your resources, as it must be tackled at Hard Difficulty. This is where you will find the Pride and Joy Prototype, and defeating that enemy will earn you the Ultimate Weapon Trophy.

As usual, I'm going to talk this Challenge to death before we even start equipping our team. If you're not interested in the rationale behind my battle decisions, feel free to skip down to the section [Equipping Your Team](#equipping-your-team).

| **Maybe I Should Wait and Do This Later . . .**<br>I'd like to convince you that you can and should undertake this Challenge now.<br>Assuming that you leveled up Materia as I described earlier, you are now in as good a position as you'll ever be to tackle this Challenge. The only thing you have to gain by waiting is that you will accrue a few more SP for your weapons by defeating certain bosses in Hard Mode. Those benefits will be barely noticeable at best so you have little to gain by waiting.<br>Moreover, completing this challenge and defeating the Pride and Joy Prototype will earn you the Gotterdammerung accessory. A character with this Accessory equipped gets a full Limit Break gauge at the beginning of battle AND that Limit Break gauge fills much faster than usual during battle. This is clearly a huge advantage and can definitely take some of the pain and stress out of earning the Hardened Veteran Trophy.<br>This Challenge can definitely be frustrating at first, but you should consider the time you spend on it to be a long-term investment. Once you are successful, your path to the finish line will be a lot easier. |

| **I Saw This Video on YouTube That Made This Challenge Look Easy . . .**<br>The key word in that statement is "look."<br>I, too, was seduced by the videos that show how to complete this Challenge in just a few minutes without ever needing to use any healing. I am convinced that this is possible. However, over a period of several months, I have tried to duplicate the methods depicted in these videos at least 100 times, and I haven't succeeded even once. Many of you are certainly way better at this game than I am, but even so I'm going to suggest that it will take most people many hours of practice to get this "Easy" method to work even once. Next time you watch one of these videos, check the timer in the lower left and see hour many hours of gameplay are behind mastering that "easy" method. I'm going to suggest that you probably want to earn your Trophies first and then work on perfecting your technique later.<br>The problem with most of these videos is that they require your timing and execution to be nearly perfect throughout 5 battles. particularly in the third and fourth Stages. If something goes wrong, this challenge will go downhill in a hurry, forcing you to start again from the beginning. Ultimately, I found that trying to mimic the success shown in the videos was an exercise in futility. When I eventually gave up and tried to just win the Challenge, I was able to do so on the first try. True story. |

| **Plan for the Worst**<br>Instead of trying to coach you through a strategy in which everything must go your way, I'm going to offer one in which you have a very good chance of success even when many things go wrong. I generally give up and restart the Challenge if I have any casualties in the first 2 Stages, and that happens fairly commonly. Big deal. So I have to repeat the first 3-4 minutes of the Challenge. However, once I make it to Stage 3, I complete the Challenge probably 90% of the time.<br>I claim that most people who follow the guidelines below will complete this Challenge much sooner than the would if they try to mimic the "Easy" strategy. As usual, though, I encourage you to make your own decisions and try your own strategies. |

In the box below, I'm going to discuss (mostly with myself) why I use Barret instead of Tifa for this Challenge. Feel free to skip it altogether.

#### Equipping Your Team

**Cloud**

**Equipment** \-\- Buster Sword, Supreme Bracer, Revival Earrings

**Weapon Materia** \-\- **Healing**, **Revival**, **MP Up** x 2, **HP Up**, **First Strike**, **Bahamut**

**Armor Materia** \-\- **Fire**, **Lightning**, **Magic Up**

**Notes** \-\- Cloud will be splitting his time between physical attacks and Magic, and the Buster Sword offers a good balance between the two. It would be nice to have that extra Materia slot of the Force Bracelet, but I feel better getting the +53 Defense boost of the Supreme Bracer. This justifies giving Cloud just one **HP Up** Materia (with better Defense he can survive with fewer HP). The Force Bracelet plus another **HP Up** give Cloud about 25% more HP, but from the quick tests that I ran, he took almost 30% more damage from physical attacks and maybe 10% more damage from Magic attacks. With limited MP, healing is at a premium, so overall I feel that the Supreme Bracer is the wise choice if we're equipping conservatively.

* * *

**Aerith**

**Equipment** \-\- Mythril Rod, Chain Bangle, Revival Earrings

**Weapon Materi** a -- **Healing** \- **Magnify**(Paired), **MP Up** x 2, **HP Up** x 2, **Leviathan**

**Armor Materia** \-\- **Fire**, **Lightning**, **Magic Up**, **Revival**

**Notes** \-\- With its best-in-game Magic stat, Mythril Rod is the obvious choice for Aerith. Aerith has inherently low Defense, so give her the protection of the Chain Bangle.

* * *

**Barret**

**Equipment** \-\- Big Bertha, Supreme Bracer, Revival Earrings

**Weapon Materia** \-\- **Lightning** \- **Elemental** (Paired), **Healing**, **MP Up** x 2, **HP Up,****Shiva**

**Armor Materia** \-\- **First Strike**, **Time**, **Revival**

**Notes** \-\- Because of his naturally high HP, Barret can get by with just one **HP Up**, Since he won't be using Magic much, one **MP Up** should suffice, and he can get by with only 3 Materia slots on his armor. The **Lightning** \- **Elemental** combo on his weapon is for the lightning-weak enemy in the last Stage.

* * *

#### Equipment Overview

- The equipped Summons are primarily for the stat boosts they provide, as detailed [back in Chapter 4](#chapter-4----mad-dash). Shiva is the only Summon we are going to call into duty, and I think it is wise to have Barret equip her Materia.
- This is an extremely conservative setup. Everyone can heal, and everyone can revive the fallen. For an Accessory, we are opting for the safety net of the Revival Earrings instead of the more aggressive Fury Rings. Your team won't be able to hit as hard, but your enemies' attacks will do less damage, and you are much better prepared to deal with it.
- I don't claim that this method is "Easy," nor is it especially elegant. We are aiming for completion, not perfection. Completing this Challenge will definitely require some practice and consequently some failures. Do not let those failures discourage you. Instead, learn from them and use them to adjust your actions. You should see steady progress towards your eventual and ultimate success.
- The entire Challenge should take somewhere around 15-20 minutes.

| **Not Enough Materia Slots? Not a Problem**<br>Some of the recommended weapons probably do not yet have 6 Materia slots. If this is the case, just give Chadley 100 gil for each weapon you need to change and have him Remove Upgrades. Then go to Menu > Upgrade Weapons and reallocate all of that weapon's SP. Be sure to get the 6 Materia slots first, then invest any remaining SP as you see fit. |

| **Managing Your MP**<br>One of the challenges of Hard Mode is conserving your MP. As mentioned earlier, at the beginning of each Stage, each character gets back 30% (NOT 50% as advertised) of his/her maximum MP. Let's see how this plays out.<br>If you use precisely 30% of your maximum MP in each Stage, then you will begin each Stage with full MP and will finish Stage 5 with 70% of your original MP intact. That may sound comforting, but there is no reason to have that many MP left over. Use 'em or lose 'em.<br>You can actually use a whopping _44%_ of your maximum MP in each Stage and never run out of MP until the very end of Stage 5.<br>In terms of numbers, Barret will start with roughly 100 MP, Cloud with about 175, and Aerith with about 200. This means that in each Stage, Barret can afford to use 44 MP, Cloud 77, and Aerith 88. That's over 200 MP per round.<br>My point is that although you need to watch your MP levels, you can probably afford to use far more MP than you might think.<br>Our attacks are going to rely primarily on Magic in Stages 1 and 2. Don't be reluctant to spend the MP necessary to complete those Stages as quickly as possible. We should recover some ground on the next 2 Stages and enter Stage 5 with more than enough MP to meet our needs. |

| **Crucially Important Information -- Please Read Carefully!**<br>We are equipping Revival Earrings on the entire team because we're assuming that Bahamut is going to execute his Megaflare attack and wipe out the entire party. The Revival Earrings will then revive anyone still wearing the Earrings, but doing so will "break" the Earrings, removing that protection. **It is therefore vitally important that at least 2 members of your team have the Revival Earrings still intact when Bahamut does Megaflare**. If only one character survives Megaflare, (s)he is unlikely to recover enough ATB to revive an ally before Bahamut finishes him/her off. With 2 members, you still have a fighting chance, but ideally, you want to keep all 3 sets of Revival Earrings unused when Megaflare hits. The success of this Challenge depends on this!<br>In case you're thinking that equipping a weapon with Reprieve will give you an extra measure of protection, I suggest that you should reconsider. Reprieve allows an otherwise doomed character to survive with 1 HP. Against vicious foes like you fight in the Top Secrets Challenge, it is unlikely that you will be able to recognize this problem and then heal that character before (s)he gets attacked again and dies.<br>If you break more than one pair of Revival Earrings before entering Stage 4, you probably won't survive Stage 4, but go ahead and try anyway. Even if your team gets wiped out, you'll gain valuable experience at fighting this battle and will be better prepared the next time you try. |

| **Summoning**<br>During this challenge, you will almost certainly get opportunities to Summon in at least Stages 1 and 2. You need to resist the temptation to Summon until we get to Stage 4. You can only Summon once during the entire Challenge, and you are going to need it most when facing both Bahamut and Ifrit simultaneously in Stage 4.<br>When we get to Stage 4, I'll show you exactly when to use your Summon as well as how to ensure that the Summon opportunity is available to you. Until then, just ignore any Summon command that shows up in your characters' menus. |

With all of those preliminaries out of the way, let's get started.

#### Stage 1 -- Shiva

| **Shiva -- Things to Keep in Mind**<br>- Predictably enough, most of Shiva's attacks are Ice-based Magic. Ice spells are generally slow to fully hit and can be dodged fairly easily.<br>- Her Whiteout attack can induce Sleep. However, follow-up attack will awaken the sleeping ally, so the effects are usually short-lived.<br>- When Shiva skates around the arena, that trail of ice she leaves behind can cause damage and inflict Slow.<br>- Her weakness if Fire, so Firaga spells (preferably cast from an Arcane Ward to conserve MP) will be our primary weapon against her.<br>- She is also susceptible to Stop.<br>- The Frost Familiars that she can summon late in the battle will shield her from Magic attacks. Fire-based spells will destroy them, but hopefully we'll finish off Shiva before she has a chance to bring them into the fray. |

| **Shiva -- Battle Guidelines**<br>- When the Stage begins, immediately switch to Barret, have him cast Haste on Aerith, and then switch to Aerith.<br>- Have Aerith lock onto Shiva and attack until her ATB gauge is full. Keep an eye on Shiva and do your best to dodge the attacks that she throws at Aerith.<br>- When Aerith's ATB gauge is full, have her set an Arcane Ward around Cloud. **The location of this Arcane Ward is important**. We want it to be as close as possible to the place where Shiva gets Pressured and Staggered so that Cloud can deliver both up-close melee attacks and double casting from the Arcane Ward without having to move very far.<br>- Make sure that Cloud is still in the Arcane Ward and have him double cast Firaga. This should Pressure Shiva.<br>- Have Barret cast Stop on Shiva. This will -- pardon me, but I have to say this -- _freeze_ Shiva in her Pressured state for 10 seconds.<br>- Have Barret follow up with a Focused Shot if he has enough ATB.<br>- Switch back to Cloud and have him hit Shiva with a Focused Thrust, then beat on Shiva in Punisher Mode until she Staggers.<br>- Move Aerith to the Arcane Ward and have her double cast Firaga.<br>- Move Cloud to the Arcane Ward and have him double cast Firaga.<br>- Have Barret unload his Overcharge, then try to get him to execute a Maximum Fury before Shiva's Stagger ends.<br>- You get to practice the Stage every time you attempt this Challenge, and after a while you should reliably be able to finish off Shiva at this point.<br>- If Shiva survives, she will skate away. Have Barret continue to attack with Overcharge and Maximum Fury, and have Aerith build up 2 ATB bars.<br>- When Shiva signals that she is going to perform Diamond Dust, have Aerith cast Magnified Curaga on the team so that they survive.<br>- Heal again after Diamond Dust, and send Cloud or Aerith back to the Arcane Ward for another barrage of Firaga to Pressure Shiva again.<br>- Shiva will probably resist Stop if you try it a second time, but you can still Stagger, and beat down Shiva like before or just whittle away her last few HP.<br>**Key to Survival** \-\- If you plant the Arcane Ward properly, Shiva should go down in one Stagger. If not, heal both before and after Diamond Dust, and you should not suffer any casualties. |

#### Stage 2 -- Fat Chocobo (FC)

| **Fat Chocobo -- Things to Keep in Mind**<br>- FC is immune to all adverse status effects.<br>- Although FC has no elemental weaknesses, Thundaga is a good choice of spells because its low charge time.<br>- His Defense and Magic Defense stats are identical, but he has a "lesser resistance" to physical attacks. Magic attacks also have a Stagger multiplier of x1.5, Therefore, Level 3 Magic (again, preferably from an Arcane Ward) is your best offensive weapon.<br>- The biggest danger in the battle comes from the random mascots that the Moogle throws into the arena ("Fingers Crossed."). These can be a Cactuar, a Plush Tonberry (indicated by ????????), or a Plush Bomb (indicated by ????). **DO NOT LET THESE ENEMIES LINGER!** These mascots have few HP, but you need to be especially wary of the Tonberrys since they can inflict instant death. Since we're trying to keep our Revival Earrings intact, instant death can be a major problem.<br>- There are at least 4 ways to deal with these mascots. I'll list all 4 but advise you to rely primarily on the first 2.<br>  - Whenever Cloud has some ATB to spend, have him choose Triple Slash and check the list of available targets. If there are any mascots among them, have him target them. This can eliminate as many as 3 mascots. If there are no mascots, back out and choose a more appropriate use for his ATB.<br>  - Whenever Barret has some ATB to spend, have him choose Maximum Fury and check the list of available targets. If there are any mascots among them, make that your targeted enemy. Once one mascot is finished off, Barret will automatically direct the rest of his attack at another target.<br>  - Many of Fat Chocobo's attacks (such as Roly Poly and Kweh Kweh) will damage the mascots as well as your allies.<br>  - If the mascots are close to FC, a level 3 spell such as Thundaga can damage them and, in many cases, will eliminate them.<br>- FC will announce his moves well in advance. Usually he will be moving quickly and therefore hard to hit, so it is usually best to dodge and then guard when you see an attack coming.<br>- Often at the end of his Roly Poly attack, FC will be Pressured. Try to move in and use Focused attacks to increase his Stagger. |

| **Fat Chocobo -- Battle Guidelines**<br>- Have Barret cast Haste on Aerith. Then switch to Aerith and have her build 2 ATB bars.<br>  <br>- Have Aerith throw an Arcane Ward under Cloud, then switch to Cloud.<br>  <br>- FC will probably now signal "Boom." Have Cloud back away from FC and guard until that attack has been executed. Then have him return to the Arcane Ward and double cast Thundaga.<br>  <br>- Move Aerith into the Arcane Ward and have her double cast Thundaga. This should Pressure FC.<br>  <br>- Use Focused attacks, Overcharge, and Punisher Mode attacks to Stagger FC.<br>- Go all out on FC and whittle away as many HP as possible. His Stagger duration is a full 20 seconds, so make use of this time:<br>  <br>  - Cloud should use either Infinity's End or a double Thundaga from the Arcane Ward.<br>  - Barret can use either Maximum Fury or Thundaga from the Arcane Ward for damage.<br>  - Aerith can either use Ray of Judgment or double cast Thundaga from the Arcane Ward.<br>- If we had Tifa on the team instead of Barret, the battle could be over now. As things are, FC probably has about 40% life left, so we'll want to Pressure and Stagger him again. This time, though, you have to be more aware of the mascots. Try to have Cloud maintain one unused ATB bar so that he can use Triple Slash quickly when Tonberrys appear.<br>- Otherwise, repeat the pattern from the beginning of the Stage. Fill Aerith and Cloud's ATB gauges, then have both unleash Thundaga from the Arcane Ward. This should Pressure FC. A Focused Thrust and a Focused Shot, and FC should Stagger. From there, just beat down like before except keep an eye out for Tonberrys.<br>- You have to be patient. Even if you are very close to Staggering FC, prioritize eliminating the mascots. They are the greatest danger in this Stage.<br>**Key to Survival** \-\- If you maintain awareness of Tonberrys and eliminate them ASAP, you shouldn't have any fatalities. |

#### Stage 3 -- Leviathan

| **Leviathan -- Things to Keep in Mind**<br>- This is where Barret and his ranged attacks really begin to shine. Leviathan spends a lot of time diving into the floor, flying around overhead, cruising around the arena, and generally refusing to stand still and let you damage him with melee attacks. Barret's shots, however, track that motion and will nearly always connect.<br>- Leviathan is immune to all status effects. He is weak against Thunder-based Magic.<br>- I don't think that his Spinning Dive move can be avoided, and whenever Leviathan does this move, he always does it twice in a row. When you see this coming, try to make Barret your active character. Have him protected with Steelskin and have him guard (R1 button) to reduce damage.<br>- When you see Leviathan performing "Aquatic Focus," he is preparing his strongest attack, which is Tidal Wave. This will hit your entire team for about 50% of their maximum HP, so make sure to top up their health when you see this coming. FWIW, dealing sufficient damage to Leviathan during Aquatic Focus can cancel (or actually just postpone) Tidal Wave.<br>- At 50% health, Leviathan will execute "Tidal Roar." Leviathan will create a bunch of moving water geysers that cause damage and disrupt the actions of anyone coming in contact with them and they fly around out of reach. The geysers don't do a lot of damage, but they do make it very difficult to stand still and execute attacks. Worse yet, they act as shields. Geysers that are between Barret and Leviathan will protect Leviathan from damage. When this happens, you will see the message "Immune" on the screen.<br>- Remember the importance of keeping those Revival Earrings intact for the next Stage. Make sure that Aerith always has one ATB so that she is always ready to heal when necessary. When in doubt, Heal. |

| **Leviathan -- Battle Guidelines**<br>It is difficult to provide a step-by-step plan for defeating Leviathan. The battle is long, and it doesn't always seem to follow the same sequence. However, if you read the general guidelines below, you should know how to proceed and how to react when special circumstances arise. As long as you are proactive about keeping your team healed, your team members are in little danger of expiring during this Stage. When in doubt, hit the Options button so that you can think things over before acting.<br>- In this fight, Barret is going to be your primary attacker.<br>  - **At the beginning of the Stage, have him cast Haste on himself to speed up the damage he does and then Steelskin to help keep himself healthy.** <br>    - The former lasts 100 seconds, and the latter lasts about 90 seconds, so try to remember to renew these spells as the Stage wears on. (When the ATB gauge stops pulsing, it is time to renew Haste, which means Steelskin has probably worn off also).<br>    - Timewise, these 2 spells definitely pay for themselves.<br>  - Barret's basic sequence will be to perform Overcharge, then fill his ATB gauge fully, and then perform Maximum Fury.<br>  - Remember that Maximum Fury always empties Barret's ATB gauge, but if he uses 2 ATB bars, he does twice as much damage plus 15% compared to using one ATB bar. That is, you get a 15% damage bonus if you use both ATB bars.<br>  - After Maximum Fury, Barret can refill his Charge meter by either using regular (Square button) attacks or by hitting Triangle repeatedly to reload. A third option is a hybrid -- do a full cycle of regular attacks, then hit Triangle once to reload and repeat as necessary.<br>  - Before using either Overcharge or Maximum Fury, make sure that Leviathan isn't about to disappear and isn't protected by geysers. You want to make your best attacks count.<br>- Aerith's main role is going to be support.<br>  - **Have her cast Regen early in the Stage**.<br>    - For the low, low price of 6 MP, Regen will allow your entire team to continuously recover HP, thus reducing the need for healing.<br>    - When cast on just one character, Regen will immediately replenish 10% of the character's maximum HP and then will recover an additional 1% every 2 seconds over a period of 180 seconds, thus recovering a total of 100% of the maximum HP over a 3 minute interval.<br>    - However, when cast with a maxed out **Magnify**, the HP recovered is reduced slightly. Specifically, each character will immediately recoup 7.5% of maximum HP immediately and then 1% every 2 seconds for 134 seconds, for a total of 74.5%.<br>    - In this particular battle, Barret is going to take the lion's share of the damage, so Regen isn't going to help him much. However, it will slowly but surely heal Aerith and Cloud back towards full health, allowing you to focus manual healing on Barret.<br>  - You probably don't use Aerith's Fleeting Familiar ability very much, but in a long battle like this, **I recommend that you use it right after Regen**.<br>    - If you just cast it and do nothing else, it deals passive damage, kind of like Poison, .<br>    - If Aerith uses a spell, the Familiars do additional damage.<br>    - At its very worse, Fleeting Familiar actually does more damage per ATB bar than any of Aerith's other abilities, including Ray of Judgment. It just does it over a period of time rather than immediately.<br>  - Aerith's MP are probably pretty low after the first 2 Stages, so you might want to use her attacking spells sparingly. Leviathan is very aggressive and can interrupt Aerith's actions easily, and you don't want to waste MP or ATB. It's also tough to use an Arcane Ward since there are few safe spots on the arena. Barret can take down Leviathan by himself if Aerith just does her job of keeping the team healthy.<br>- Cloud is going to be filling in where he is needed.<br>  - When Leviathan is easy to reach (like at the beginning of the Stage), Cloud can attack in Punisher Mode to do some damage and build ATB.<br>  - If someone needs healing and Aerith is low on ATB, Cloud can fill in as a healer.<br>  - Since Cloud's MP reserve is probably in good shape, he can throw an occasional Thundaga spell when he's certain that Leviathan isn't about to dive underground and dodge it.<br>  - Cloud is probably going to have some stretches where he isn't very active, and that's okay. He will be recharging ATB so that he's ready when we need him to do something.<br>- Eventually, Leviathan will become Pressured. When he does, follow the usual drill -- Focused Thrust from Cloud and Focused Shot from Barret. This would also be a good time for Aerith to quickly throw down an Arcane Ward and cast Thundaga.<br>- It is an annoying reality of this battle that Leviathan will very likely become Staggered while in the air and will fall down a long ways from where your team is standing, meaning that running over to attack him with Cloud's sword would waste a significant part of that precious 10-second Stagger period. If this happens, consider having Aerith put down an Arcane Ward (or use an existing one) and have anyone with an ATB bar throw Thundagas from there. This will probably all happen late in the battle, so those Thundagas should "spell" the end of Leviathan.<br>**Key to Survival** \-\- Have Aerith focus on healing. Keep everyone's health above 50%, and your team should survive intact. |

#### Stage 4 -- Bahamut

| **Bahamut -- Things to Keep in Mind**<br>- This can be the toughest battle in this Challenge. Bahamut is a relentless attacker with no weaknesses, a devastating ultimate attack, and about 90,000 HP.<br>- However, if you got this far with at least 2 sets of Revival Earrings intact, you should be able to win this battle.<br>- Bahamut can grab one of your team and take him out of the fray for 5 seconds ("Bind Impulse.") This is almost a good thing because during this time he will hold still, giving you a chance to attack and/or rebuild some ATB, while taking minimal damage.<br>- Bahamut will count down very slowly from Five. Every time he stops to count down, he will hold still long enough for you to attack unmolested. Take advantage of these few opportunities!<br>- When his countdown reaches zero, Bahamut will unleash Megaflare, which will kill off your entire team. Revival Earrings will be your only salvation.<br>- When Bahamut reaches 50% health, he will summon Ifrit to fight alongside him. This can be the toughest part of this Challenge. When this happens, we are going to shift our team's attention to Ifrit until he is vanquished. |

| **Bahamut -- Battle Guidelines**<br>- Your team members will have the same basic roles that they had against Leviathan:<br>  - Barret is still your primary attacker.<br>    - He should keep himself buffed with Haste and Steelskin and attack with Overcharge and a 2-ATB bar Maximum Fury.<br>    - If Bahamut is about to attack Barret and he has at least one ATB bar, go ahead and execute Maximum Fury. In most situations, this will prevent Bahamut's attack from knocking Barret off his feet and disrupting his attack.<br>  - Aerith's main role is support. Have her cast Regen first, then Fleeting Familiar. She should cast Magnified Curaga whenever anyone's HP go below about 50%, and she should try to keep an ATB bar at all times.<br>  - Cloud is going to attack Bahamut when he can, heal individuals when necessary, and try to keep an ATB bar in reserve whenever possible.<br>- Bahamut isn't going to give Cloud and Aerith many clear opportunities to attack. Watch for him to go into a countdown or execute a Bind Impulse and take advantage of those opportunities.<br>- When Bahamut's countdown reaches 3, start watching his health bar carefully.<br>  - As soon as possible after Bahamut's HP go below 65% (just less than 2/3), you need to let the HP of one of your team members to go critical (i.e., the screen goes **red**).<br>    - When this happens, the Summon timer should show up on the right side of the screen.<br>    - When the Summon timer appears, heal immediately to avoid untimely casualties.<br>    - If it doesn't show up, then either you already used your Summon in an earlier Stage or your team member went critical before Bahamut's HP went below 65%. In this case, you need for someone to go critical again.<br>  - For more information about what makes the Summon timer appear, please read the box below this one.<br>- From now until Bahamut reaches 50% health, try to keep one ATB bar in reserve on all characters.<br>  - This is tough to do with Barret since most of his attacks use his full store of ATB.<br>  - However, he is going to need an ATB bar to summon Shiva, and you want to do that as soon as Ifrit appears.<br>  - It's okay to use his ATB for Steelskin or Haste since those each use just one ATB bar. He can also use ATB to cast Haste on teammates.<br>  - For attacking, stick with Overcharge and regular Square attacks for now. After using Overcharge, you can just hit Triangle multiple times to refill Barret's Charge bar, allowing you to perform Overcharge again.<br>- Bahamut should reach 50% health and summon Ifrit when his countdown reaches 1 or 2. As soon as he does:<br>  <br>  <br>  - Have Barret summon Shiva. **LOCK ONTO IFRIT** or Shiva will attack Bahamut. Our immediate goal is to get rid of Ifrit.<br>  - Get Cloud involved in the action to speed the demise of Ifrit. His safest use of ATB is to have Shiva execute Icicle Impact or Heavenly Strike.<br>  - Aerith will need to do some basic attacks so that she has ATB for healing. If she fills her ATB gauge, Icicle Impact is a good choice. She will stay relatively safe and still have an ATB bar in reserve.<br>  - If either Barret or Cloud has a Limit Break charged, now is the time to use it. Make sure that it targets Ifrit.<br>  - Ifrit only has about 20,000 HP. You should be able to take him down in a minute or two. If nothing else, Shiva's Diamond Dust will finish him off more often than not.<br>- With Ifrit out of the way, you can concentrate on Bahamut. It is possible to eliminate him before he executes Megaflare, but if you get hit with Megaflare and your Revival Earrings are still intact, you should still be able to win this Stage.<br>- Be very careful about using a Limit Break at this point! If Bahamut goes to Megaflare before the Limit Break is executed (which has happened to me multiple times), the Limit Break will be canceled and lost. It is probably safer to hold that Limit Break in reserve until after Megaflare so that you don't waste it.<br>- After Megaflare, everyone with Revival Earrings will emerge with 50% health but no ATB. Mercifully, the Limit gauges do not empty. You will need to regain ATB to cast Arise on anyone is down. As usual, Barret can build ATB with relative speed and safety, plus he should have plenty of MP, so he should be your first choice for resurrecting, followed by Aerith.<br>- At this point, Bahamut will start another countdown, but you should be very close to finishing him off. Continue attacking as before, use any Limit Breaks that you gain, and maybe have Aerith cast a Thundaga from an Arcane Ward if necessary to end this nightmare of a battle.<br>**Keys to Success** \-\- This is a really tough Stage, but you can survive it if you 1) have at least 2 team members protected by Revival Earrings when and if Megaflare hits; 2) get Shiva to help you take down Ifrit quickly; and 3) have Aerith continuously stockpile enough ATB to keep your team healed and alive. |

| **Availability of the Summon Command**<br>The battle against Bahamut is considerably easier if you can summon Shiva as soon as Ifrit appears. When I first wrote this section, I was able to summon Shiva every time that I needed to. However, several times recently, the Summon timer never appeared during this battle. The battle was still winnable, but it was certainly more difficult. So, curious person that I am, I set out to determine the exact conditions that must be met in order for the Summon command to appear.<br>I was unable to find any source on the Internet that had a definitive answer on this matter. Even the ever-reliable Final Fantasy Wiki gave answers that were either guesses or just plain vague, neither of which will do us any good. So, I fought many different Summons battles many, many times, using the speculation I found on the Internet as a guide. I won't bore you with the specifics of my tests, but here are my conclusions, and I feel pretty good about them.<br>Assuming that you meet all of the other necessary conditions -- Summon Materia equipped, enough ATB to perform the summon, and an arena large enough to accommodate the given Summon -- the Summon timer will appear if either of the following occurs:<br>1. The enemy is Staggered, or<br>2. The enemy's HP decrease to approximately 65%, and after that the HP of one member of your team go critical (**RED**). **Order here is important**. The Summon timer will **not** appear if your team member's HP go critical first and then you reduce the enemy's HP below 65%.<br>As far as I can tell, these 2 criteria are the same for all battles against major enemies. In my tests, for battles in which a Summoning was possible, the Summon timer always appeared as soon as one of the 2 conditions above was met.<br>In the context of Stage 4, it is unlikely that you are going to meet condition #1 because you probably won't Stagger Bahamut. So, since Bahamut summons Ifrit at 50% health, you want one of your team members to go critical as soon as Bahamut's HP fall below 65%. This will give the Summon meter time to fill completely so that the Shiva Summon will be available as soon as Bahamut hits 50% health and summons Ifrit.<br>Unless you want to squander one of your Materia slots on the **Assess Materia**\-\- and I don't think that you do -- you are going to have to estimate Bahamut's HP in the battle. And obviously, you want to heal that critical teammate ASAP after the screen goes red lest you use up one of your precious sets of Revival Earrings.<br>A certain amount of luck is going to be involved here (and no, it won't help to equip your **Luck Up Materia**). Sometimes Ifrit and Bahamut gang up on a member of your team and just overpower him/her. However, if you are particularly vigilant as Bahamut's countdown gets to about 3 or 2, you should be able to time all this just about right. If you don't, all is not lost. The battle is still winnable. However, if you do, Ifrit will go down much faster, and the battle will be considerably less difficult. |

#### Stage 5 -- Pride and Joy Prototype

If you made it this far, there is a very high probability that you will complete this Challenge. I have never been defeated in this last Stage.

That is not to say that Pride and Joy is a pushover. He has plenty of weapons at his disposal. However, after the previous Stage, this one will seem like a welcome break.

| **Pride and Joy Prototype -- Things to Keep in Mind**<br>- Pride and Joy is immune to all status ailments. However, he is weak against Thunder (which is why we put that attribute on Barret's weapon) and mercifully he can't fly. He just kind of lumbers around the arena.<br>- Generally, the safest place is behind P&J. Most of his attacks are forward, but when he raises his arms, he is about to execute an attack both in front and behind, so move to the side when you see this coming.<br>- His Spark Whip attack shoots a lightning-based beam that sweeps from left to right and stuns anyone it hits for 8 seconds.<br>- His Jammer Laser attack shoots beams that inflict a variety of status ailments, including Stop, Silence, Sleep, Poison, and Slow.<br>- His Napalm Shot creates pools of Fire on the ground. Touching them causes about 800 HP damage. It is especially annoying when one of these winds up in your Arcane Ward. Grrrrrrrr!<br>- His 2 most devastating attacks are Brute Force and Catch<br>  - Brute Force is an AOE attack in which P&J slams himself to the ground. Allies in the vicinity can lose more than 50% of their maximum HP. Ouch! When the enemy telegraphs this attack, switch to the character furthest from P&J.<br>  - With Catch, P&J will grab an ally who is in front of him and bind him. Eventually, he will throw the ally to the ground, doing 9999 damage. This is another reason to try to stay behind P&J.<br>- Despite appearances, Pride and Joy can be surprisingly fast. When he runs towards a member of your team, a less agile ally like Barret will have difficulty evading.<br>- However -- and here's why you want to always have someone behind P&J -- if you switch to a character who is behind P&J, the character in front will generally not get grabbed or suffer a major attack. Part of the game's AI is to have enemies focus on your active character and do little harm to characters that you are not controlling. By strategically changing your active character, you can avoid a lot of damage by making P&J shift his attention from a character in front of him to one who is behind him. Once I got the hang of this relatively simple concept, I was able to avoid casualties almost completely.<br>- The key to victory, then, is to spread your team out and avoid remaining with any one character too long. Switching characters will minimize damage from Pride and Joy and ease your path to victory. |

| **Pride and Joy Prototype -- Battle Strategy**<br>- Since your team members will need to be more equally involved in this battle, their roles will change slightly.<br>  - Barret is still your go-to attacker.<br>    - With the **Lightning** \- **Elemental** Materia pair on his weapon, his attacks will do more damage than before.<br>    - Have him begin by giving himself Haste. Steelskin is less important because he shouldn't take much physical damage.<br>    - Often Barret will be able to blast away at P&J safely from a distance. If P&J doesn't come after him, stick with this as long as you can. When P&J charges at him, switch to another character.<br>    - Barret should also have full MP and very little use for them. When he gets an ATB bar, consider moving him to Aerith's Arcane Ward so he can double cast Thundaga.<br>  - Aerith is going to need to help with attacking, but healing is still her primary function. <br>    <br>    <br>    - You need to make sure that she always has sufficient MP for healing and resurrecting.<br>    - I still recommend having Aerith open with Fleeting Familiar.<br>    - If she has plenty of MP, an Arcane Ward and some Thundaga spells can do a lot of damage.<br>    - When her MP run low, consider using Ray of Judgment instead of casting spells.<br>  - Cloud is going to need to provide offense, both from melee attacks and from Magic.<br>    - Cloud has great agility, so he should be able to get behind P&J, attack in Punisher Mode, and then roll away when P&J is going to attack.<br>    - Remember that Disorder will do damage, change to Punisher Mode, and restore ATB all at once.<br>    - Cloud should have plenty of MP left, so consider using his ATB and Aerith's Arcane Ward to double cast Thundaga on P&J.<br>- Basically, you are going to attack, switch characters often, and try to avoid Pride and Joy's attacks as much as possible.<br>  - Try to avoid Catch by having the character furthest away from P&J do the attacking.<br>  - When you see Brute Force coming, get away ASAP and be prepared to heal.<br>  - P&J will signal most of his attacks well in advance, giving you time to react.<br>  - If you get hit, most of his attacks except Catch can be survived.<br>- If everyone's MP get low (i.e., below 15) stop using attack Magic. If someone has to be resurrected, you want to have the 14 MP necessary to bring them back with full HP.<br>**Key to Success** \-\- This battle is all about switching characters. Time those changes correctly, and you will own Pride and Joy. |

That's it! Congratulations -- the Ultimate Weapon Trophy and the Gotterdammerung accessory are yours!

Our next task will be completing all 18 Chapters at Hard Difficulty. Let's take a moment to see how that is going to work.

## Completing Chapters in Hard Mode

To earn the Hardened Veteran Trophy, you are going to have to complete all 18 Chapters in Hard Mode. In this section, I'm going to provide some general advice for doing this. Then in the next few sections I'll give you specific plans to follow for each Chapter.

In [the section of this guide on the Shinra Combat Simulator](#on-to-the-shinra-combat-simulator), I gave you some general guidance on how to survive in Hard Mode. Honestly, though, many of those tips were suggestions offered by others and do not reflect my own strategies. I have never, for example, found it beneficial or necessary to use Chakra or Prayer because they use large amounts of ATB relative to the healing you receive. Moreover, those suggestions were for fighting Combat Simulator Challenges in Hard Mode. Chapters are longer, so completing them in Hard Mode is going to require a different mindset than doing Challenges in the Simulator.

Surviving an entire Chapter in Hard Mode is primarily an exercise in managing your MP. Doing so largely comes down to 3 strategies:

1. Minimizing damage taken so that less healing is required. Cloud's Parry ability and use of Steadfast Block Materia are 2 good ways to do this. There is really no substitute for learning your enemy's attack patterns.
2. Learning strategies for defeating bosses that are not easily beaten by conventional battle techniques. I'll give you as much guidance on this as I can (and way more than you probably expect) as we go along.
3. Knowing the locations of healing benches so that you can replenish HP without using MP, and making optimal use of Mako Shards for replenishing MP.

You might still have some other Trophies that you have been postponing (I, for example, am still putting off Biker Boy because I so loathe that mini-game). A list of possibilities can be found [here](#look-at-where-we-are).

Let's take a closer look at how Mako Shards work. The information in the box below is extremely important! Be sure to read it all, paying particular attention to the passage in **bold** typeface.

| #### **Mako Shards**<br>Smashing a crate that contains a Mako Shard will restore 10% of the maximum MP of everyone on your team. In Hard Mode, this can be a Godsend. For characters with 2 - **MP Up Materia** equipped, this can be 10 - 20 MP. That may not sound like much, but Mako Shards alone will allow you to go into boss battles at the ends of many Chapters with full MP.<br>_Items_ found in Shinra Crates are random, e.g., a Crate may contain a Potion during one run and then a Phoenix Down during the next. However, _within a given Chapter_, Crates that contain Mako Shards are _almost_ _always_ be in the same places, and they _almost_ _always_ seem to contain only Mako Shards. I say "almost" because I have found one Mako Shard (in Chapter 3) that does not reappear consistently. Otherwise, as far as I know, this rule is solid.<br>Oddly, a Crate that contains a Mako Shard in one Chapter doesn't necessarily contain one in another Chapter. Go figure.<br>**Once you have found a Crate that contains a Mako Shard, you can use it repeatedly by simply doing the following:**<br>1. **Smash the Crate, thereby regaining 10% of maximum MP per Shard for all members of your active team.**<br>2. **Do a manual Save**<br>3. **Reload the game.**<br>4. **Repeat steps 1-3 until your MP are full.**<br>You would think that if you Save after smashing the Crate that when you reload your game, that Crate would be smashed, but it turns out that isn't the case. When you reload the game after smashing a Crate containing Mako, the Crate will be intact and will again contain a Mako Shard.<br>Yes, the long Save and load times of FFVIIR make this method extremely tedious. However, right before a major battle, this can make the difference between success and failure. Most Chapters contain enough Mako Shards that this harvesting will be minimally necessary. Still, this technique is good to know for when you need it. |

I would like to suggest that you will have ample MP to get through Chapters in Hard Mode if you only take advantage of all available Mako Shards. The "Save and Reload" method outlined above should only be necessary when you find your resources to be below your comfort level.

| **Getting Mako Shards to Regenerate**<br>For the record, saving and then reloading your game is not the only way to get Mako Shards to regenerate. Any action that causes the game to reload will have the same effect. For example, if you choose to start a battle over again, that reload will regenerate all Mako Shards. Likewise, changing location by riding in a chocobo coach in Chapter 14 will reload your game and will therefore regenerate shards.<br>Whenever you visit an area multiple times within the same Chapter, always check and see if crates that you have already smashed are again intact. If a crate that originally contained a Mako Shard regenerates, then so will the Shard. |

| **Heal First, Then Recover MP**<br>Part of the advantage of knowing the locations of Mako Shards is that you can plan ahead and make optimum use of your MP. Part of this planning is to heal your party's HP BEFORE smashing a crate containing a Mako Shard. That way, your team emerges from this sequence of events with maximum MP.<br>Likewise -- and again, this is probably really obvious -- you can often hold off on healing when you know that you are about to come across a Bench that will heal your team's HP for free.<br>I've invested a lot of time locating these healing opportunities and documenting them for you. Make good use of them, and your path through Hard Mode won't be nearly so ... well ... hard. |

| **Treasure Chests in Hard Mode**<br>As a rule, there is no reason to track down or open Chests in Hard Mode. Chests usually contain either Items, gil, or Accessories. You don't need Items because you can't use them in Hard Mode; you don't need gil because you should already have more than you will need; and most Accessories in Chests can be purchased elsewhere. Therefore, don't waste time on Chests. You will have more important considerations to occupy your mind.<br>I will notify you when you encounter a Chest that contains something worth grabbing. |

Over the next 6 sections, I will walk you through every Chapter of the game in Hard Mode, giving you locations where you can refill HP and MP as well as strategies for beating the toughest enemies. With these resources (and a LOT of perseverance!) you should earn your Hardened Veteran Trophy.

Having established all that, let's try a couple of Chapters in Hard Mode and chalk up three more dresses and a couple of Trophies while we're at it.

## Hard Mode--Chapters 3, 8, and 9

We now have at least 3 more Trophies to earn before attaining Platinum status and getting the Master of Fate Trophy:

1. To earn the Hardened Veteran Trophy, you have to complete all 18 Chapters in Hard Mode.
2. To earn the Dressed to the Nines Trophy, you still have to make 3 more dresses appear in Chapter 9. Since you can do the Chapters in any order, I'm going to suggest doing Chapters 3, 8, and 9 now to complete the requirements for that Ultra Rare Trophy.
3. To earn the Best in the Business Trophy, you have to complete all side quests. This will occur simultaneous with our quest for the last 3 dresses.

In case it is not clear, let me emphasize this point: **For everything you undertake from this point on, you need to designate HARD MODE**. Otherwise, you're just repeating stuff you've already done, which is precisely what we set out to avoid whenever possible.

| **General Tip from Now On**<br>When navigating through the Chapters in Hard Mode, you can really simplify things by keeping your main character (usually Cloud) equipped with a maxed out **First Strike** Materia at all times. This way, he begins each battle with some ATB and can often end an encounter by executing a Triple Slash right away. |

#### Chapter 3 -- Take 3 (Hard Mode)

| **Important Points to Keep in Mind**<br>- We are here primarily to unlock Tifa's blue dress. We're going to do this by avoiding the Alone at Last Discovery Event.<br>- Places to restore HP and MP will be few and far between.<br>- If you equip both Cloud and Tifa with **MP Up** x 2 and **HP Up** x 2 Materia, you should be able to make it through this Chapter with minimal need to refill MP or HP. |

First, let's examine our resources for replenishing MP and HP.

We're only doing a couple of Side Quests, and except for the Wrath Hound, none of the enemies are very tough. HP can always be refilled at the following places:

- Cloud's apartment at Stargazer Heights.
- Bench in the Municipal Storage Area where you rescue Johnny.

In case you run low on MP, here are the places I've identified that you can find Mako Shards:

- Scrap Boulevard -- at the NW end of the tunnel that contains the Ice Materia, but see the note [below](#hard-mode--chapters-3-8-and-9).
- Municipal Storage Area -- where you rescue Johnny. I actually found TWO crates with Mako Shards in there! However, after you rescue Johnny, you can't return to this area. Tifa tries to rush you out of the area, but be sure to bust open all of the Crates before you leave.

| #### **Chapter 3 Mako Shards**<br>The Mako Shard in the Scrap Boulevard area is the only one in the game that has been unreliable for me. Sometimes it shows up in a different set of Crates, and sometimes it doesn't appear at all.<br>Refilling MP shouldn't be necessary in this Chapter, as explained above. However, if you find yourself in a desperate situation, I would suggest initiating the Nuisance at the Factory Side Quest and going through the Abandoned Factory. There are LOTS of Mako Shards (and another Bench) in there, and it isn't necessary to complete the Quest. |

| **Warning! Talking to Chadley in Chapter 3 Has Consequences**<br>If you are interested in unlocking that last Chapter 14 Resolution, you need to be very careful to NOT interact with Chadley. Chadley is right next to Wymer -- with whom you must speak -- and you could easily talk to Chadley by accident.<br>If you talk to Chadley, the Side Quest "Chadley's Report" will automatically be marked as completed. By itself, this isn't a bad thing, but if you then complete the 2 Quests recommended below, you will not get Barret's Resolution when you return to Chapter 14 in Hard Mode.<br>As you may or may not recall, you only get Barret's Resolution if Tifa and Aerith both have _less than_ 6 points. Completing 3 Side Quests in Chapter 3 will give Tifa 3 x 2 = 6 points, which is too many. You definitely want to complete the 2 Quests below because each of them earns you a Manuscript, but fulfilling the conditions for Barret's Resolution could cost you considerable time through repetition. Your best play here is to simply avoid Chadley on this pass through Chapter 3.<br>If you mess up and talk to him, try to load your last manual save or Autosave and see if that fixes things. |

As mentioned above, Tifa's scandalous blue dress is unlocked by avoiding the Alone at Last Discovery Event. Here's the plan.

- You have to do at least one Quest to advance the story line. So, after clearing out the rats and talking to Wymer, I would advise taking on the two side quests that lead to Manuscripts when tackled in Hard Mode. They are as follows:
  - The [Rat Problem Quest](#chapter-3----home-sweet-slum), which leads to Cloud's Art of Swordplay, Vol. V. This time, the Doomrats should be there on your first visit.
  - The [On the Prowl Quest](#chapter-3----home-sweet-slum), which leads to Tifa's Way of the Fist, Vol. IV. Wrath Hound hits like a tank in Hard Mode. Equip Binding Materia and put him to Sleep. In the first phase, physical attacks will do fine, in the second phase a Blizzaga will Pressure him and you can finish him off.
- Head back to Seventh Heaven. When you get close, you'll get pulled into saving Johnny. Do that, then return to the bar and notify the game that you are giving up on the rest of the Quests.

The "Alone At Last" Discovery Event will not trigger, so you don't have to answer Tifa's question about what type of clothes would suit her. **BE SURE TO FINISH THE CHAPTER!** This locks in Tifa's dress and gives you credit for completing the Chapter in Hard Mode so that you don't have to repeat it again.

When Chapter 4 begins, skip the opening scene. When the bike mini-game begins, press the Options button and choose to skip the game. Skip through the ending scenes of the mini-bike game until you get to the point where the team climbs some stairs and you have control of Cloud. You can now safely use Chapter Selection to access Chapter 8 in Hard Mode.

* * *

#### Chapter 8--Take 3 (Hard Mode)

| **Important Points to Keep in Mind**<br>- We're here primarily to unlock Aerith's light pink dress. **We are going to accomplish this by doing 0 Side Quests**.<br>- As we go, I'll list places in the Chapter where HP and MP can be restored. |

- Your first job is to defeat Reno. A description of how to do this appears in [the previous section on Chapter 8](#chapter-8----budding-bodyguard). Doing so in Hard Mode will earn you Cloud's Art of Swordplay, Vol. VII.
- After that, just proceed across the rooftops and into the Slums as before.

Here are the places that you can restore HP and MP.

**Benches**

- The Station -- N part. It's marked on your Map and is difficult to miss.
- Sector 5 Slums -- Central District (where Chadley is standing).

#### Mako Shards -- From the Rooftops to the Slums

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Objective | Map Area | Notes |
| Head for the Station | Sanctuary Way | (1) North central part of the Map, about 1/4 of the way from the rooftops to the Station. |
| Head for the Station | Sanctuary Way | (2) East central part of the Map, about 2/3 of the way from the rooftops to the Station. |
| Through the Backstreets | Scrapyard Back Alley | (3) Dead end in the N central part of the Map. Aerith tells you it's a dead end, and at this point in time it is an unnecessary detour, but it's there if you think you need it. |

Here is a map of those 3 locations:

- After Aerith turns Cloud loose on the town, find Oates and talk to him like you did before.
- Return to Aerith and do the Danger in the Hideout Objective.
- Rescue the kids and escort them back to the Hideout. I didn't find any Mako Shards along the way.
- When you leave the Hideout, heal at the Bench that is right in front of you.
- You really should have plenty of MP to deal with Rude before ending the Chapter. In case you want to top up your MP, you can still find Mako Shards in the 3 places indicated above.

- Head back to Aerith's house to fight Rude.
  - The strategy presented earlier can be found [here](#chapter-8----budding-bodyguard).
  - Remember to equip Aerith with a Headband to protect her against Sleep.
  - Beating Rude will earn Aerith's Telluric Scriptures, Vol. IV.
- Tiptoe past all of Aerith's crap to escape. Let Chapter 8 segue into Chapter 9, and the Dressed to the Nines Trophy is almost yours.

For the record, I am aware that we could have collected 2 more Manuscripts for Aerith while we were here. My opinion is that doing so is not worth the risk. Messing up and completing too many Side Quests will necessitate repeating both Chapters 8 and 9 again, and that is precisely what you are trying to avoid by using this guide. As I described [earlier,](#skill-points-and-weapon-upgrades) if you want all of the Manuscripts, you're going to have to repeat Chapter 8 in Hard Mode anyway. I believe it is better to be absolutely certain that you nail down that elusive Dressed to the Nines Trophy now and then go chasing after missing Manuscripts later. This is, after all, a Trophy-based guide.

| **VERY IMPORTANT ALERT! PLEASE READ!**<br>If you began your current run through FFVIIR prior to January 20, 2021, you really need to follow [this link](#important-revision-alert) to another section of this guide before going any further. I have made some revisions to this guide that may affect your ability to earn the Dressed to the Nines Trophy in an efficient manner, and I want to make sure that you know the best way to proceed.<br>If you already responded to this notice in the [Three More Dresses](#three-more-dresses) section of this guide, you need not do so a second time. It was just necessary for me to post warnings in both places. |

* * *

#### Chapter 9--Take 3 (Hard Mode)

| **Important Points to Keep in Mind**<br>- This is a fairly long Chapter with lots of fighting and only one opportunity to replenish MP. Conserve your MP on the routine battles at the beginning of the Chapter so that you will have a good reserve for the Colosseum battles and the Quest battles.<br>- As before, the decisions that you make in this Chapter will determine the dress that you are trying to unlock. Be especially careful to follow instructions, and the Dressed to the Nines Trophy will at last be yours.<br>- The toughest part of this Chapter is beating Hell House in Hard Mode. Be sure to go in with full MP and proper Materia. |

- Head into the Collapsed Expressway. I only found 1 Mako Shard on the way to Evergreen Park.

|     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Objective | Map Area | Notes |
| Take the Shortcut | Collapsed Expressway, Machinery Graveyard | NW part of the area. It is just to Cloud's left after he climbs the ladder after completing the first Mechanical Arm puzzle and Aerith suggests that they "Keep on Truckin'." |

- There is a Bench at the S end of the Collapsed Expressway - Large Cave-In area.
  - You can use it before and after fighting the 2 Lesser Drakes, OR
  - If your HP are in good shape, you can ignore Aerith's request that you follow her and go directly to the ladder that you're going to take down to the next level. By doing this, you will miss the Bench, but you can skip the battle with the Drakes altogether. If you don't remember where that ladder is, its location is circled in the second screenshot under [this heading](#chapter-9----the-town-that-never-sleeps).
- When you get to Evergreen Park and Tifa goes by in the Chocobo coach, she should be wearing a very short, low-cut royal blue dress.

- Head on in to Wall Market, but please read the box below before going much further.

| **Refilling MP in Wall Market**<br>I may have missed something, but I was unable to find ANY place near Wall Market to replenish MP. If you are REALLY desperate, you do have the following 2 options:<br>- Head N of town past Madame M's and take a right towards the Collapsed Expressway area. The Mako Shards (there are actually 2 of them, for a 20% MP recovery) in the Machinery Graveyard area is still there.
